| the per-tenant rewrite | `rewriteImage` walks the block's tenant windows (`Windows.of(task)` from the refs; two refs disagreeing on a tenant's length trip `rekey_tenant_len_conflict`), decodes each frame and re-encodes it in place; a re-encode whose stored length differs trips `rekey_image_len_drift` and defers the block | a packed block's tenants are frames at fixed `rel_off`s the mappings carry; the same plaintext under the same compression yields the same stored length (the bounded-output law `transform.writeScratchLen` states: a frame body never exceeds its plaintext), so a drift is corruption, never a layout change |
| the confirming census | a pass with nothing rewritten quiesces the write side (`rekeyQuiesce`: the packer's open packs sealed as `.rekey`, in-flight tenants 0), lists every allocated block (`allocatedBlocks`) minus the census's referenced ones, classifies each header (`current / previous / foreign / none`) and defers the close (`rekey_confirm_deferrals`) while any reads `previous` | the census walks the map; a block allocated but not yet mapped (a promotion between the walk and the seal) could still be under the old key — the allocator is the superset the map is not |
| the close | decode the control record → `closeRekey` → encode → `stagePut` + commit + fsync → `plane.closeRekey` (publish the one-key wrap, codec barrier, `retireWrap`) → `rekey_windows_closed` | "the old key is forgotten only after the LAST block's publish is durable": the last publish is a mover tx already durable at the pass's end; the config flip is its own tx; the wrap retires behind both |
| what crosses the admin lane | `admin_rekey` (`0x0705`, 48 B request: `{kdf_salt, key_id, throttle_pct}`; 24 B reply: `{verdict, reason, job_id, config_generation}`) carries NO key material: the CLI derives the new key in the invoking process, installs `/etc/squeezefs/keys/<id>.key` first (an identical file is fine, a different one under the same id refuses), then asks the daemon to open the window | the lane is a local socket with a verdict codec; a secret on it would need a second zeroize discipline and a frame that must never be logged; the key dir is the resolver every mount already trusts |
| the refusals | plaintext set → `plaintext`; a window already open → `window_open` (the sweep resumes on adoption, `job status` names it); the next key unresolvable from the key dir → `key_unresolved`; `key_id == the primary` → `same_key`; not the coordinator → `NotCoordinator` | each a typed arm of `RekeyRefusal`, text at the CLI |
| the mount in a window | resolves the next key from the key dir (`resolveNextKey`: by id, the salt from the config) and refuses by name without it; the window is logged OPEN; the adopted job resumes under the fabric's throttle | a mount that could serve under one key alone would write new blocks under a key half the set cannot read |
| the packed-tenant read on a transformed volume | `read_path.readMappingWindow` ran the window law under `block_size` (a transformed tenant's block offset is a multiple of `chunk = block + 4 KiB`: every tenant past index 0 refused `MappingRefused`, EIO, and tripped `packed_mapping_refusals`) and never decoded the frame; now the stride is the volume's and a transformed tenant is one frame decoded whole on the lane's codec | found by the rekey row's byte-exact check of 40 packed files under `lz4 + aes-256-gcm` (`packed-bad=40` before the fix; the earlier rows compared shas of the SAME read path on both sides and passed with every tenant EIO'd — `sha256sum` skipped them identically). A pre-existing defect of the transformed packed arm, never of the rotation; the unit test plants a framed tenant at block 3 (3 × chunk is not a `block_size` multiple) |

### O.3 A joiner learns an online `volume add-data` / `remove-data`

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| the push | the renewal grant's head carries the owner's `set_config` generation (`GrantHead.config_generation`; the owner reads `lifecycle_stats.volume_config_generation`, set at the mount's read and at every lifecycle commit — `PlaneWorld.commit`, the rekey close); a member stores the word at `learn` | the grant is the standing call every member makes at its cadence (265 ms under the smoke's `T_owner` 3 s, 10 s shipped) — the carriage already pushes slot offers through it; 0002 row 488 |
| the pull | `ResolveSetConfig` (manager block 0x05B5; request `{volume}`, reply `{generation, len, bytes}` ≤ `SET_CONFIG_MAX` = `geometry.recordValueCap(1 MiB node)`; `volume ≠ 0` or a non-manager answers `not_manager`; the bytes travel in the reply scratch); the joiner decodes with `set_config.decode` (total, fuzzed) and the reply codec has its own round-trip fuzz | a frame's `len` is a claim checked before any allocation (§1.8); the record's codec is the ONE decoder of its bytes |
| the apply | per record: a volume this plane lacks and the record names Active/Draining with a uri → `attachVolume` (router row disabled) → `WriterPlane.armVolume` (the next row in place; the first top-up pulls a grant) → `setVolumeState`; a volume it has takes the record's state through the router's lattice; `draining` / `retired` ALSO return the joiner's unconsumed window to the holder (`returnWindowOf`); the word moves to the record's generation only when every volume applied | a run left SET at the holder holds the drain's "used 0" forever; a partial apply retries at the next renewal (counted `volume_config_pull_failures`), never half-moves the word |
| the fabric-endpoint shape | a volume added with a `fabric_endpoint` record is NOT connected by the joiner online (logged loud, the word stays behind): it joins at the joiner's next mount | the daemon-owned connect (§O.1) is the mount's act under the mount's identity (`mount_fabric.Plan`, released with the mount); an online connect would need the plan's lifetime to outlive the mount's `defer` chain — owed with the next operations pass |
| the writer plane's shape | pre-sized at `router.MAX_VOLUMES` with `n_vols` (the holder's law): every `Vol` is pointed to by the allocator's seams, so a row never moves | the online arm is the next row in place; `create` is `armVolume` per volume |
| the mover's census under the symmetric plane (owed) | the manager's drain plans from its OWN slot trees (`forEachLayout` ranges `TREE_XATTRS` of this mount's view): blocks a JOINER's files reference on a draining volume are never planned, and the drain re-plans forever ("census empty but the victim still holds N") | the row removes B's files on d2 before `remove-data` (the frees ship to A) and states the limit; the cross-owner census (the holders' maps through tokens, or a joiner-side mover under the manager's plan) is the next lifecycle item |
| the row's placement | `SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_PROMOTE_STAGED=1`: each 4 MiB file promotes at its fsync through the router's round-robin band, so the first batch of 8 lands on d2; a streaming write chooses a volume per STRIPE UNIT by the ino's hash (`router.stripeUnitChoice`), which a 16-block file sits inside | the row reads placement per file instead of at B's dismount |
## P. Rung-10 design calls — the `MutualTls` channel class (2026-10-12; `docs/evidence/rung-10-mtls.md`)

The spec's second channel class (`sym_wire_membership_recovery.md` CW:987–1008; charter §8 "remote workers over
TCP/TLS … verify-read sampling below 100 % is admissible only on authenticated ∧ confidential"). The engine
decision first, then the PKI's shape, then the wire's.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **The TLS engine: handshake in-tree, record layer = kTLS** (the owner's direction, 2026-10-12) | **The HANDSHAKE written in-tree over `std.crypto`'s primitives** (`src/wire/tls.zig`): ONE TLS 1.3 profile — `TLS_AES_128_GCM_SHA256`, X25519, Ed25519 — server AND client, mutual authentication under a pinned CA, the RFC 8446 §7.5 exporter. **The RECORD LAYER on Linux is the kernel's**: after the handshake the traffic keys go to the `tls` ULP (`TCP_ULP` → `TLS_TX`/`TLS_RX`, `tls12_crypto_info_aes_gcm_128` at `TLS_1_3_VERSION`), the socket is plain TCP to the daemon, control records arrive as `TLS_GET_RECORD_TYPE` cmsgs on `recvmsg`, the rekey is a second `TLS_TX`/`TLS_RX` after a KeyUpdate (`rung-10-mtls.md` §9.2). `Framer.confidential()` is admitted only with BOTH kernel halves installed. The in-tree record layer (`Conn.linkRead/linkWrite`, KeyUpdate, close_notify) is the handshake's transport everywhere and the PORTABLE application path (macOS tests, the OpenSSL oracle rows) — on Linux the product requests `RecordLayer.ktls` and REFUSES loud without it, never a silent fall-back. The first 7.2.6-sqz had `# CONFIG_TLS is not set`; the orchestrator rebuilt it with `CONFIG_TLS=y CONFIG_TLS_DEVICE=y` (the reference's `feat/kernel-sqz-container-arm64`, `44752ce5`) and the kTLS path ran for real (`tls.ktls`: install, the whole-record `recvmsg` with the cmsg dispatch, the rekey by a second `TLS_RX`/`TLS_TX`, close_notify): the pair rows, OpenSSL in both roles, the wire's 1,000 verbs, the two-daemon matrix — all green; every kTLS row is gated on the runtime probe (a ledgered `other_step` skip on a kernel without the ULP), and the fail-loud path stands (`KernelTlsMissing` / `KtlsUnavailable` by name, `KtlsInstallFailed` when the ULP refuses the keys). **Measured** (ReleaseSafe, loopback, both ends' CPU; `rung-10-mtls.md` §9.6): plaintext 19.2–19.7 GB/s at 0.10 CPU-s/GB; userspace TLS 1.95–2.04 GB/s at 0.94–1.00; kernel TLS on the first kTLS kernel **0.41 GB/s at 4.3 CPU-s/GB** — its `gcm(aes)` was `gcm_base(ctr(aes-lib),ghash-lib)` (no `CONFIG_CRYPTO_AES_ARM64_CE_BLK` / `GHASH_ARM64_CE`) while `std.crypto` uses the CPU's `aes pmull`; after the second rebuild (`gcm-aes-ce`, Image `36a9604b…`) kernel TLS reads **3.51 GB/s at 0.50 CPU-s/GB (64 KiB) / 2.12 GB/s at 0.54 (1 MiB)**: 2.0× less CPU per GB than the userspace layer and 1.1–1.8× its throughput; 5× the CPU of plaintext — the AEAD itself, which only NIC offload removes. NOT vendored | The three candidates judged against the requirements (TLS 1.3, mutual authn under our own PKI, a channel exporter, no per-frame allocation, a CA-less config refused): **`std.crypto.tls`** (0.17) ships a CLIENT only — no server, no client-certificate arm (a `CertificateRequest` is `TlsUnexpectedMessage`, `Client.zig:604/707/826`), no exporter — so "use std" meant writing the server, the client-auth arm and the exporter anyway; **BearSSL** has no TLS 1.3; **mbedTLS 3.6.4+** has everything (its exporter landed 2025-06, `MBEDTLS_SSL_KEYING_MATERIAL_EXPORT`) at the price of a ~100-file Apache-2.0 C tree, a `config.h` whose whole job is to switch OFF what this wire must never negotiate (TLS 1.2, RSA, resumption, tickets, 0-RTT, renegotiation, ALPN), a translate-c binding and a second allocator discipline; the rule "measure both before choosing if both are viable" did not bind — the std arm is not viable without the write, and a vendored mbedTLS could only be measured after the same integration work. One profile is the design — there is nothing to downgrade to; a peer offering no common suite/group/scheme is refused by name — and every primitive is the one `std.crypto.tls.Client` itself uses (AES-GCM, X25519, HKDF-SHA256, the `Certificate` parser with Ed25519 verification). Correctness is judged by an EXTERNAL oracle on the venue: OpenSSL 3.0.13 `s_server -Verify` accepts our client and our certificate; `s_client` under our server exports the SAME keying material byte for byte under the binding label; a foreign-CA `s_client` gets `unknown_ca` (`tls_e2e_linux.zig`). Measured (ReleaseSafe, loopback, the 9-vCPU aarch64 venue): connect + handshake 509 µs vs 10 µs plaintext; 64 KiB writes 2.0 GB/s vs 21.5; 1 MiB writes 2.0 GB/s vs 10.0 — the wire moves verbs and token pages, never blocks (the 200 MiB `dd` across the TLS wire costs 9 KB of frames) |
| `std.crypto.Certificate.parse` is not total | Every certificate off the wire or a file runs `pki.Screen` FIRST: a bounds-checked walk of exactly the schema std's parser will index (`Certificate.zig:480–612` indexes `bytes[i]` unchecked; a 0-byte or lying-length certificate is a panic, i.e. a daemon abort under a hostile peer), then std's parser and verifier | charter §11 "every untrusted decoder total and fuzzed"; `fuzz: the PKI decoders` |
| **The PKI's shape** | **Ed25519 everywhere, issued by this binary** (`src/wire/pki.zig`; `squeezefs key tls-init [--name] <dir>` mints `ca.pem` + `ca.key`, `key tls-issue --node NAME [--out DIR] <ca-dir>` a node's `node.pem` + `node.key`): minimal X.509 v3 DER (version, serial, `CN=` names, UTCTime/GeneralizedTime by RFC 5280's 2049 rule, the Ed25519 SPKI, `basicConstraints` CA:TRUE + `keyUsage` on the CA, `keyUsage` digitalSignature on a node), PEM files, the private key the 48 B PKCS#8 v1 form `openssl genpkey -algorithm ed25519` emits; the CA ten years, a node one year, `notBefore` backdated five minutes (an unsynchronised peer's clock); the chain is exactly ONE leaf under the pinned CA (no intermediates, no revocation — rotation is re-issuing under the same CA, or a new CA and every node re-issued) | 32 B keys, 64 B signatures, deterministic signing, the fastest verify in `std.crypto`, and `Certificate.zig` already verifies Ed25519 — one signature primitive for the certificate AND the CertificateVerify. OpenSSL verifies the issued files (`openssl verify -CAfile ca.pem node.pem: OK`). The `CN` is the node's name for the logs; identity on the wire stays the ladder's `peer_id` — the certificate proves membership in the PKI, the HMAC proof proves the set's secret, and the exporter binds the two to one channel |
| **The posture is the process's, from knobs, not the set's record** | `SQUEEZEFS_WIRE_CHANNEL = plaintext \| mutual_tls` (switch) + `SQUEEZEFS_WIRE_TLS_CA / _CERT / _KEY` (config; defaults under `/etc/squeezefs/tls/`). `mount`, the admin lane and `job worker` install it ONCE before any fork or dial (`tls.installFromEnv`; the identity crosses the daemon fork in memory like the encrypt-key stash); the listener and the dialer read `tls.posture()`; tests pass an explicit `Posture`. A CA-less or malformed `mutual_tls` configuration REFUSES on the console naming the file and the law — never a downgrade | One daemon = one listener = one identity (0002 row 227). Every mismatch fails CLOSED and LOUD on both ends: a TLS dialer at a plaintext listener sees the Challenge's frame magic where a record header should be (`PeerPlaintext`, counted `cluster_wire_tls_dial_refused`) and the listener logs the bad magic; a plaintext dialer at a TLS listener is SILENT (the ladder's coordinator speaks first, TLS's client does) — the listener answers at half the dial bound with a plaintext `Refused` naming the class (`PeerSilent`, counted `cluster_wire_plaintext_refused`) so the dialer's log names it too. A durable class byte in `set_config` was weighed: it would let a joiner learn the class off the volume, but it is a format act (a reserved byte of `HeadWire`, a `format`/`config` flag, the mount reading it before the knob gate) in the format's and the ops agent's territory, and it buys nothing the fail-closed knob does not — the set's class is a deployment fact the operator sets on every node together with that node's certificate. Owed if the owner wants the class stamped |
| The session key stays, bound to the channel | The storage-trust ladder runs INSIDE the TLS channel unchanged, with `binding` = `TLS-Exporter("squeezefs-cluster-wire/v1/binding", "", 32)` (`auth.sessionKey`'s slot, reserved since rung 6); every frame still carries the HMAC trailer | Defence in depth per the spec: TLS makes the channel confidential, the per-frame MAC under a channel-bound key keeps a frame from being replayed across channels (two sessions from one secret and one nonce pair under two bindings hold different keys — `session.zig`'s test); `Framer.confidential()` = `authenticated() ∧ channel == mutual_tls` is what the job fabric's verify-read law reads (`jobs/wire.Channel` IS `tls.Channel`; the coordinator takes the process posture) |
| KeyUpdate, not a record ceiling | Our writer rotates at 2^23 records (half RFC 8446 §5.5's AES-GCM bound) with `update_not_requested`; a peer's `update_requested` is honoured by the NEXT write under the writer's own serialization (a reader thread never writes: the mux's halves belong to different threads). Under kTLS the same law: our KeyUpdate goes out as a `TLS_SET_RECORD_TYPE = handshake` `sendmsg` then a `TLS_TX` re-setsockopt; the peer's arrives as a `TLS_GET_RECORD_TYPE = handshake` cmsg then a `TLS_RX` re-setsockopt (Linux ≥ 6.14) | A standing session (the member's six control sessions) must never exhaust a key, and tearing it down mid-call would turn the no-retry law's sent-then-lost ambiguity into a cadence |
| zcrx and kTLS | `RECV_ZC` delivers raw TCP payload pages; software kTLS decrypts in the receive path zcrx bypasses — only NIC TLS RX offload (`CONFIG_TLS_DEVICE`, `ethtool -K tls-hw-rx-offload`) combines. The wire's sockets are never zcrx sockets today; the rule binds (refuse, counted, without device offload) the day a `RECV_ZC` path touches a `MutualTls` socket — a hardware-owed row beside the zcrx lane's | charter §6.3's lane is the NVMe/TCP read lane; the cluster wire is TCP/TLS by §1.2 |
| The wire's frame path and io_uring | The kTLS socket rides the Plaintext class's frame path — the blocking-syscall path on the wire's OS threads (spec §1.1 "deliberately not io_uring", charter §9 thread-per-connection), with `recvmsg(2)` where Plaintext uses `recvfrom(2)` (a non-data record without a control buffer is `-EIO`). Moving the wire's frames onto io_uring (`IORING_OP_RECVMSG`, `SEND_ZC`) is a decision about BOTH classes — named for the owner, not taken here | the direction read the Plaintext class as io_uring-driven; it is not, by the spec's own measurement |
| `dead_on_arrival` under TLS | The pre-send peek keeps its law (EOF / unsolicited bytes / EAGAIN) on the RAW socket; a reaper's close_notify is an encrypted record and reads as "bytes queued" — in practice the mux reader, parked on the record, poisons the session on the alert before the caller's next send, but the peek alone cannot classify it | TLS 1.3 encrypts alerts; the peek is a heuristic on either class, the idempotent retry against durable state is the guarantee |

## Q. Rung-10 design calls — directory striping end to end over the wire (2026-10-12; `docs/evidence/rung-10-striping-wire.md`)

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Q.1 A stripe is known by its RECORD | `record.INODE_FLAG_STRIPE` on the stripe's inode (format v5's unused `flags` word): the flip's remainder and every `SupplyStripeInos` mint it set; `stripe.isStripeRecord` is the one question the flip (`IsAStripe`), the served-insert census and the handover window ask. | 0002 row 493: the reference's reverse scan needs a parent memo a nameless stripe never has at a holder that acquired its slot by handover or recovery. One record read answers wherever the record is read. |
| Q.2 Phase A supplies in ONE batch per creator, an even share | `SupplyStripeInos{dir, indices[]}` per creator with `share = K / (creators + 1)` indices (the holder keeps the rest, `Striping.supplyShare`); a declined or unreachable creator leaves its batch to the holder (`dir_stripe_supply_declined`). No speculative reservoir of stripe inos refilled under the renewal carriage. | 0002 row 494. The flip happens once per directory and its cost is `creators` round trips; a reservoir of pre-minted nameless directory records is a population of C9 orphans whenever the flip never comes, for the saving of one RTT per creator per flip. The even share is what striping is for: the load spreads over every writer of the directory from the flip. |
| Q.3 The explicit flip from a non-holder is the `setxattr` COMMAND shipped as a record verb | `kv_fs.setxattr(D, user.squeezefs.stripes)` on a foreign `D` ships `.setxattr{name, value}` over `record_ship`; the holder's `RecordShipService.apply` intercepts the command name and runs `StripingFace.flip` (the plane's face the filesystem binds); a plane without the face rejects. `getxattr` answers the K of a STRIPED directory and ENODATA on any other object (the reference's read face). | The command is a record-level mutation of one object at its holder — exactly the record ship's class; a dedicated verb would duplicate the resolver, the redirect and the door for one name. |
| Q.4 The census' CREATOR is the shipped child's slot holder | `Plane.serveStep` derives the creator of a served insert from the child's slot (`holderOf(child)` — a create in a foreign directory mints the child in the creator's rotor), the requester's word standing in only when the child is local. No peer-identity → appender-id plumbing on the step service. | The spec's own definition (`sym_tokens_intents_striping.md` §4.3 "the CREATOR = the shipped child's slot holder"); the resolver already knows every slot's holder on both postures, where a joiner has no directory to map a wire identity through. |
| Q.5 The shipped publish carries the writer's DMA proofs and the holder stages the refs | `RecordShipHead.n_proofs` + `DmaProofWire[]`; the holder's `PublishFace` (the data plane's) runs the same `publishLayout` as a local publish — the record, the `+ref/−ref` diff against the record in place, the proof law. A publish for an inode with no record at the holder is refused; a holder without a face rejects; `foreign_publish_refusals` is the REQUESTER's count and `record_ships` / `foreign_publish_ships` count the verb once at its terminal reply (a redirect re-dispatches the same verb, a rejection lands nothing) so `ships ≡ served` holds at rest. | 0002 row 495; charter §5.6 (the refs live in the owner's tree, the delta rides the layout transaction) and §6.1 (block I/O strictly before the type flip — the proof type keeps that shape across the wire). |
| Q.6 A FOREIGN-slot file is written through the striped arm, always | `kv_fs.write` routes a file whose slot another appender leases to `writeStriped` whatever its size (`fuse_write_foreign_striped`): the bytes land on a block of the writer's own grant, the promotion's layout and every block-map merge ship as publishes (`DataPlane.publishForeign` / `mapMerge`'s foreign arm → the one `record_ship` publish), the size claim ships as one `setattr{size, mtime, ctime}` (never parked: the holder's record is the fold's authority). A tree-mapped head or the inline cap's crossing on a foreign file is REFUSED, counted and named (`foreign_publish_kvmap_refusals`): the kind-7 records are the holder's slot tree's. | The inline and staged arms stage the inode's record beside the layout in ONE tx the holder's door refuses (`SlotBusy`), and two shipped verbs would break the atomicity the single tx had; the striped arm's three records are each ONE object's mutation at the holder — the record ship's class. A PB-class file written through a foreign holder is a bounded, stated limit (the file is written from its holder), not a silent EAGAIN loop. |
| Q.7 The prelude's verdict cache: maps stand, plain hints carry the lease generation | `kv_fs_stripes.Stripes.verdictOf`: a positive map is cached until the directory's `rmdir` (a map is immutable; an ino never reused); a PLAIN verdict is cached for a LOCAL directory keyed by the slot's lease generation (`ops.Home.local.g`, the lease table's word) and read only under the same generation; a foreign directory's verdict is re-read off its token (RAM while cached); a stripe is known by its record (Q.1) and remembered with its directory. Caps `W / 16` maps (≈ 2 MiB) and `W` hints, flushed whole. | The spec's `unstriped: D → (holder, g)` ("a stale lease stamp reads as no hint"): only the holder flips a directory it holds, so its own flip is the one invalidation while the lease stands, and a slot that left and came back carries a new generation. A map read is `K + 2` targeted name probes (`findDentry` of the markers), never a page walk of the directory. |
| Q.8 The lazy migration runs on the cadence, one page per directory per tick | `Stripes.tick` (the xv cadence) moves one `MIGRATION_SCAN_PAGE` of names per migrating directory per tick (`Striping.migrateRound`), clears `migrating` when a round finds `D`'s tree empty of user names under the exclusive guard; the explicit flip and the census flip both queue the directory. | The reference spawns a task per flip; the census flip runs inside a served step on the wire service thread, where an inline migration of thousands of names would hold the service for seconds. Reads serve from `D`'s own tree as the secondary home and every mutation re-homes first, so the migration's pace is hygiene, not correctness. |
| Q.9 The widest intent image and the wide local set | `intent.IMAGE_MAX` = the header + 135 steps at a marker name (14 B → 16) + one step at `NAME_MAX`: the striped `rmdir` of a K = 64 directory under a long parent name; `guards.Travelling.local` is the WIDE set (`dlm.WideGuardSet`, `lockManyWide`) — the one-intent `rmdir` holds `I{D}` + 64 `I{D_i}` + 67 names locally when the stripes are the holder's. | The smoke's `rm -rf` of a 64-way directory answered EINVAL twice: once off a 56 B-per-step image buffer (the marker names pad to 16), once off the 16-request local set; the spec's bound (`2 × MINT_SPREAD + 8`) was the plan's, and the two buffers that hold it must carry it too. Pinned by the plan row and the K = 64 RAM row. |
| Q.10 The command's read face | `getxattr(user.squeezefs.stripes)` = the map's K on a striped directory, ENODATA elsewhere (the rung-6 binding answered the knob's width on every directory). | The reference's read face (`dir_stripe.rs:116–122`): the command's value is a fact about THIS directory; the knob's width is `.config`'s. |
| Q.11 A handover's released slot is RESERVED for its accepter | The holder's release keeps the offer's stand on the unleased slot (`LeaseTable.reservedFor`); until the accepter's grant lands or the stand ends (`offerStandNs`), a first touch by any other appender — the releasing holder included — is refused naming the accepter (`slot_acquire_reserved_refusals`), and the rotor pick skips it; the resolution names the accepter too (`SlotHolderCache.holderOf`, `ResolveSlot`), so every touch in the gap goes where the accepter's own first touch takes the slot (`LeaseTable.stands` gates the check lock-free: a table without stands — every joiner's — pays nothing). | 0002 row 502. Found by the shared hot directory row: in the release → grant gap the requester's OWN creates, still shipping to the releasing holder, first-touched the slot back there and the accept was refused — the handover undid itself under exactly the load that earned it. |
| Q.12 A joiner resolves a slot it knows nothing of at the manager ONCE | `set_bind.homeOf` on a joiner: a slot neither leased by it nor learnt is asked of the manager (`ResolveSlot`); a holder is learnt, an `unleased` answer remembered (`SlotHolderCache.known_unleased`) so the ask is one per slot, not one per op; a later grant supersedes, a release re-marks. | A joiner's lease table knows its own leases only: a THIRD appender's slot read as the manager's, every step bounced `not_holder`, and the striping census at the directory's holder saw one creator where there were two (the flip never came). |
| Q.13 The census flip runs on the cadence | A served insert that makes its directory DUE queues it (`Striping.pending`); the xv cadence (`Stripes.tick` → `runPending`) flips it, a failure named loud and counted (`dir_stripe_flip_failures`), the directory re-entering the census at its next served insert. | The served step that made the directory due still holds its initiator's travelling scope over `I{D}` at this holder while the initiator waits for the step's reply; the flip's own `I{D}` waited for that scope — every create into the directory parked until the scope's grace belt (45 s). |
| Q.14 The served guard acquisition never parks the session | `XvGuards` and an uncovered `XvStep` take their stripes in the TRY form at the holder; a held stripe answers `Busy` (nothing taken, nothing parked; `xv_cross_owner_guards_contended`) and the INITIATOR retries on its own thread with a doubling backoff 1 → 16 ms under the wire's reply bound (`xv_cross_owner_guard_retries`; past the bound `GuardRefused`, `xv_cross_owner_guard_contention_refusals`). | 0002 row 503. A listener serves one frame at a time per connection while the dialer multiplexes every call of a daemon to that peer on ONE session: a served ask parked on a stripe another scope of the SAME initiator held waited behind itself (that scope's step and release frames queued behind it) until the grace belt. The retry spends the patience a parked ask had where it blocks nobody. |
| Q.15 A joiner's word on every other member is the owner's census | `membership.census_view.CensusView`: the member's beat pulls the owner's `Census` pages once per renew interval (the page carries `grace_open`); a row `live/grace` ⇒ live, `departed` ⇒ expired, ABSENT ⇒ expired only when the view is newer than the asker's last contact with the member (`since_ns`) and the owner's grace window is closed; a view older than two renew intervals answers unknown. ONE law for the token holder's lease oracle (`since` = the reader's last grant ask) and the parked guard scopes' sweep (`since` = the instant the scope parked; a dead member's scopes go with its lease, `xv_cross_owner_guards_swept_dead`); the census commit runs the token holder's sweep at once (`Holder.sweepExpired`) and a pass waiting on the corpse's recall wakes; the commit names its DEPARTURES with the listener each advertised (limbo under the owner's grace window), and every reader plane bound to a departed listener is re-pointed with every token dropped (`joined_dead_holder_planes_dropped`) — a dead holder's grants are recalled by no one once its slots are recovered. | 0002 row 504. A joiner hears no eviction: its holder judged a dead reader `unknown` and waited each recall to the 45 s deadline (a served step's commit, and the session serving it, parked that long), and its parked scopes of the dead member held their stripes the whole belt. The `since` term is what keeps the census from killing a member that joined after the pull. |
| Q.16 A holder that cannot be reached is re-resolved once inside the op | The step dispatch (`ops.dispatch`: `unreachable_` or a failed ship ⇒ `refresh`, up to `SLOT_MOVED_RETRIES`, then the intent stays open, counted abandoned) and the token read (`Divert.serve`: no endpoint, a refused or lost session, a re-probe that fails ⇒ ONE `refresh` shared with the redirect's follow, `dlm_token_reader_unreachable_resolves`); the same holder named again is the typed EAGAIN; a manager's `unleased` answer also drops the joiner's table projection of a foreign holder (a refused first touch loads one; `LeaseTable.dropForeign`), never its own lease. | A dead holder's slot the manager has recovered is not announced to anyone: a creator kept dialling the corpse until its intents stood past the TTL (`xv_cross_owner_intent_stuck`), and a joiner's `ls` of the striped directory answered EAGAIN for every stripe the dead holder had held. The manager's word is the one re-resolution; a dead holder never redirects. |
| Q.17 The shared hot directory row's writers die with the row | The row's storms are process-group leaders killed by the row's teardown on every exit path (success, a failed expectation, a timeout), and each loop also ends when the test process is gone; the harness checks `pgrep -fc 'true > "hot/'` = 0 after every run. | An earlier draft left six orphaned writer loops spinning on a detached mount (parent = init): a test's writers are its resources, released like its daemons. |
| Q.18 Every served mutation's reply follows the holder's durability lane | `Plane.serveStep`, `RecordShipService.apply` and `SupplyService` barrier the holder's ring after their commit and before the reply; a failed barrier answers the I/O class. | The spec's law (§2.3: "the holder's reply follows its durability lane") was kept by no served arm: in the coalesced posture a commit acks before its window lands, and the requester ACTS on the answer (retires its intent, acks its application, names the stripe in a map) — a holder killed inside the window lost the mutation with nothing left to replay it. One ring barrier per served mutation, shared by the conveyor's group commit. |
| Q.19 A roll-forward that re-meets a live refusal compensates | `recoverOne` keeps each step's outcome; a `foreign_skipped` runs the live compensation over the steps applied by the pass and the fresh mints found applied, then retires (`xv_recovered_compensated`). | 0002 row 505. A ship that failed against a dead holder leaves its intent open and answers EAGAIN; the caller's retry of the same name wins it after the recovery, and the old skip-then-retire orphaned the first attempt's child (fsck C9). Only a fresh mint's effect is attributable to the intent; every other step's earlier effect stands. |
| Q.20 A striped lookup reads the stripe, `D`, then the stripe again | `stripe.locateName`: on a miss in both, the stripe is read once more. | 0002 row 506. A move out of `D` inserts into the stripe before it removes from `D`, so the third read closes the window the first two leave; the cost is one cached-window read on a negative lookup. The merged readdir has the same window across its streams' refills and is not closed here (the rung-10 striping evidence note's owed item). |

## R. Rung-10 job fabric design calls (2026-10-12; `docs/evidence/rung-10-jobs.md`)

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D3's "spilled" term | `defrag --fold`'s targets are the write side's PARKED W2 extents alone (`accumulate.Table.extentKeys`); the spec's `Σ spilled extent-record bytes` is 0 by construction in this port — a parked extent under RAM pressure ESCALATES whole into the staging ring and is handed to the writeback worker at once (`writeback.spillParkedBlock`), so no durable extent record waits for a fold (the `extent_overlay` record codec stays the recovery scan's) | `ops_jobs_lifecycle.md` §3.1 names the sum; the port's spill is already a flush in flight, and a second fold of it would be a reimplementation the row forbids ("never reimplemented") |
| The fold kick | `plane.foldKick(ino)` runs `accumulate.foldExtentBlock` on every parked extent block of the ino — the in-place landing for a sole-owner passthrough block (one page DMA per touched run), the seeded escalation to a content-valid full buffer otherwise (the block then lands once, at its flush, like any parked buffer); the count returned is the entries that left the extent class | the §J landings are the write side's law (0003 §J.1); the job relieves `parked_extent_bytes` without inventing a fourth landing; the venue row reads device ÷ user = 8× for 512 B sector-aligned writes, exactly the bound |
| D4's candidate measure | `leaf_merge.leafOccupancy`: `indexed` = the serialized log past the header page, `live` = the ON-DISK length of the one frame a compaction writes for the fold's upper bound (page-rounded like the log); a leaf compacts when `dead ≥ 500 ‰` (`smo.DEFRAG_COMPACT_DEAD_PERMILLE`) | the one law's node face (the pack face's `live ≤ CHUNK/2`): the rewrite costs `live`, reclaims `log − live`, pays iff `live ≤ log/2`. Measured raw (fold bytes against the log) the first live row rewrote 7,742 one-page leaves whose only "dead" share was the frame's page rounding — a rewrite cannot shrink a leaf already one frame long, so the measure must round both sides |
| D4's serialization | both faces run under the volume's `smo_lock` — the compaction nudge (`smo.compactForDefrag`: `freezeForSmo` then `smoReplace` with the FIFO valve KEPT, a cycle run on `PendingFreeFull` / `JournalReserveExhausted` and one retry, `NoSpace` ending the chunk) and the merge lap (`leaf_merge.mergeUnderfull` with the valve kept, bounded calls of one tick period, the lock dropped between calls, one whole lap per tree) | the SMO context is ONE per volume by the checkpoint task's law; a maintenance job is not the flush pass and must not take its forced-retirement posture (the pass's forcing exists to discharge tail-pinning floors; a job forcing it would walk past the FIFO valve for no floor's sake) |
| `admin_fsck`, not the generic submit | `fsck|scrub --online [--shards k/N]` rides its own admin verb (0x0706, `AdminFsckReq{flags, throttle, shard_k, shard_n}` → `AdminJobSubmitted`); the generic `admin_job_submit` REFUSES `JobType.fsck` (as it refuses `.rekey`); the shard travels in the record's `arg1` as `N ‖ k` (`fsck.jobShardArg`), 0 = the whole set | the shard word is validated at the codec (`k < N`, the known option bits, a duty ≤ 100) — never a CLI's private encoding of `arg1`; an adopted sharded run resumes as the same shard because the word is durable |
| The online shard's law | an online `--shards k/N` judges its residue exactly as the offline shard does (the allocator-dependent classes, C8, C11, C12 and C13–C17 skipped — the whole set's run keeps them); `merge-reports` over the N online reports ≡ the whole online report's inode plane (the venue row: 3 shards summed to the whole run's inode census, 0 findings each side) | `ops_fsck.md` §5's partition key is the ONE law for both modes; the reference refused `--shards` online only because its online run was coordinator-whole by construction |
| The JOINED posture's binding | at every online pass the host's `SlotScope` is REBUILT from the live forest (`runner.linux.refreshLiveScope`): the posture (`member` on a joiner, `manager` on the D0 winner), every routing slot classed off tree 0's `slot_state` and the appender pages — leased HERE (this process's appender id; a native slot of a volume it manages), FOREIGN-LIVE (another lessee whose page is Live/Recovering, or the recovery's `releasing` mark), UNLEASED (nobody's) — and the WINDOWS of every live foreign appender read off the device (`ringPartOf` → `replayScanImage` from the page's tail; the slot-tree frames' GLOBAL inos, a dentry's child off a Put's value or through the tree for a DELETE) into `window_inos`; a ring that cannot be read sets `window_unknown` (the inode plane abstains, loud) | `ops_fsck.md` §2.5; the mount-time binding ("every slot this open's") judged a joiner's slots as the manager's own — a live lessee's tree changes under the census. A flat set keeps the open's binding. The window scan is a snapshot of a ring being appended: the journal's total decoder stops at the first torn entry, which is the appender's in-flight write, never an acked record |
| C17's `missing_stripe` under scoping | a stripe named by a map whose ino sits in a slot the census does not judge, or past the start watermark, is NOT missing — `hasRecord` answers true (its record is its holder's to judge) | the stripes of ONE directory are minted across the members' slots; the first N-daemon online run read every foreign stripe as `missing_stripe` — the zero-FP law before the class's reach |
| C16 on a real set — NOT bound (stated) | `host.shared` stays null on the Linux binding: the class does not run online or offline on a real set | the port has no PRODUCT index home: the clone protocol's kind-9 `ShareBlock`/`ReleaseShared` verbs are served by the loopback hub alone (`shared_index.IndexHome` over `RamIndexStore` in `clone_protocol.zig`'s rows) and the offline `clone` shares by SHARED `+ref` on both owners without an index entry — so a kind-6/kind-9 drift check over a real set would report every clone's reference as `flag_without_entry`: a false positive by construction. The face binds the day the symmetric plane's owner lands the index home on tree 0 (the next wave of `rung-10-symmetric-completion.md` §5e) |
| C13's live-appender arm — NOT bound (stated) | `orphanImagesFn` stays the offline/recovery census's (empty on a mounted set) | the class judges "claimed in a grant ∧ reached by no root" UNDER THE APPENDER'S OWN SMO SERIALIZATION (an image between its claim and its publication is the law's exemption); the coordinator's fsck runs on the manager, which holds no joiner's serialization and so cannot judge a live joiner's grants without a census verb the member answers under its `smo_lock` (plus the `slot_tails` spill runs the joiner's grant also carries); the manager's own appender 0 holds no grants (it allocates from the heap). A member-side `census_orphan_images` verb is the next wave's; fabricating the arm from the manager's view would read every in-flight successor as an orphan |
| A worker is a member daemon | a JOINED mount with `SQUEEZEFS_FLEET_JOBS` on runs ONE fleet worker fiber (`coordinator.Node.fleetMain`) enrolled `CAP_FLEET_READ` at the coordinator over the member's OWN manager link (`joined.SharedLink` — the session the membership plane dials), re-enrolling at the heartbeat cadence while the block is unserved (a manager mid-bring-up) and after a dead link; its engine is the mount's fsck `JobBody` over the mount's own binding (`readRunner`: the residue the descriptor names, the member's posture and scope, the scrub through its plane's read path) | charter §8 "remote workers … a worker is a member daemon"; the `job worker` PROCESS stays the operator's noop-class worker (the matrix row). Riding the member's session makes the member's death THE departure: the listener's hook expires its shards at once (no 30 s wait) — the lease TTL stays the law for a hung-but-connected worker (the sweeper) |
| The fleet dispatcher's shape | `FleetDispatch{readCapacity, queueReadShard, collectReadShard, release, retireFleetShards, shardLeaseTtlMs}`: the coordinator QUEUES shards `1..n` (no destinations — the lease's fencing arm alone), runs shard 0 itself ONLINE, then collects: a read-capable PULL leases a queued shard (fencing 0, or bumped on a re-lease), `read_shard_result` is `complete`d under the lease's fencing and its payload kept for the collector; a lease the sweeper or a departure expired re-queues the shard while `attempts < 2 ∧ capacity > 0`, else it is LOST and the collector runs the residue locally (`job_fleet_shards_relocal`); the collector's progress bound is 4 TTLs; the pass end retires the job's shards (a zombie's late proposal is the unknown-shard rejection) | spec §1.9a verbatim with the pull shape of 0003 §G; the queue/collect split (not one blocking dispatch) lets shard 0 run while the fleet walks. Every residue lands exactly once: a proposal under a bumped fencing is stale; a re-lease and a local run are mutually exclusive by the shard's state word under the coordinator's lock |
| The worker's heartbeat is a fiber | a read shard's walk runs with a concurrent heartbeat fiber (`Worker.Heartbeat`: a renewal every `heartbeat_ms`, the local lease clock extended by each ack; a non-live verdict or a dead wire raises the engine's CANCEL — the walk ends at its next step and the shard is ABANDONED, never proposed) | the reference's `sqz-jw-hb` thread; the engine's own per-step `cancelled()` read is the one stop latch every walk already honours (the online job's pause/cancel ride the same word), so no second seam enters the census |
| The fleet residue is a CUSTODY, never an ino residue | `runFleet` partitions the set by custody: the coordinator runs the whole of ITS view (shard null), every read-capable member runs its engine over the inos of the slots IT leases (`Host.custody_only`, fed by the LIVE lease table `heldBy(me)`, never by a joiner's tree-0 projection); the dispatcher's `fleet_k/fleet_n` name the residue, not an ino class | a live lessee's slot trees leave the coordinator's forest at the grant (`forgetTree`) — an ino-residue shard at the coordinator could not read half the data (the first venue run merged 128 of 256 blocks); the member's custody is exactly what the coordinator cannot see |
| The custody run's classes | a member's custody judges the block plane's SCRUB and C1/C4 of its slots; the name-dependent arms (C9, C10) take no verdict there (`dentries.complete = false`: its names live in other members' directories) and the allocator arms (C2/C3/C6) none at all (a non-holder's allocator is a pulled window); a LOST custody counts `job_fleet_shards_relocal` and the coordinator re-runs its own view ONCE (a dead member's recovered slots are its again; a live-but-silent member's stay its own) | every residue lands exactly once; the first fleet run read B's own directory as C9 (its name is in A's root) and B's 128 blocks as C2-lost (B's allocator tracks none of them) |
| The coordinator's reference-count arms under a live lessee | C3, the leaked arm and C8 take NO verdict while any slot is foreign-live (`alloc_census_declined`); a block a visible foreign record references is scoped out of every allocator arm and counted (`block_plane_foreign_scoped`, `block_refs.Exemptions.isForeignRef`); the lost arm stays | the coordinator's derived counts are incomplete by construction (the lessee's trees left its forest with every record they carry — a predecessor's files included: `refcount 64 vs 63` on packs A itself promoted); the members' derived censuses merged at the coordinator are the fleet's next step |
| C4's custody under scoping | a staging custody naming an ino of an unjudged slot, or one minted in the current era, is scoped and counted (`c4_custody_scoped`), never an orphan | the staging dir is the data plane's: a joiner stages through it too (the two-daemon row read 103 of B's inos as C4 orphans) |
| C10's cross-owner names | a dentry whose CHILD sits in an unjudged slot is scoped like a dentry of that slot (the child's record is the lessee's); a name the bitmap cannot index takes the inode plane's gates (the lessee scope, ONLINE the era floor, then the record) instead of a blind dangling verdict | a joiner's files under the manager's directory read as 50 dangling names on the set |
| The offline open's forest classes | `bind` classes every slot off tree 0 and the pages for the OFFLINE posture too (the probe keeps its prior-era law): an appender LIVE on the directory with its ring unrecovered keeps its slots foreign-live | the open refuses those slots until the recovery ladder runs; the offline fsck after a killed member read the member's directory name as dangling |
| `commitLocked`'s failed arm names the tx's owner | `CommitOutcome.failed: enum { publish, commit }` — a failed publish leaves the tx the caller's to abandon, a failed COMMIT was consumed by the backend (`KvBackend.commit`'s contract) | the dismount promotion of a joiner abandoned a consumed tx (`KvTx.deinit` on `undefined` → the bus error at `0xaaaa…`) |
| The job list's row count is `usize` | `@min(out.items.len, LIST_ROWS_MAX)` narrows to u9 and `n * 96` overflows at the sixth record; the count is typed `usize` on its line | the online fsck's sixth submission killed the daemon (`admin_job_list` panicked in the wire thread) |
| The rig's second daemon joins a PROMOTED set | the two-daemon stage remounts A (its dismount promotes every resident) before B joins, and asserts B's `staging_recovery_records == 0` | the rig's daemons share ONE cache root (the paths are the set's format decision) and the staging marker is scoped to the set alone (`openStaging` writes `node_token = 0`, `bindVerdict(.., peer_live = false)`): a second mount on the host ADOPTS the first's live residents (B recovered A's 200 records and promoted them at ITS dismount through slots it did not hold: 197 refused, the pack pin tripwire) — the marker's per-mount scope is owed to the data plane |
| The kill row's window is the duty cycle | the fleet row kills B inside its residue by throttling the job to 5 % (a 512 MiB residue stretched to tens of seconds; B's `job_fleet_worker_shards` moves at the shard's START) instead of a remount with the hold seam | a joiner's clean unmount + rejoin while the manager stays up refuses the mount (`corruption outside the replay window`, reproduced with no fsck in play) — a symmetric-plane defect stated to its owner, not this rung's |
| The name arms under a live lessee | C9's reverse difference and C10's count arms take no verdict while any slot is foreign-live or any dentry was scoped (`DentryPass.names_complete`, `inode_plane_names_declined`); C10's dangling arm over a judged parent and child stays | a slot is the container: a lessee's tree carries every record ever minted in it — a predecessor's directories and the names they hold — and left this forest at the grant; the two-daemon row read A's own `d/e` as C9 once B leased A's released rotor slot 1 (`rung-10-jobs.md` §3a) |
| A join row's rendezvous is the join | the accumulate row waits on `write_pipeline_join_waits` (the joiner OBSERVED the in-flight upload), never on `upload_settled.waiters()` (a registration taken before the lock) | `.discarded` is the correct word for an entry gone before the joiner's first look; 200/200 under load without a reproduction, the hole by inspection |
| The merged block census | a member's custody proposal carries its derived census (`Report.owners`, dropped by `merge`); the coordinator runs the LOST arm and C3 over every member-referenced block against its allocators (`classes.crossMemberBlockPlane`: the holder's bitmap bit exempts, and the ladder's own live-owner shields — `liveOwnerShielded`, the in-flight registry then the pack-open ledger, ONE function for both arms), and CONFIRMS a suspect against a second census round from the members AND the holder's own view census'd again (`runner.confirmMerged`, the snapshot law: every side's references read after the first round's shields, the allocators re-read after both; confirming against the holder's pass-start census re-judged its own release in between as the same suspect twice); the leaked arm and C8 stay declined under a live lessee | the data-loss class of 0002 row 500 (a nonterminal free the holder executed under live tenants) read clean: no arm had the lessee's references; the second round is the ladder's re-check with the member's own fresh evidence (a reference deleted in between clears); the pack-open ledger joined 2026-10-24: an OPEN pack of the holder whose tenants straddle its view and a member's custody holds `refcount = tenants + 1` (the pin) — the ONLINE FSCK row's `[C3] refcount 37 vs 36` (100 promoted 64 KiB tenants per volume = 64 sealed + 36 open), offline clean every time (`rung-10-c3-open-pack.md`) |
| The holder's bitmaps reach the online binding | `runner.linux.BitmapFace` over the wire plane's holder plane, resolved at every pass (`Bound.refreshBitmaps`) | the Linux binding carried `bitmap = null` (the flat arm) since rung 8a: the C6 bitmap arm and the lessee-carve exemption never ran on a live set |

## S. The orderly unmount of a BUSY mount (2026-10-12; `docs/evidence/rung-10-umount-busy.md`)

The defect (reproduced with `scripts/dev_mount.sh`): `squeezefs umount <mnt>` while another process held
a reference inside the mount (a shell's cwd, an open fd) met EBUSY after the session's drain, detached
lazily, and — fusectl not mounted, so the abort rung was a silent no-op — sat in the workers' 30 s
residency answering ENOTCONN on a connection the holder kept alive; the supervisor's probe (a stat of
`<mnt>/.stats`, a path that no longer existed) missed six times and SIGABRT'd a healthy daemon; the verb
saw "exited ∧ not mounted" and returned 0 after 29 s. Three laws broken: charter §1.1's no-wedge (a daemon
that stops answering a connection it still owns), the supervisor's "unresponsive" (a detached-but-referenced
mount is a DEFINED state), and the verb's truth (0 after a kill).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **The contract: shape (a), refuse loud, by default** | `squeezefs umount <mnt>` runs the holder census (`cli/holders.zig`: every process's cwd/root/exe and every fd through `statx(STATX_MNT_ID, AT_STATX_DONT_SYNC)` on the `/proc` magic links (never `fdinfo`: its read generates the fd's whole document — a fanotify or inotify instance prints every mark), mappings through `maps` by device, stacked mounts by parent id — keyed by the kernel's MOUNT ID, never by path text) BEFORE it signals. A held mount answers `error.Busy` (EBUSY through the ONE table), the holders named on stderr (`pid 1234 (sleep) cwd`, `pid 40 (vim) fd 3`, `mmap`, `a mount stacked at …`; eight named, the rest counted), exit 1 in milliseconds, nothing signalled: the mount keeps serving. The verb `chdir`s to `/` first (it is typed from inside the mount more often than not) | An operator's `umount` of a busy mount is EBUSY on every Linux filesystem; the surprise was the silent detach. The census exists because the kernel's one authority on "busy" — `umount2` — is destructive on success and the charter's teardown order (§4 "drains RELEASE every retained slot … BEFORE the unmount, while the connection is live") cannot be undone once begun: the question must be asked before the drains, and the only non-destructive way to ask it is to look |
| **The daemon refuses too** | SIGTERM/SIGINT (what the verb sends; a raw `kill -TERM` as well) runs the same census in the serve loop before any teardown: busy ⇒ the stop is REFUSED — logged with the holders, counted `transport_umount_busy_refusals`, the daemon serving on; the next signal re-asks. The verb reads the counter back through `.stats` (every 100 ms = the drain's lease budget, only while the mount is attached and the daemon alive) so a holder that arrived between the two censuses is still answered EBUSY with its name | Two censuses close the window to the drain's milliseconds; the daemon's is the one that matters (it decides the teardown), the verb's is the fast answer and the names on the console |
| **Shape (b) behind `--lazy` / `-l`** (SIGUSR2) | An unheld mount stops orderly (the same drain-first `close`, the plain `umount2`). A held one is detached NOW (`Session.detachLazily`: `MNT_DETACH`; `fusermount3 -u -z` unprivileged) and the daemon keeps serving EVERY request on the detached superblock until the kernel ends the connection — the last holder's reference drops, the superblock dies, `fuse_abort_conn` ends the sideband's `/dev/fuse` read, the `on_shutdown` hook fires and the teardown runs (staged promotion, final checkpoint, claim release). The verb returns at once naming the holders it leaves (exit 0: the detach happened, the daemon is healthy) and waits for the exit only when none are left | The explicit opt-in to the libfuse/reference behaviour, with the one difference that matters: the daemon never stops answering before the kernel's end. The drain-first order cannot hold here — the superblock's death is the kernel's event — so a retained zc slot at that moment is `transport_retained_kernel_drained` (counted, the recourse the charter names for a kernel-begun disconnect) |
| **The verb's verdict is order-based on kernel facts** — never 0 after a kill | Watched: the mount's id at the path (`/proc/self/mountinfo`), the daemon's liveness, the census. The mount's entry gone while the daemon lived (or the daemon gone with the mount detached and no holder left on its id) ⇒ its own unmount ⇒ 0 once exited. The daemon gone with the mount still attached, or with holders still on a lazily detached mount ⇒ a death (the supervisor's ladder, a crash) ⇒ 1, the dead connection detached externally so it does not sit at the path as `ENOTCONN`. Alive past the bound ⇒ 1, never detached from under. No log is parsed, no wait status is needed: the verb is not the daemon's parent (the supervisor is, and logs the status) | charter §8 "no string-parsed outcomes decide a fail-stop" generalized to the verb's exit code; the ORDER of facts (unmount before exit) is the kernel's own record of an orderly teardown |
| **The supervisor probes by the mount's STATE** | `probeKind(attached)`: the control-inode stat while the mount's id is in the table at the mountpoint (the charter's probe: a LOOKUP with TTL 0 that proves the FUSE path answers); the daemon's HEARTBEAT once it is not — one `MAP_SHARED\|MAP_ANONYMOUS` page the intermediate maps BEFORE forking the daemon, stamped at `ready` and by every watchdog tick (`Watchdog.on_tick`), stale after two ticks, the same 30 s law to escalation. A stat that failed on a mount that left the table meanwhile is the teardown in progress, judged by the heartbeat. The watchdog now runs to the END of the teardown (its stop moved below `be.close()`): a long dismount pass (many staged files promoting) is no wedge | A detached-but-referenced mount and a daemon inside its teardown are defined states with no path to stat; the heartbeat is the one thing the daemon always answers. It proves the executor's meta lane schedules (the tick is a fiber there), which is the liveness a stat proved for the FUSE lanes; a wedge in the FUSE path of a DETACHED mount is the daemon's own deadline watchdog's to log (the supervisor's stat could not see it either — no path). The escalation ladder is unchanged for a real wedge and names the probe that failed |
| The race-case exit | An EBUSY that outlives the 2 s window AFTER the drain (a holder inside the drain's milliseconds, both censuses clean) is named for what it is in the log, detached lazily (fusectl's abort when mounted), and the workers' residency ends at the detach (`Shared.detached`) instead of 30 s later: the straggler's next request answers ENOTCONN while the workers live and `ECONNABORTED` at the exit (the kernel aborts the connection when the last `fuse_dev` goes); the daemon finishes its teardown in its normal time | The session is drained; there is no way back to serving. A prompt, loud, clean exit beats 30 s of ENOTCONN; the straggler's wake owner is the exit, bounded by the teardown. The old 30 s was the supervisor's kill window, which this ruling removes from the orderly path entirely |
| The session's `umount2` by path proves the mount is still ITS mount | The mount id captured at `mount(2)` (`core/mountinfo.zig`) is compared before every `umount2`/`MNT_DETACH` by path; a kernel-ended mount with a successor at the same path is left alone ("no longer this session's mount") | A path is not an identity: an external `umount -l` + a remount by another daemon at the same path, then our teardown, would have unmounted the successor |
| Not walked | A per-thread cwd/root (`unshare(CLONE_FS)`), another mount namespace's view, mappings of a bind of the same superblock elsewhere (they match by device, a documented over-count); unreadable processes are skipped — another user's under an unprivileged verb, and a NON-DUMPABLE one (a hardened daemon, a setuid program) when the caller lacks `CAP_SYS_PTRACE` (root on a host has it; the venue container does not: the gate found it through the census rows' forked holder, which inherited the unit process's hardening — the rows make it dumpable) | The census is the names and the early answer; the kernel's `umount2` stays the authority on "busy" — a miss meets the race-case exit, an over-count meets the operator's retry or `--lazy` |
| `--lazy` only to a daemon of this build | The verb compares `.stats`' `build.commit` with its own before SIGUSR2 and refuses a foreign build by name (exit 1, "the orderly stop works across builds") | SIGUSR2's default action terminates a process with no handler: a verb from this tree sending it to an older daemon would be a kill dressed as an unmount |
| Rejected: un-shutdown on EBUSY | Reversing `Session.close` (drains done, the host disarmed, the hook fired) to resume serving | Not a state machine this port has or wants; the census before the drain makes it unnecessary except in the drain's own window |
| Rejected: the verb's own `umount2(MNT_DETACH)` for `--lazy` | The daemon owns the detach (SIGUSR2) | The daemon logs the fact with the holders, keeps `mounted` honest for its own teardown, and the unprivileged path rides the existing `fusermount3` seam; an external detach gives the daemon no word |

## T. Rung-10 staging scope and the joiner's rejoin (2026-10-12; `docs/evidence/rung-10-staging-scope-rejoin.md`)

Two defects the jobs agent stated (`rung-10-jobs.md` §4) and one the rejoin row found under them. T.1 supersedes §R's "the rig's second daemon joins a PROMOTED set" (the online fsck row's B now joins beside A's live residents and recovers none).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| T.1 The staging ring's scope is the MEMBER | every mount binds its OWN root under the format's cache path, `<cache path>/member-<mount slot as 8 hex>` (`staging.Ring.bindMember`): its marker, its staging segment and its NVMe read-cache segments. The member scope is the mount slot of the identity the mount presents on meta volume 0 — the manager's home-volume index (0), a joiner's mount-point derivation (`JOINER_MOUNT_SLOT_BIT ‖ hash31(mount point)`), a `--single-writer` D0 winner's 0 — stable across the member's incarnations (the node token is boot-scoped and is NOT the scope: a reboot's remount at the same mount point is the successor). The LIVE CLAIM is an exclusive `flock` on the member root's directory taken BEFORE its marker is read and held for the ring's life (the kernel drops it at the process's death, `kill -9` included). The marker (v3, 80 B) names its owner: the set, the epoch, `(node, mount slot)`, the appender id and the stamping incarnation's pid. The ladder under the claim: no marker ⇒ fresh; our set + our member ⇒ ADOPT (a predecessor incarnation's residue is its successor's — the §5.9 successor of the manager's region is the D0 winner, slot 0; a joiner's is its remount at the same mount point); another member's marker ⇒ `StagingForeignOwner` REFUSED, never adopted, never wiped; another set, a newer epoch or an unreadable marker ⇒ wiped (a cache path is one set's). A root whose claim another process holds ⇒ `StagingOwnerLive` REFUSED naming the owner — two mounts presenting one member scope, or ANOTHER SET formatted on the same cache path (the lever named: `config set-cache-paths`). Other members' ownerless roots holding ring bytes are reported loud at every bind (`staging_member_residue_roots`), never touched. A `-o ro` reader binds no root (staging and the NVMe tier OFF). The `staging list` census never scans a LIVE member's ring (its `.stats` is the authority) and scans a dead one through a PRIVATE mapping (the recovery scan's tombstones stay in its own pages) | the old marker carried `node_token = 0` and the bind passed `peer_live = false`: two mounts of one set on one host bound ONE ring — B adopted A's 200 live residents at its open and promoted them at ITS dismount through slots it did not hold (197 refused, the pack pin tripwire), two writers mapping one segment with two placement indexes; a second SET on the same cache path read A's marker as dead and WIPED A's live ring (A's 200 acked staged files lost at its next `kill -9`, measured on dev); the NVMe tier's shards were punched under a live peer at every co-located open; and `staging list` ran a WRITING recovery scan (tombstones, punches) over whatever ring it found. The reference (`R/config_ops.rs` `mount_scoped_staging_prelude`) roots per mount point with a liveness lock too; its content-judged peer ladder is 0002 row 501 |
| T.2 The joined arm's projection law | at the JOINED arm (`slots.Plane.armJoined`) every tree-0 projection row naming the joiner's OWN appender id loads `unleased` (`lease_table.joinedProjectionRow`: the words kept as the cold floor, the holder cache never learns it); counted `slot_projection_stale_self_rows`, logged at info. This incarnation holds exactly the manager's wire grants; a first touch of such a slot asks the manager, the live truth | the joined open refuses a `Live` page under its identity (`PredecessorLive`), so a rejoin is always a fresh incarnation — but the manager's hint hands it the SAME appender id, and the projection a rejoin reads is the manager's last record plus whatever tree-0 leaves an SMO carried onto the device since: rows of the previous incarnation's leases (`Leased{1, g}`) whose releases the manager journaled but had not yet recorded. Loaded as held, they were this incarnation's leases to the door and the page plan: its first cycle published their roots (`PublishRoots`), the manager refused (tree 0: `Unleased`), and the open answered `Corrupt` ("corruption outside the replay window") — and had a publication not been due, the door would have admitted writes into slot trees this mount did not lease. Reproduced 4 of 5 on the venue (B remounting inside A's 1 s cadence); deterministic in the unit row (one rotor slot, the manager's cadence held) |
| T.3 A non-holder ships only its TERMINAL frees | `Reclaimer.freeBlock` on a non-holder first releases the reference LOCALLY while its RAM count covers another (`BlockAllocator.releaseUnlessLast`: CAS down from ≥ 2, a SHARED block excluded) — nonterminal, nothing shipped; the last reference, a SHARED block and an untracked one ship as before, and the shipper's verdict seams retire or release the local word | charter §5.5 "terminal frees of a non-holder SHIP"; the port shipped EVERY release before its own ladder ran. The holder cannot read the references a live lessee's slot trees carry, so its untracked arm seeds ONE reference and frees the block: a joiner's dismount sealed its packs, the seal's pin drop shipped, and A punched both pack blocks under their live tenants — every staged file the joiner had written read EIO on A and ZEROS everywhere after (fsck clean: the refs and layouts were intact, the bytes gone). Found by the rejoin row's staged files; the same path freed a pack whenever one tenant of a joiner's pack was deleted |

## U. Rung-10 recovery correctness (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-recovery-correctness.md`)

The owed items of `docs/evidence/rung-10-striping-wire.md` §3.6 around §5.9's recovery: the
recovering manager's abort, the coherence window after a recovery, the merged readdir's window,
an abandoned `link`'s undo, a dead member's endpoint row, the untested shapes.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| U.1 An extent leaves a dead grant by ONE path | From its first root install (§5.9 step 4) to its table release (7b) a recovery HOLDS every retirement of a dead grant's extent an SMO on the volume makes (`smo.RetireHold` on the volume's `SmoContext`, consulted first by `freePending`): the bit stays set, the coverage clock never sees it. Step 7's record is the grant minus `keptExtents` — the images of every tree this process maintains (the recovered slots', the unleased ones, its own regions', the control and native trees; a live foreign lessee's projection is never walked) and the held — read in ONE SMO-lock window; the census judges only the record's remainder; 7b parks the held extents in the FIFO gated on ring 0's reservation head (every gate the FIFO took is a position below it, and so is each held extent's own free record — later is conservative); a rollback drops the hold and keeps them allocated (the restored tree may still reach them). Every return keeps only the extents the record names and clears exactly those bits. **Ownership law:** an extent of a dead grant has ONE custodian at every instant — the grant (named by the record), a tree (reached), the recovery's hold, or the coverage clock — and moves between them only under the volume's SMO lock; the lock order is the SMO lock → the manager's verbs lock (the returns run after the hold ends, outside the SMO lock). **The structure law beside it:** the run holds the SMO lock from its first root install to its replay's last record (`Volume.holdStructure`), ring 0's seq floor is raised above the dead ring's frontier BEFORE the install (a manager pointer flip stamped below the dead window's interior record for the same separator loses the per-key LWW), and every walk of a recovered tree (the tails, the kept extents) runs under the SMO lock. | 0002 row 508: an image an SMO retired mid-recovery was named by the record and reached by no root, so the census returned it while the FIFO still parked it — two custodians — and the FIFO's later drain cleared a live node's bit (the row's `freePending` abort). Holding the retirement keeps the extent off the coverage clock until the record has let it go; the head gate keeps the single-producer FIFO monotone. |
| U.2 The successor holds a recovered slot's writes until every member's census retired the dead holder | At the table release (§5.9 step 7b) every recovered slot takes a COHERENCE HOLD until the death record's ts + `coherenceHoldMs = VALIDITY_RENEWALS × renew + 2 × skew_max` (`recovery.coherenceHoldMs`; 20,044 ms past the eviction at the shipped `T_owner` of 45 s, 534 ms at the smoke's 3 s), kept on the volume's monotonic clock (`LeaseTable.holdCoherence`): the door answers a commit's first touch `SlotBusy` (`slot_door_coherence_refusals`, the EAGAIN class the recovery's own window answers), `AcquireSlot` refuses a joiner's first touch naming the manager (`slot_acquire_coherence_refusals`), the rotor pick skips the slot. Reads are never held. Rejected: pushing the eviction to the joiners on the renewal carriage — the carriage reaches a member at its NEXT renewal (≤ renew + one beat), so whenever the recovery lands sooner (at the shipped clocks ≈ 2.4 s after the eviction against a 10 s renew) the successor's first write still precedes some member's drop; it would also need the hold as its bound for a member whose renewal is late. | A joiner drops the tokens a dead holder granted it only at the census commit that retires the holder (0003 §Q.15, fix 11 of `rung-10-striping-wire.md`), and nobody else can recall them: the dead holder's grant table died with it. Every member pulls the owner's census once per renew interval and a view answers for `VALIDITY_RENEWALS = 2` of them (one late pull kept), so by the hold's end every member whose view still stands has committed a view naming the death; a write before it could contradict a token a live member still serves. The price is availability, stated: at the shipped clocks a recovered slot takes writes ≈ 17.6 s later than the recovery lands (≈ 65 s after the death instead of ≈ 47.5 s); the smoke's clocks pay nothing (the recovery lands seconds past the hold). A member whose census pulls keep failing keeps its cached tokens past the hold — the partitioned-reader class, owed in `rung-10-recovery-correctness.md` §2. |
| U.3 The merged `readdir` reads `D` before its stripes over every cookie range | `stripe.readdirMerged`: `D`'s own stream is loaded first; a stripe's entry past `D`'s loaded range is not eligible until `D`'s next page; when `D` reads its next page every stripe whose loaded page reached past `D`'s old range is read again from its last consumed cookie; the order is (cookie, the stripe's copy before `D`'s) and a name already listed in its hash group is listed once (the snapshot dedupe replaces the per-entry fresh stripe scan, so the `name_key` argument is gone). | Every move out of `D` inserts into the stripe and then removes from `D`; reading `D` first makes every stripe snapshot of a range later than `D`'s, so a name `D`'s snapshot lacks is in the stripe's — the merge form of defect 13's second stripe read (0002 row 509). The restart costs one extra page read per stripe per `D` page only while `D` still holds user names (the migration's window); a migrated `D` is a page of markers, exhausted at the first read. |
| U.4 An open intent attests the steps it applied; a compensation rebases a count onto what it finds | `ops.execute`: a dispatch failing at step `i` in a ship class (the intent stays open) rewrites the record with the header's `applied` word (`intent.IntentHeaderWire.applied_le`, the former reserved u16; decode refuses `applied > step_count`) = `i`, barriered (`xv_applied_attested`; best effort — a failed rewrite is loud and leaves 0). `recoverOne` takes the attested steps as applied without re-dispatching them. `compensate` rebases every applied `set_nlink` onto the count read under the plan's guards (`post` = now, `pre` = now − delta) BEFORE writing the ABORTING image, so the live path, the roll-forward and a re-run after a crash mid-compensation all invert exactly the intent's own delta. | The roll-forward's compensation knew only fresh mints as the intent's own (§Q.19): an abandoned `link` whose name another object took kept its `+1` (C10); and a `set_nlink` re-dispatched after a later link moved the count was refused outright, compensating nothing. The op that leaves an intent open KNOWS which steps landed — it held their guards — so it says so durably; a count is a delta, so its undo is relative. Undecidable and left in the leak direction (a count too high, C10 reported, never a count too low that a later unlink would free under a live name): an initiator crash before the attestation, and the failing step itself when a later op had the same effect (the same file linked to the same name). |
| U.5 A dead member's listener leaves the manager's endpoint table at its eviction | `Manager.EndpointRow` carries the identity the publish was bound to; the home shard owner's death fan-out (`wire_plane.DeathFanout.noteDeath`) calls `retireEndpointOf(identity)` on every volume manager in the process, and every page the manager writes away from Live (`writeForeignPage`: the recovery's Recovering and Recovered, a region released Free) retires that appender's row (`manager_endpoints_retired`). `ResolveEndpoint` answers no endpoint from then; a `PublishEndpoint` under a page that is not Live refuses (as before); the same-node successor's publish under its new Live page binds anew. | The row was dropped only on an in-process leave: after a death `ResolveEndpoint` kept naming the corpse's port — a port the kernel may hand to any other process next, so a dial by it could reach a stranger. The eviction is the instant the identity stops being a member; the page write is the volume's own durable word for every manager that is not the shard's owner. |

## V. Rung-10 pack read across members and the MutualTls teardown (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-pack-read-tls-teardown.md`)

The jobs agent's two found defects (`rung-10-jobs.md` §3b, §4).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| V.1 A member's incarnation word ledgers ITS lifetimes only | The word is unstable exactly while a transition runs on this member (a claim before its publish, a W1 patch, a free from its retire to its free-list publication). `BlockAllocator.endLifetime` re-stabilizes it under the retired generation when the local lifetime ends — first in `publishFreeList` (the cell still `limbo`, the bit not yet cleared, so no claim interleaves) and in a give-back before the grant window takes the block back. A cell outside a local lifetime (`limbo`, `free`, `virgin`, `trim_windowed`) with a non-zero word never deposits into the read tiers (`fillDepositable`, consulted by the fill, the hold-serve ceremony and a victim's dehydration): such a fill serves on its `still` and the caller's binding recheck. A never-touched cell (word 0) deposits as before. The packed window read carries the binding recheck (the same mapping current after the bytes; a move re-resolves under `IDENTITY_LOOP`; an undecodable frame is EIO only when the binding stands) | A wrapped carve hands a freed block to another member whose mint this word never sees: left unstable it read as mid-write forever — every read of that member's packed tenant or striped block there answered EIO (0002 row 510). A stable word over an ended lifetime covers no bytes of this member's, and no foreign publish purges this member's tiers, so the deposit gate is what keeps a dead lifetime's residue out of them (model 7: the publish's release orders the `limbo` store before the stable bit; its weakening deposits the residue). A never-touched cell's entry outliving a FOREIGN lifetime is the token recall's purge (`RecallSink.discardLayout`/`purgeCensus`), owed by the symmetric plane's device-path arm and unchanged here |
| V.2 The MutualTls rows' signal deaths are the transport's teardown order, not the channel class | No TLS-specific change. The death the kTLS loop reproduced (1 of 60) is a LOOKUP handler fiber reading its request's name from a kmbuf region the SIGTERM teardown had unmapped (the `squeezefs umount` verb's own `.stats` read racing its stop); the fix is the transport's (the region unmapped at `Group.deinit`, after the session awaited every handler) with the joiner's beat joined before its writer plane goes — the gate agent's two commits; with them the same loop ran 60 of 60 clean | 120 loop runs met no kTLS-socket, KeyUpdate/close_notify, supervisor or SIGUSR2 class; plaintext rows meet the same death (the gate agent's loops on branch `gatefix`, `rung-09-gates.md` §10: 1 of 60 mount/umount cycles, 1 of 40 KVDA loops) |

## W. Rung-10 concurrency defects (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-concurrency-defects.md`)

The races the pack-read agent stated for their owners (`rung-10-pack-read-tls-teardown.md` "Found on the way") and the gate's two flaky rows.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| W.1 A free-list publication moves the state, then the bit, then the budget | `publishFreeList`, `returnFromTrim` and `holderReturnBit` store the cell's state FIRST (`free` below the cursor, `virgin` at/above), then clear the bit — the clear's acq_rel is the release a claimant's acq_rel take (`tryTakeBit`) acquires, so the claimant's own state store lands after ours — then credit the budget (bit before budget: a claim that won the credit finds a clear bit). On the holder the bit and the credit move in ONE critical section under its ordering lock (`holderPublishFree`, the sink's half), the lock every carve takes: the carve is the one consumer that takes its bit before its debit, and its credit is always there. Every other consumer — the free-list claims, `claimFreeForTrim`, `quarantineBlock`'s free arm, the fsck free-list eviction — wins its entitlement BEFORE its bit and refunds a lost take. A quarantine's lost take (a claim won the block, or a publication sits between its state and its bit) is the flag alone, as the limbo arm | The shipped order (bit, then state, then credit) let a carve + mint between the holder's bit and its state end at `free` on the minted block with a phantom credit, and a quarantine of that live block assert on its lost take; a bit-first consumer between a publication's bit and its credit debited a credit that had not landed (the debit's underflow assert). Model 1 (`free_list_model.zig`, the local list) and model 2 (the holder) hold; `bit_first`, a state store buffered past the clear, a bit-first trim and a credit outside the holder's lock each fail. No double hand-out was reachable: the bit stays the one arbiter of a take; the defect was the cell's word and the budget lying about it |
| W.2 The token recall purges the reader's tiers before it acks | `token_cache.Reader` keeps every revoked entry pinned through its purge (the inline ack, the drain lane's deferred ack, a voluntary release or eviction, a stop, a re-point; a revoke that cannot allocate purges over no entries — the census) and hands it to `RecallSink.discardLayout`. `wire_plane.RecallSink`, bound to the data plane at mount (the wire and the `-o ro` reader plane), purges every block key the entry's carried `layout` names through the ONE purge (`DataPlane.recallPurgeLayout` → `ReadPath.purgeRecalledLayout`); no entry, a block-map tree head or an undecodable value is `no_entry` → the census once per recall (`ReadPath.purgeForeignCensus` over `purge.purgeCensus`: a structural, allocation-free census face per tier, every picked key through `purgeBlockKey`), which drops FOREIGN keys only (a cell this member never touched).
