In the read path a binding loss after a fetch that may have deposited a foreign key purges it (`loseForeign`; a speculative fill rechecks a foreign deposit the same way), and the deposit-only paths (a victim's dehydration, the hold-serve ceremony) never deposit a foreign key | The recall IS the qualification (spec §1.7; design :651): the owner frees a recalled object's blocks only after the ack, so the ack must follow the purge. A foreign block's lifetime is invisible to this member's allocator (word 0 whatever the owner does), so the token is its only witness: a fill whose DMA lands before the purge and whose deposit lands after it is caught by the recheck after its deposit — that recheck's resolve misses the revoked token and refetches (a re-grant before the owner's commit is either parked or recalled again, `grant_gate_model`). The reference's census dropped every key; a local lifetime's keys need no recall (0002 row 513) |
| W.3 A listener's last connection reads nothing of it after the decrement `stop` waits for | `Listener.retire` reads the station and the live word's address before its `fetchSub`; the gauge takes the decrement's own result; the futex wake names the word by its address (a private futex is keyed by the address — the kernel reads no memory there) | The decrement is what lets `stop` see 0 and free the listener; the old `self.live.load()` for the gauge read the freed listener (0x55555555 under the testing allocator) — plaintext and TLS connections alike |
| W.4 The idle stack trim claims a free slot in place; a recycle drops `live` after its push | One claim word per slot outside the `Fiber` (`Lane.claims`: FREE / ARMED / TRIMMING). The trim claims FREE → TRIMMING in place and releases after the madvise, never popping the slot out of the free ring; `spawn` claims the slot it popped (FREE → ARMED) and waits out a trim (`exec_spawn_trim_waits`), never refusing; `recycle` releases the claim after the reset, pushes, and decrements `live` last | The popping trimmer's push in flight hid every slot queued behind it from spawn (Vyukov's head cell answers empty): spawns refused with free slots, and the trimmer-race row's early return then freed the lane under its trimmer (121 of 300 loops under load, 118 SIGABRT — the gate's). `live` dropped before the push made `live == 0` a false quiescence barrier. `slot_pool_model.zig` holds; `trimmer_pops`, `decrement_before_push`, `free_before_switch_out` and `spawn_skips_claim` each fail |

## X. The slot-move retry law (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-handover-retry.md`)

The last release-gate blocker: an op shipped across the wire, or one step of a cross-owner change, that met a slot WHILE it moved got a retryable refusal, and the refusal reached the user — B's `chmod` EAGAIN in the handover row; B's `printf > xN` EEXIST / EAGAIN and a `[C9]` orphan in the online-fsck row; `scrub --online` `HolderUnreachable, AGAIN`. Charter §1.1/§1.8: a RETRYABLE refusal is the system's to retry; only terminal and refusal classes reach the application.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| X.1 A move class is the system's to retry, bounded by the fail-stop lattice | The served `Filesystem` (FUSE and the IPC host) is wrapped by `move_gate.MoveGate`: an op whose attempt answers a move class (`errors.isMoveClass` — the wire's typed retryable answers: a slot moved or moving, a holder not yet resolved or unreachable, a verb deferred) samples the mount's slot-move epoch BEFORE the attempt, parks on it, and runs again from the top with every home re-resolved. The epoch (`slots/moves.zig`) is bumped by every lease-table and resolver transition (`LeaseTable.noteMoved`); a park registers before it re-checks (modelled: `move_epoch_model`, the weakenings `recheck_before_register` and `observe_after_attempt` fail); a REMOTE move wakes nothing local, so the park re-polls, doubling from `REPOLL_MIN_NS` = 1 ms (one device barrier — a move barriers its release and its grant) to `REPOLL_MAX_NS` = 40 ms (twice the measured upper handover, `acquire.HANDOVER_BOUND_FLOOR_NS`). Past `T_park_max` (`park_reclaim.tParkMaxMs` at the membership's `T_owner`; the wire's `moveDeadlineMs`) the op answers the TERMINAL `HolderUnresolved` (EIO), loud; the mount's close ends every park with the same answer. Counted: `slot_move_reissues`, `_parks`, `_park_wakes`, `_repolls`, `_unresolved`, the `slot_move_ns` phase; a move class reaching a FUSE reply is the `fuse_move_class_at_boundary` tripwire | EAGAIN is reserved for `O_NONBLOCK` (POSIX-5); a shell's `chmod` or `printf >` neither retries nor should. The holder that never resolves is the lattice's to declare: the same bound the joiner's park gate and the reclaim use, so no op waits past the point the membership itself gives a holder up, and none spins unbounded |
| X.2 A re-issued mutation is idempotent across the move by its intent's id | An op whose cross-owner intent a move left OPEN keeps it (`ops.executeHeld` → `HeldOpen` on the op's `Ctx.move`); the gate's re-issue resumes THAT intent (`resumeHeld`) and answers the first attempt's result: a create its child's ino (`ownCreate`; a name found landed whose ino is the op's own subject is the op's, never EEXIST), a remove / rename / link / write postlude their landed state. An op ending otherwise (another error, the deadline) abandons the intent to the roll-forward cadence, which completes or compensates it (§Q.19, the `applied` attestation of §U.4). Counted: `xv_held_open`, `xv_resumed`, `_completed`, `_gone` | The shell retried the SAME name and met its own first attempt (EEXIST) while that attempt's inode, its dentry never landed, stayed live and named by nothing (`[C9]`). The intent's id is the op id the protocol already carries end to end; a second id would be a second truth |
| X.3 In a handover's release → grant gap the slot is its accepter's at the door too | `Door.tryEnter` on an unleased slot whose stand reserves it for ANOTHER member refuses naming the accepter (the move class; the caller re-resolves to it) instead of sending the committer to a first touch the reservation refuses | §Q.11 made the stand route to the accepter in the resolver; the door still answered `first_touch`, and the committer looped on refused first touches until the grant |
| X.4 A joiner's own grant in flight is settled, never re-asked forever | An AcquireSlot from the slot's own holder (its accept landed at the manager, its install not yet) answers the grant's words as REPLAYED items after the fresh grants (the same `AcquireReply` items — no codec change); a joiner whose resolver names itself before its table does settles the grant through that replay (`Plane.settleOwnGrant`, `slot_own_grants_settled`); the carriage's accept and the replay install idempotently at the held generation, serialized (`slot_install_waits`) | The carriage's accept and the door's first touch race on every handover to a joiner; the replay answered `already` with no words and the op re-asked until the accept's install, or forever when its reply was lost |
| X.5 A wire grant installs its tree before its word; a moved slot's cached nodes are forgotten | `installWireGrant` puts the transferred root in place FIRST and loads the table word LAST (the word is what the resolver, the door and the applier act on); a commit mints a missing tree only for a slot this mount may write (`mayMintAtCommit`); a tree at another root than the grant's is the `slot_install_shadow_trees` tripwire, replaced. Every lease move across processes — the manager's adoption of a released slot, its grant away, a joiner's install and its release — drops the slot's node mappings from the node cache (`slot_move_nodes_discarded`) | The word first let the accepter read its new slot EMPTY (a create's parent `dying`, EEXIST / ENOENT) and a commit in the gap minted an empty tree that shadowed every record the holder wrote. A wire lessee appends frames into its leaves IN PLACE under an unchanged root pointer, so the manager re-adopting the released slot read its PRE-grant leaf images: every name B created there was gone after B's unmount (`[C9]`) |
| X.6 The online scope's window scan reads inode keys only | `foreignWindowInos` takes `census.globalOfOwnedKeyIno`: a slot's reserved locals below the mint base (an intent's home is local 0 of its slot's guest namespace) name no inode and are skipped | `globalOfKeyIno` asserted on an intent home from a member's ring window and killed the coordinator; the admin lane's `scrub --online` then answered `HolderUnreachable, AGAIN` — a dead daemon, not a move |
| X.7 A namespace op whose every object is ANOTHER holder's is one intent | `kv_fs.needsIntent`: create / remove / rename / link take the cross-owner arm when the objects span homes OR the first is foreign; the local arm's door refusal + one redispatch stays for the race it was built for (the slot ours at the plan, another's at the door) | A rename inside a foreign directory ran as a local commit and revalidated its source against this mount's view of a slot it does not hold: ENOENT (the move matrix, red at the offer and drain steps) |
| X.8 The plan's guards re-resolve a dead or stale view of a holder | `ops.acquirePlanGuards`: an unreachable or stale holder re-resolves every foreign slot of the plan's keys at the manager and asks again, ≤ `SLOT_MOVED_RETRIES`; a refusal or a lost ask at the holder the view names is asked again only where the re-resolve names another holder; counted `xv_cross_owner_guard_stale_reresolves` | §Q.16 gave a step's SHIP the re-resolve and left the guards without it. Under X.1 the re-issue re-took its guards at the killed holder for the whole lattice deadline: C's create into a stripe of the dead B parked 48 s, answered EIO, and C created 5 files across the death where it had created thousands (the shared hot directory row, the first full gate on this law) |

## Y. Rung-10 syncfs, the quarantine's release and timing, the holder's bit-takes, the tick commit ledger, the nvmet port (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-syncfs-quarantine.md`)

The items `rung-10-concurrency-defects.md` "Found on the way" and `rung-09-gates.md` §11 left open.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Y.1 `sync -f` is a durability point for the daemon's write custody | The INIT reply echoes `FUSE_HAS_SYNCFS` (uapi 7.47, flags2 bit 12) iff the kernel offered it; FUSE_SYNCFS (opcode 50) is the per-file fsync ladder for the WHOLE mount (`KvFs.syncfs` → `DataPlane.syncfs` → `fsync.syncfs`): every parked stamp journaled; a census of every ino holding write custody when it runs (the accumulation table's parked keys — by key, no per-ino page bound —, the device overlay's open records, the rewrite epochs open or saving); each overlay drained with its barrier and each parked block uploaded durably (the ENOSPC ladder once); the staged promote by its lever; ONE data barrier over every volatile namespace (the stripe words snapshotted before it clear after it); every census ino's epoch saved; then the store-wide meta barrier (every member's coalesced barrier, the strict cadence's per commit). `fuse_syncfs` counts it, `fuse_syncfs_phase_ns` is its exact-sum family (`times_drain / data_flush / staged_promote / staged_sync / data_barrier / meta_publish / meta_barrier / total` — `staged_sync` the whole-ring sync of the staged residents, charter §1 item 10 as amended 2026-10-19), `fuse_syncfs_armed` (0/1) says whether the kernel forwards syncfs at all. The venue's 7.2.6-sqz carries the upstream patch (`scripts/kernel/patches-7.2/0027`, `scripts/kernel/build-venue-kernel` reproduces the venue kernel from the reference recipe plus this repo's deltas) | A plain `/dev/fuse` mount never sees syncfs on 7.2.6 (`fuse_sync_fs` returns 0 unless `fc->sync_fs`, which only virtiofs and fuseblk set); upstream gates the opt-in on the opener's CAP_SYS_ADMIN in the initial user namespace because a server can stall `sync` — the daemon runs as root and is that server. Custody born after the census is a write the syncfs did not see begin (POSIX syncfs covers what was written before the call); the census reserves its room outside every spin lock. 0002 row 517 |
| Y.2 The jobs sink returns a released cohort to the free list | `AllocSink` (coordinator) delegates to `custody.DestinationCohort`: `admit` quarantines the destination and, a minted one (a pre-allocated unpublished destination is a live mint), relinquishes it — its terminal free runs and the quarantine defers the publish (OWED); the cohort keeps the live admissions the allocator cannot (a flagged live cell carries no epoch). `release` under `DrainProof.preemptLanded(n)` names them to every volume's `releaseQuarantine` and `custody.releaseCohort` finishes every owed publish, draining `out` in passes (a full pass leaves the rest in the cohort). One cohort is in flight: the lease table admits, preempts and releases under its one lock; a cohort no preempt released stays quarantined when the next begins | The released blocks were owed a free nobody ran: `out` was dropped and the live admissions never named — the destinations stayed allocated and flagged until fsck (0002 row 515). Relinquishing at the admission keeps the do-not-reallocate law (the free is deferred while flagged) and gives the release something to finish |
| Y.3 A quarantine admitted after a finish passed its gate is taken back | The gate and the admission are a Dekker pair: the publication stores the state (seq_cst), clears the bit, credits, then RE-CHECKS the flag (seq_cst); the admission sets the flag (seq_cst RMW), then reads the state (seq_cst) — one side sees the other. A re-check that finds the flag takes the block back out of the list (the entitlement, then the bit — a claim or the admission's own free arm that won it owns the block) and leaves it `limbo` OWED. Every OWED mark (the gate's, the take-back's) is a CAS that REQUIRES the flag, against the release's one `fetchAnd` of both; a take-back that finds the cohort already released publishes the block again. The re-check sits in `publishFreeList`, so the grace ring's late publication is covered too (`block_quarantine_takebacks`) | The gate ran before the publication: an admission between them read `limbo`, took the flag alone, and the block reached the free list flagged — reallocatable under a dead epoch, the flag outliving the cohort. `free_list_model.zig` model 3 holds (with and without the release); `no_recheck`, `recheck_before_state`, `owe_unconditional` and `gate_two_steps` each fail |
| Y.4 Every non-carve take on an armed holder runs under its ordering lock | `FinishFreeSink` carries the holder's `takeOutOfList` and `returnTrim` (`DataBitmap`): the trim claim, the quarantine's free and virgin arms and the re-check's take-back run their whole take (the state, the entitlement, the bit) inside the lock the carve takes; a quarantine's take (and a take-back) moves the population and queues a SET delta; the trim's return clears its bit and credits inside it. Unarmed, the same body runs in place (`holderTakeOutOfList`, `holderReturnTrim`) | The carve scans the lowest clear run and asserts every take of it under the lock; a take outside it between the scan and the take tripped the assert, an entitlement won outside it ahead of the carve's debit underflowed the budget, a trim return outside it showed the carve a clear bit before its credit, and a quarantine's take moved no population (the drift check's tripwire). `free_list_model.zig` model 4 holds; `take_outside_lock` (the carve's assert) and `return_outside_lock` (an underflow) fail |
| Y.5 The tick's commit ledger splits into a lost-wake tripwire and a late counter | Every arm-winning `Wake.wake` counts its eventfd write after the write returns (`Parked.written`, seq_cst); the lane adds each reaped READ's count (`consumed`). Before a park the worker reads `wakeOwed` (written > consumed, the READ standing); a backstop tick on a `DEFER_TASKRUN` ring whose park was owed fires `transport_park_tick_commit_lost_wakes`; the pass after a tick counts what its pump found with no wake CQE as `transport_park_tick_late_commits` unless the tick was classed lost. The 60 s attribution WARN goes with the old counter (the tripwire logs; a late arrival is no fault) | Owed means the write's count is in the eventfd — the standing READ completes inside the wait — or in a READ CQE not yet reaped (the CQ is not empty): the wait cannot return -ETIME, so the read is exact; a message the pump finds otherwise was published after the park began (between the post-tick reap and the pump, or a producer between its push and its write) — its wake in flight. A forced write is uncounted, which only lowers the verdict (never a false owed). 0002 row 516 |
| Y.6 Every nvmet fidelity tcp listener takes a TCP port of its run's own | `Rig.tcpPort`: `bind(127.0.0.1:0)` + `getsockname` — the kernel's ephemeral choice — the probe socket held bound (SO_REUSEADDR, never listening) until the rig ends, so no concurrent run's `bind(0)` is handed it, and nvmet-tcp's listener binds beside it (`nvmet_tcp_add_port` sets SO_REUSEADDR; only a LISTENING holder conflicts). The legs' fixed ports (4433, 4434, 4440, 4450, 4460, 4461, 4470, 4480, 4490) are gone; the adopt row's hand-built port id is the run's own (`smallPortId`: pid-keyed below both ranges, the first id no port holds). The port-id slice stays `SQUEEZEFS_NVMET_PORT_ID_BASE=54000`: `allocatePortId` probes past another run's ports. The host-global censuses judge only the run's own objects (`Rig.ownsNqn`): the daemon-connect leg's `/sys/class/nvme` census, the loop port's last-out (the host's ONE loop port carries every run's loop shares), `foreign_live` read by name, the adopt row's configfs-untouched check on its own share and port; the hand-built namespace's uuid carries the pid (the host's nvme core ignores a namespace whose uuid another subsystem already shows) | Two gate runs on one venue (several worktrees) bound the same 127.0.0.1 service ports: the second run's port link was refused (`PortLinkRefused` — nvmet-tcp's bind answered EADDRINUSE) and its failed shares left their subsystems behind. The port-id base was never the collision — the TCP service ports were (rung-09 §11 named them by the wrong number); with them derived, the next interference was the legs' host-wide censuses (another run's controllers, loop links, foreign shares and a duplicate namespace uuid), each scoped to the run |

## Z. A frozen daemon never hangs the host's `sync` (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-sync-posture.md`)

§Y.1 made every host-wide `sync(2)` a FUSE_SYNCFS to every squeezefs daemon on the host. The definitive gate then hung 23 min in the nvmet fidelity row `sigstop-preempt`: its helper on mount B ran a global `sync` while daemon A was SIGSTOPped, and the FUSE_SYNCFS to A's mount waited uninterruptibly (`request_wait_answer` once FR_PENDING is cleared — `kill -9` of the waiter frees nothing). Charter §1.1/§1.8: a daemon that cannot answer is fail-stopped by the lattice; nothing outside its mount may wait on it forever.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Z.1 The INIT reply carries a request timeout derived from the fail-stop lattice | `request_timeout = T_owner + T_park_max` in whole seconds rounded up (`geometry.requestTimeoutS`): 135 s at the constants (45 s + 45 s failover + 45 s grace). A wire member or an `-o ro` reader takes its membership's `T_owner` and the move gate's `T_park_max`; a `--single-writer` mount (no park) the same bound at the constant `T_owner`. Sent with FUSE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT (flags2 bit 10) iff the kernel offers it; the kernel checks every 15 s, so a request is aborted between T and T + 15 s of its creation. The abort ends the connection: every caller ENOTCONN, the mount stays in the table (the failure visible). The daemon judges "the connection ended while our mount id is still attached" (an unmount removes the entry before the superblock ends the connection) as the abort, logs it as an error, tears down in order and exits 1. `transport_request_timeout_s` is the timeout the kernel applies (`/proc/sys/fs/fuse/max_request_timeout` caps it — a cap below T is a loud warning at mount; 15 s floors it). Seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_FUSE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S` [15, 65535] | The longest a HEALTHY daemon holds a request is a wait on a peer the lattice is giving up: ≤ `T_owner` for the set to declare it dead (a member's own park starts at `T_self < T_owner`), then the park, ≤ `T_park_max`, past which the op answers the terminal EIO (§X.1, `park_reclaim`). Every other bounded wait a handler can sit in is shorter: the journal's FAILED latch (3 × `PARK_ESCALATION_NS` = 90 s), the coherence hold (20 s), a token recall (≤ one lease TTL), the zc bridges (30 s), the syncfs drain (parked custody ≤ budget / 16 — seconds at device rates). A request older than T waits on nothing the lattice sanctions: the daemon is stopped, wedged, or its substrate stalled past every deadline. This is the supervisor's abort rung (charter §4) done by the kernel — armed with or without `--supervise`, and still armed when a SIGSTOP freezes the supervisor with the daemon. It is not a per-op timeout wrapper (charter §4 bans those: dropping a handler mid-commit wedges the ring): no handler is dropped, the whole connection fail-stops and the daemon's teardown runs in order |
| Z.2 The price: a substrate that stalls a request past T fail-stops the mount | A data device whose I/O holds a request longer than T (nvme-tcp's own give-up is `ctrl_loss_tmo`, 600 s) ends the mount ENOTCONN instead of resuming when the device returns; a remount recovers | Device I/O is "watchdog only" (no per-op timeout, decision 0002 row 34); unbounded, a stalled device held every caller AND every host-wide sync in an uninterruptible wait. A device that holds one request past the lattice's whole deadline has held an application's I/O for over two minutes; the metadata side (the claim heartbeat) is already fenced at `T_owner` |
| Z.3 FUSE_SYNCFS only beside the timeout | `FUSE_HAS_SYNCFS` is echoed only when FUSE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT rides with it; a session that asks for no timeout (the transport tests' EchoFs) declines syncfs; a kernel that does not take the timeout gets a loud warning and no syncfs | Opting into syncfs makes every host-wide sync wait on this daemon (upstream gates the opt-in on CAP_SYS_ADMIN because "a server can stall sync"). Unbounded, the opt-in is a host-wide hazard; bounded, it is the durability point §Y.1 needs |
| Z.4 The venue kernel's abort ends requests a stopped server never claimed | `scripts/kernel/patches-7.2/0028`: the uring dispatch marks an entry `tw_pending` under the queue lock; the task work claims its request under the lock; the abort ends every still-pending entry's request (-ECONNABORTED, the queue's background charge credited) and leaves the entry without one, which the task hands to the teardown (cmd done -ENOTCONN) when it finally runs — a dying task frees it as before; the stuck-entry dump is logged once (`stop_debug_log` was set and never read) | Measured on 7.2.6-sqz before it (request timeout 15 s, the daemon SIGSTOPped, a global `sync`): the timeout's abort fired, yet the FUSE_SYNCFS sat in `ent_w_req_queue` (FRRS_FUSE_REQ) waiting for the stopped ring thread's task work (DEFER_TASKRUN runs it only at the owner's next `io_uring_enter`) — neither the abort nor the entry teardown reaches that list. The sync stayed in D state until SIGCONT and dmesg logged the entry every 50 ms. Substrate law (owner 2026-10-12): fix the substrate, never a userspace fallback |
| Z.5 A rig syncs only what it wrote | Every smoke / nvmet fidelity / suites / bracket / venue script syncs its own mount or file (`sync -f .`, `sync -f <set dir>`, `sync -f <results>`, `sync -f /build`); the rails' rig sync rail fails on a host-wide `sync` in any rig script (every string literal in `src/` and `tests/`, every shell script under `scripts/`). The frozen-daemon smoke row's spawned `sync` is the one host-wide sync: the claim under test | A host-wide sync waits on every FUSE mount on the host — another row's or another run's frozen daemon included — for that daemon's whole timeout, an older binary's forever. The sigstop-preempt helper did exactly that to the A it had frozen |
| Z.6 Owner ruling (2026-10-13): keep both kernel deltas | The venue/box kernel carries 0027 (`FUSE_HAS_SYNCFS`, a backport of the patch queued in fuse for-next) and 0028 (the uring abort ends requests a stopped server never claimed) on top of the 26-patch sqz series the Rust reference already required. Rejected: syncfs opt-in behind a knob, default off, to run on the unmodified series | With them `sync`/`syncfs` is a durability point for the daemon's write custody and a frozen daemon is fenced by the kernel; without them `sync -f` returns success over data no device holds (measured: kill -9 after `sync -f` lost the file — the Rust reference has the same gap). 0028 fixes a latent kernel bug any FUSE-over-io_uring server meets when its connection is aborted while it is stopped or blocked: owed upstream |

## AA. The dev container's performance gap — the bounds and the fixes (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-dev-perf.md`)

The owner's dev container (4 CPUs, a 2-MDT + 2-OST set on raw virtio-blk partitions, the venue
kernel) measured SqueezeFS at 3.2 k creates/s against ext4's 21.8 k and 16.7 k random 4 KiB O_DIRECT
reads/s against 291 k on the same disks. The performance ruling (§J's preamble) makes each gap a
defect; the evidence note decomposes every row before fixing it. Symbols: `R` the bare FUSE round
trip on this kernel (a negative LOOKUP: 13–14 µs send → end, 5.3 µs of it the handler), `W` one
journal entry's write landing (≈ 38 µs on the MDT partition), `D` one 4 KiB device read (49.8 µs at
QD1).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AA.1 A child of a directory this mount leases outside the native slot mints on the directory's member | `MetaSet.allocateIno` asks `Plane.homesChildren(parent_slot)` FIRST: the parent outside the member's native slot and, on an armed member, leased by this mount ⇒ the parent's member (then §5.5's affinity under `A_max`, else that member's rotor); else §5.4's round-robin over the healthy members. Tie test: `meta_set` "a child of a directory this mount leases outside the native slot mints on the directory's member" (0 intents over 100 creates); harness closure: `Δxv_cross_owner_intents_minted = 0` over a create phase in a directory the mount created | The port (like the reference: `pick_mint_volume` before `lease_mint_slot`, "no affinity across volumes") chose the member first and granted §5.5's parent affinity only to a parent on that member, so on a 2-member set half of every directory's children landed on the other member and every create, mkdir, unlink and rename of them was a cross-owner intent — two device barriers each (`intent_barrier` 191 µs + `retire` 166 µs of a 424 µs intent; daemon create mean 266 µs). The root's children (the native slot) still round-robin, so a set spreads by subtree; a foreign directory's child still mints in the creator's rotor (§5.5). Charter §5.4's mint-spread bullet amended; 0002 row 518 |
| AA.2 The create bound | A create in a directory this mount leases is ONE single-member entry acked at its write landing (coalesced mode; strict adds the barrier). The kernel runs a create's negative `LOOKUP` and its `CREATE` both under the directory's `i_rwsem` (`atomic_open`), so a shared directory's create rate is `1 / (LOOKUP + CREATE round trips) ≤ 1 / (2R + W + C_sw)`: on this venue `2R + W ≈ 46 µs` ⇒ ≤ 21 k creates/s in one directory. The measured remainder `C_sw` — three cross-thread handoff pairs (worker ↔ handler lane, lane → journal lane, journal lane → lane: ≈ 52 µs of a QD1 create's 90) and the handler's CPU — is the stated open gap, never an accepted floor (the evidence note's §6 names the levers) | Charter §5.2: "Acks only after the entry landed" — the device write is owed; nothing else is |
| AA.3 The random 4 KiB O_DIRECT read bound | A cold aligned single-block read on a zero-copy session is the direct device leg: ONE device read into the app's pages + ONE FUSE round trip, zero daemon copies (`Δtransport_zc_direct_legs ≡ ΔREADs`, `read_copy_*` 0). Its resolution: the file ONCE and the binding TWICE — before the DMA and after the bytes (charter §6.3) — tie test `warm_ladder` "a cold direct-leg read resolves its file ONCE and its binding TWICE"; harness closure: `Δmeta_kv_node_cache_hits ÷ Δtransport_zc_direct_legs = 3` (the inode; ONE layout read for the file facts and the binding, `read_path.Meta.file_at`; the layout for the post-DMA recheck — was 7). Throughput ≤ `min(devices × device QD capacity, queues used ÷ worker CPU per read)`: one fio thread drives ONE queue (the kernel queues by submitting CPU), so the drain worker's CPU per read is the single-job ceiling (17.2 → 5.5 µs measured) | The leg re-read what the probe had just resolved, and every layout read was a chain RANGE with copies — 72 % of the worker's cycles |
| AA.4 The zero-copy verdict is the ring the reply will create | The daemon writes `fuse.enable_uring=Y` BEFORE the probes; the verdict is `probes.ringAtReply(offer, the parameter after the write)`; tie test `probes` "the ring exists at reply time iff enable_uring reads Y after the daemon's write" | The kernel offers `FUSE_OVER_IO_URING` only when the parameter ALREADY reads Y — never on the first mount after a boot (default N) — while that mount arms its ring all the same (7.2.4+: created at reply time): every first mount silently ran `buf_ring` (rand-4k 17.4 k against 58 k on zero-copy) |
| AA.5 The process allocator | `mem/process_allocator.select`: every release build takes `std.heap.smp_allocator`; Debug keeps std's SafeAllocator; tie test `process_allocator` "a release product never runs on std's SafeAllocator" | Zig 0.17 gives ReleaseSafe the debugging SafeAllocator (`start.zig` `use_safe_allocator`); ReleaseSafe is the product's build — 38 % of the FUSE worker's cycles on rand-4k, every allocation of every op |
| AA.6 The kernel's entry and attr caches stay at the charter's 1 s | Not raised here. A stat of a dentry younger than 1 s costs no request (a cached mdtest stat phase runs at the kernel's rate); an expired one costs one LOOKUP | The TTL is the staleness bound for the remote namespace changes no recall names: a reader's kernel learns of a peer's create (a cached NEGATIVE dentry) or rename only through it — the recall invalidates and prunes an object's inode, never a `(parent, name)`. Raising it needs entry-grained invalidation (`FUSE_NOTIFY_INVAL_ENTRY` from the served namespace steps and the dentry-set recall) — the owed lever, stated in the evidence note with its measured price |

## AB. The four levers §AA left open (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-dev-perf.md` §9)

The owner approved the four levers the dev-perf pass stated with their measured price (§AA, the
evidence note's §6), in order: the metadata warm ladder (AB.1), entry-grained invalidation and the
TTL it lets rise (AB.2), the drain worker as the handler lane (AB.3), the committer's bounded ack
spin (AB.4). Each lands with a red row first and a same-mount A-B-B-A (`scripts/perf/abba.sh`, the
dist binary, ONE freshly formatted 2-MDT + 2-OST set per leg); the hard laws do not move (token
coherence — AB.2 makes it stricter; the crash-consistency kill rows; the durability modes;
latch-free zero-copy hot paths; no per-op allocation). Symbols as §AA: `R` the bare FUSE round
trip, `W` one journal entry's write landing; here also `w` the committer's wake (the journal lane
writes the committer lane's eventfd, the lane is scheduled, the fiber resumes).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AB.1 LOOKUP and GETATTR answered on the drain worker when the answer is in RAM | The transport's `probe_meta` (beside `probe_read`): the worker asks the filesystem's try-only `probe_lookup` / `probe_getattr` under `node_cache.try_only` — a node the cache does not hold (`KvTree.fetch` answers `WouldBlock` instead of a demand load), a foreign slot (the token serve is a round trip), a striped parent (the stripe fold is a fan-out), a virtual or dot name ⇒ `WouldBlock` and the request DEMOTES to the handler lane unchanged; an answer is committed from the worker's own reply slot (`commitReady(.local)`), the lookup reference noted exactly as the lane notes it (`Dispatcher.noteEntry`). Partition: `transport_meta_inline_serves + transport_meta_inline_demotes ≡ LOOKUP + GETATTR` reaching the worker. Red rows: `session` e2e "a LOOKUP and a GETATTR the filesystem answers from RAM never leave the worker" (handler-lane handoffs 0, inline serves 2); `kv_fs` "the drain worker's try-only LOOKUP / GETATTR answers exactly what the lane answers"; `smo` "try-only reads" (a cold node is `WouldBlock`, never a load) | The handoff pair (worker → lane → worker's ring) was 22 µs of a 27 µs QD1 LOOKUP against a 5 µs handler (§AA's decomposition); LOOKUP and GETATTR are the most frequent requests of every metadata workload and the READ law's arm (a) already answers reads the same way. The try-only rule keeps the worker non-blocking: it never waits on a device, a peer or a lock a pass holds |
| AB.2 Entry-grained invalidation, and the TTL it lets rise | (1) **The names travel with the change.** Every namespace op notes the `(parent, name)`s it changes on its tx (`KvTx.noteName`: `economy.stageDentryPut / stageUnlink / stageRename`, the cross-owner applier's insert/remove steps; ≤ 2, deduplicated; a third or an arena refusal is `overflow`). The holder's A1½ recall carries them (`RecallFrameHead.n_names` + `RecallNameHead` records after the objects; `unnamed` = a member changed a dentry its notes do not name), each reader told only the names under the objects it held; the reader's service writes `FUSE_NOTIFY_INVAL_ENTRY` for each BEFORE it acks (`Divert.noteRecallNames` → `KernelInvalidator.noteName`; `unnamed` ⇒ `FUSE_NOTIFY_INC_EPOCH`), beside the object's `INVAL_INODE` + `PRUNE` as before. (2) **This mount's own kernel** is invalidated for every change it did not ask for — a peer's served step or ship, a job, a tool (`!kernel_origin`; `kv_fs.commit` marks a request the move gate carries), unless the change's own channel tells the kernel (`kernel_told`, 2026-10-24: the IPC host's handoffs — a ring write announces its size awaited before its ack, and the notifier's late duplicate, landing inside a GETATTR the kernel sent after that ack, made it discard the fresh reply and read against its stale size; `rung-10-interception-host-load.md`) — and for every member of a window that FAILED (its records were readable in RAM until the rollback): `conveyor.KernelView` at the window's terminal outcome, after the RAM apply and before the member's ack; the journal lane only QUEUES (names, objects) and the mount's notifier thread writes them. (3) **Never dropped.** An `INVAL_ENTRY` that finds no sideband cell owes an `INC_EPOCH` the loop writes from a reserved cell; attrs-only `INVAL_INODE` (non-blocking in the kernel) waits for its own reserved cells (`SIDEBAND_NB_CELLS` = 8) on the notifier; a full notifier queue owes a whole-view flush (`INC_EPOCH` + `INVAL_INODE` of every ino the kernel holds, `OpenTable.trackedInos`). (4) **The TTL law** (`mount_options.TtlLaw`): an ISOLATED mount — `--single-writer`, or a manager no member has joined, and no interception host — derives every class no knob or `-o` names to `T_owner` (45 s); a PEERED mount (a joiner, a reader, an interception mount, a manager after its first join) keeps the charter's classes (1 s). The first join flips the law (`Manager.peer_gate`, before `joinAppender`) and the join is answered after the whole-view flush is written. Red rows: `tokens` "invalidation coherence" (fails with the reader's name pass removed), `conveyor` "the kernel view", `mount_options` "the TTL law", `open_table` "the tracked inos", the wire round trip + screen rows, and the smoke "INVALIDATION COHERENCE ACROSS DAEMONS" (every class at 60 s on both daemons: a served create, a recalled create, unlink, rename and chmod are each seen at once — red without the lever at its first step) | **Why the TTL may rise, and only there.** The kernel's caches go stale only through a change the kernel did not make. On a live path every such change now reaches the kernel by name or object before its ack (a reader's before the recall is acked, so before the holder's change lands; this mount's queued before the ack and written by the notifier within its round), and the kernel's own ordering closes the races: a lookup in flight holds its parent's `i_rwsem`, so `INVAL_ENTRY` (which takes it) lands after the dentry is installed; `INVAL_INODE` bumps `attr_version`, so an in-flight GETATTR's older attributes are discarded; `INC_EPOCH` invalidates every dentry whose lookup began before it. On an isolated mount nothing else can change what the kernel holds, so the TTL bounds no live path — only a bug; it derives to `T_owner`, the longest any coherence protocol of the set waits without an answer (the token recall's deadline), so no kernel view outlives the protocols' own window. With a peer the failure windows remain (a reader whose recall cannot reach it, a member evicted while its daemon lives): there the charter's 1 s stays the bound, and the names make every live path stricter than it was (staleness = the notify's propagation, was ≤ 1 s). An interception mount keeps 1 s: its coherence limiter's window IS the attr TTL (`ipc/host/inval.zig`). Measured on the dev container: see the evidence note §9.3 |
| AB.3 The drain worker drives a handler lane of its own; a namespace request runs on the thread that delivered it | A DRIVEN `exec.Lane` per worker (`Options.driven`: no thread; its 64 B `ParkedRing` and coalescer unchanged), attached on the worker's thread (a ring without `DEFER_TASKRUN`, `IORING_REGISTER_EVENTFD` on the lane's wake eventfd); the worker's SQE128 ring holds a standing READ of that eventfd beside its own, so ONE wait covers a foreign enqueue and the lane ring's completions, the lane's timer and backstop folded into the bound. The pass pumps the lane after the commit cells and bridge pumps (step 4a: disarm → inbox → timers → the fibers ready at entry) and gates what they published in the same pass (4a'); a publication from the worker's thread elides the eventfd write (`self_kick`); a runnable fiber or an elided wake is work in hand. Dispatch law (`worker.onWorkerLane`): the namespace and attribute class (LOOKUP/GETATTR demotes, MKNOD/MKDIR/SYMLINK/LINK/CREATE, UNLINK/RMDIR/RENAME, OPEN and CREATE without O_TRUNC, OPENDIR/READDIR(PLUS)/RELEASEDIR, the xattrs, ACCESS, STATFS, FLUSH, a SETATTR without a size) runs on the worker's lane; the DATA class (READ, WRITE, the syncs, RELEASE, a truncate, FALLOCATE, COPY_FILE_RANGE, LSEEK, IOCTL, POLL, the locks, BMAP, the forgets) stays on the CPU's lane. Teardown: the worker's FUSE side drains and releases as before and ends the session's `join` (`transport_done`); its thread then runs the lane until every fiber finished, detaches it, quiesces; `Group.deinit` joins, frees the lane, then the kmbuf region (`f326cbc`). §AB.4's spin counts a driven lane's committer `busy` (its owner serves a ring). Knob `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_HANDLERS_ON_WORKER` (1; 0 = the control arm). Model `src/model/worker_pass_model.zig` (weakenings: park with a fiber ready; a same-thread cell neither pumped after the run nor checked at the park; the lane disarmed after its scan; no READ of the lane's eventfd on the worker's ring — each fails). Red rows: `session` e2e "a namespace request's handler runs on the thread that delivered it" (256 own dispatches expected, 0 found on the cross-thread dispatch), `lane` "a DRIVEN lane" | The handoff pair was 18–33 µs of every request (send → end): GETXATTR 30–45 µs → 12 µs — the bare round trip `R` — OPEN 32–45 → 19–20 µs, and in mdtest MKDIR 108.5 → 88.8, CREATE 113.1 → 92.3, UNLINK 125.0 → 100.0, RENAME 109.5 → 86.6, OPEN 52.9 → 8.7 µs. A-B-B-A (dist, 3 rounds each): creates +19 %, removes +17 %, dir ops +9–14 % (the quiet series; +25–35 % in the noisy one), open/close 2.7–3.7×; random 4 KiB reads and writes and IOR unchanged. The data class stays on a lane because the data plane's device completions park the CALLING THREAD (`sync/completion.zig`, WP-C row 14): the all-on-worker cut lost 40 % of random writes (patch writes 121 k → 207 k, inode-key waits 53 k → 822 k behind a ring blocked on the device) — the worker never waits on a device (AB.1's rule). Owed, the owner's call: fiber-aware device completions (a fiber waiter parks its fiber, the completer enqueues it) would let the data class join; a ring-aware idle predicate (the worker ring's TASKRUN flag, its CQ, its cells) would let §AB.4's spin run on the merged lane. Evidence §9.4 |
| AB.4 The committer spins on its ack only when the spin can beat the park | `conveyor.AckSpin`: two RFC 6298 estimators — the ack (enqueue → answer, sampled at every answer) and the wake `w` (answer → the committer's resume, sampled ONLY on a park that left the caller's lane idle AND whose resume was the lane's next poll: `exec.lane.lanePolls` one more than at the park). The bound is the spin-then-park competitive rule on the measured costs: spin at most `2·w` — the CPU a spin may burn is at most twice the wake it saves — and only when the ack's mean is inside it (`spinBound(w, ack) = 2w iff ack ≤ 2w, else 0`; no `w` measured ⇒ 0), and only when the caller's lane has nothing else to run (`laneIdleBesidesCaller`: no ready fiber, an empty inbox — a spin on a busy lane delays its work). Ledger: every park is exactly one of `meta_commit_ack_spin_{hits,falls,skips,busy}`; the inputs are gauges (`_wake_ns`, `_ack_ns`, `_bound_ns`, published on change). Red row: `conveyor` "the ack spin's ledger" (5 parks off a lane are 5 `busy`, no wake sample; 5 on an idle seam are hits + falls + skips with ≥ 1 skip and ≤ skips + falls wake samples), plus the bound's tie test and the hit/fall-through row | §AA's decomposition priced the ack → committer hop at 17.5 µs of a QD1 create. Measured on the dev container (dist, 4 ranks, coalesced cadence): `w` = 8.5 µs, the ack's mean 148–309 µs (the §L window-fill hold and the write landing) ⇒ the bound is 0 and 98 % of parks skip; the spin engages only while the ack is short (674 hits, 12 fall-throughs at a phase start). A first cut sampled `w` on every idle-lane park and read 252 µs (other fibers ran in the gap), which let a committer spin half a millisecond — hence the poll-count rule. The lever is neutral at this posture by derivation, not by a tuned constant |
| AB.5 `FUSE_NO_OPEN_SUPPORT` / `FUSE_NO_OPENDIR_SUPPORT` — measured, the owner's call | Not implemented. The mode (7.2.6 `fuse_file_open`): the first OPEN answering `ENOSYS` sets `fc->no_open` for the connection; every open is then local (`fh` 0, `FOPEN_KEEP_CACHE`, a directory `| FOPEN_CACHE_DIR`) and no RELEASE is ever sent. What it costs: (1) `FUSE_ATOMIC_O_TRUNC` must go — with it, `fuse_setattr` skips the `ATTR_OPEN` SETATTR and no OPEN carries the truncate (an O_TRUNC open becomes a SETATTR); (2) the virtual inodes lose their generation pin and `FOPEN_DIRECT_IO`; (3) the write-back drain at last close (charter §6.2) loses RELEASE — it moves to a synchronous FLUSH per close (the clean-FLUSH `ENOSYS` fast path would set `fc->no_flush` forever) or to eviction / a timer; (4) no-opendir lets the kernel cache readdir pages, which a peer's change reaches only through the directory's `INVAL_INODE` (AB.2 names dentries, not pages). The open count becomes the kernel's lookup count (the last FORGET already certifies no open file). Unchanged: regular-file coherence (KEEP_CACHE is already every open's flag), `O_DIRECT` / `O_SYNC` (they ride every READ / WRITE), the shim's bind (control socket + `fstat`), the token leases | mdtest's census: OPEN 8,000 + RELEASE 16,000 of 88,013 requests (27 %) vanish; the file-read phase (2 requests per file today, OPEN 8.7 µs send → end after AB.3) would run at the kernel's local open + close ≈ 1–2 M/s against ≈ 180 k/s (5–10×; ext4 5.9 M/s); a create sheds its asynchronous RELEASE (≈ 47 µs of lane time, ≤ 5–10 %). Evidence §9.7 (a) |
| AB.6 One drain worker per kernel queue — measured, the owner's call | Not implemented. The kernel (7.2.6 `fuse_uring_task_to_queue`) picks the queue by the submitting task's CPU (`task_cpu(current)`, one queue per possible CPU, no fallback). A FUSE O_DIRECT read cannot issue NOWAIT, so fio's io_uring engine submits through io-wq workers whose CPUs vary: ONE io_uring job already lands on all five queues (workers 11–24 % each), and numjobs scales — QD32 1 job 167 k, 2 jobs 228 k, 4 jobs 244 k with no worker above 50 % (the 5-vCPU container is the ceiling); psync 1 job stays on its CPU's queue (16 k at 62 µs — latency-bound), 4 jobs 43 k. Designs: several workers per queue (the 7.2.6 code admits N rings registering entries of one queue — dispatch takes the first free entry; price: `queue->lock` contention × N at every commit, N kmbuf regions, N − 1 workers off the queue's CPU) or work stealing between lanes (a cross-thread handoff, 18–33 µs, per stolen request). **Corrected 2026-10-19 (`rung-10-workers-per-queue.md`): that admission is UserEnts-only — under kmbuf/zc the queue pins ONE ring's kmbuf payload ring at creation (`queue->bufring`) and the recycle resolves the group in the entry's ring, so a second ring on a queue corrupts the buffer accounting; N rings per queue need series patch 0030, and the in-process hand-off moves only the handler's CPU (the slot's DMA and the commit stay on the delivering ring) — the shipped depth-8 spill already does that. Landed 2026-10-19 on the booted 0030 kernel: the FEATURES probe (cmd_op 4) and two rings per kmbuf/zc queue (`SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_RINGS_PER_QUEUE`, derived 2 where the probe reads present — back to derived 1 the same evening after the train ran 3 reds in 7 on the two-ring daemon, then derived 2 again once the reds were pinned elsewhere (`rung-10-workers-per-queue.md` "the stall and the kill matrix": the > 5 s lookups were the shared-hot-dir row's daemon A waiting on a killed holder, logged on green one-ring gates too; the kill matrix's red was its `umount2` racing the kernel's release of the SIGKILLed daemon's references, now waited out by the harness; the lane-park unit test raced its own unheld DMA); each ring `ceil(depth/2)` / the rest of the entries, the depth and pinned bytes unchanged) — 4k randread QD32×1 1.73× (281 → 487 k), QD32×4 1.43× (871 k → 1.25 M, the §1.4 bar cleared); the 4k write QD1 / QD32×1 rows inside their spreads, all-own (depth 0) still 0.83× on QD32×1 with both workers at 0.83 cores — the bound moved from the worker's CPU to its pass/park structure, so `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_WRITE_OWN_DEPTH` stays 8, now a QUEUE's knee split over its rings (`worker.ownDepthShare`: 4 per ring, 1.06–1.07× over the whole knee per ring); the one red row was 4k randwrite QD32×4 at 0.85–0.93× of one ring (+1.2–2 cores) — the worker's pass structure, answered by the OWED SPIN (`worker.owedSpinBudget`: a pass with nothing in hand spins for a device completion it owes, bounded by twice the measured wake a park would cost, until one wake past the predicted landing; its A/B in the evidence note); `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_RINGS_PER_QUEUE=1` is the one-ring control arm.** | The "one job → one worker at 5.5 µs per read" ceiling binds only for submissions that stay on one CPU (a synchronous thread, a pinned job); no row here shows a saturated worker. Evidence §9.7 (b) |

## AC. fsck is linear in what it visits; the committer's frame law; tests bounded by counters (2026-10-13; `docs/evidence/rung-10-fsck-scale.md`)

The gate-speed measurement (rung-10 gate-speed §2.2) found the shared-hot-directory row's closing `fsck --offline` at 83.5 s for 52,994 entries in one 64-stripe directory and a flat directory near-quadratic (0.04 → 1.13 s for 2 k → 16 k). The performance ruling (§J's preamble) makes it a defect: fixed red-first with a derived bound.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AC.1 The set's merged range fetches a slot tree only once its front reaches the tree's routing-law bound, in doubling chunks | `meta/merged_walk.merge`: a k-way merge over the slot trees; a tree is consulted when the least head in hand reaches `key.slotTreeLowerBound(slot, start)` — an ino-major key of forest slot f lies in `[f << 40, (f + 1) << 40)`, a refs key leads with 0x06 above every ino-major key, so a walk over the ino-major space reads the trees one after another and a refs window reads every tree; each consulted tree is read from `⌈max / T⌉` doubling per refill, capped at what the call still needs. Per call over T trees consulting p and taking e: reads ≤ 2e + p·⌈max / T⌉ (exactly e when the call ends short of `max`), fetches ≤ p·(1 + ⌈log2(T + 1)⌉) — tie tests `readBound`, `fetchBound`, and the paging-equivalence row over 1/3/64/512 per page | The port's merged range read `max` from EVERY slot tree per call, sorted, kept `max`: a one-member set carries 64 slot trees (the native slot + 63 rotor slots), so a 512-entry page read up to 32,768 entries and descended 64 trees — a full walk quadratic up to T × page entries and 64× amplified past it; every fsck walk (the census's inode and layout kinds, the dentry pass, C1's raw walk, C12's overrun scan) and each of C11's per-ino owner probes paid it. Measured before → after on the venue (one flat directory, `fsck --offline`): 29 → 8 ms at 2 k, 2,713 → 152 ms at 32 k, 9,435 → 510 ms at 100 k; node reads per entry visited 8.1–9.4 → 0.18–0.23; entries read per entry visited up to 37 → 1.07; the hot-directory row's fsck 83.5 s → 0.79 s for 52,871 entries. No 0002 row: the reference walks the slot trees in slot order and scans refs one cursor per tree (`forest.rs` `range`/`refs_window`) — linear already; the defect was the port's own merge |
| AC.2 A record a tree holds outside its slot's window is visited only by C1's law-free walk | The merge fetches a tree FROM its bound, so its output is in key order whatever the trees hold, and a key a tree holds below its bound is not visited by the routed walk (no lookup reaches it either: lookups route by key). C1's raw pager takes `MetaSet.rangeRawCounted` (`census.Store.range_raw`): every tree's bound is the start — the true k-way merge, consulting every tree per call (≤ 2·max + T reads, ≤ T·(1 + ⌈log2(T + 1)⌉) fetches per page: linear) — so C1 still sees every record of every tree, a misplaced or malformed key included | C1 is the one class whose object is a record no reader can reach; every other walk reads what the routing law can serve |
| AC.3 fsck's walk is accounted and bounded in its own report | `census.WalkTally` through the store face (`KvTree`/`MetaSet` `rangeCounted`/`lookupCounted` count at the tree): the report's `nodes_walked` (set at last — the pager bumped the global per page and the report's field stayed 0), `entries_visited`, `entries_read`, `store_calls`, `trees_consulted`, and the wall clock per pass (`census_ms`, `dentries_ms`, `c1_ms`, `classes_ms`, `recheck_ms`, `scrub_ms`), MAX-merged across shards; `.stats` `fsck_entries_visited`, `fsck_entries_read`. The bound: `census.walkReadBound = 3 × visited + trees consulted` (AC.1 summed) and `census.walkNodeBound = height × (visited + calls)`. Red rows `fsck/scale.zig` (a real one-member set, 2 k → 8 k names in one flat directory and over a 64-stripe directory: both bounds, 4N ≤ 4.5 × N on reads and nodes — red before AC.1 at 2 k: 132,082 / 134,976 nodes against bounds 36,172 / 24,508) | A counter bound is the same verdict on an idle host and a saturated one; a wall-clock ratio is not. An fsck over N entries in D directories is O(N) entries read and O(N·height / page) node reads for its walks plus O(height) per point probe — whatever D and however the names are striped |
| AC.4 A waker never writes an object its waiter owns after the wake that may release it | `conveyor.Done.send` stamps `acked_ns` BEFORE its channel send (the spinner's non-zero stamp now means "the outcome is on its way": its recv takes it after the sender's channel lock). Red row `conveyor` "Done.send writes nothing once the receiver can take the outcome" (the `hold_after_send` seam holds the sender past the channel send; the receiver poisons its Done; any byte the sender writes after is found) | The committer's `Done` is a frame of its stack. The stamp after the send landed 8 bytes in whatever reused the frame — silent stack corruption of a committing thread or fiber — or, for a thread that had exited, in an unmapped stack: the journal-lane storm segfaulted at a stack address in `conveyor.answer`, 1 in 100 runs under 8-way load |
| AC.5 A test's timing property is restated on counters or driven by a seam | The journal-lane storm bounds its fiber parks by every wake the lane counts (publishes, one CQE per run pushed — the lane snapshot's `runs` — and per barrier, the structural entries' completion kicks, backstop ticks, the idle park) instead of `2 × windows + 1` (which held only while windows coalesced enough to absorb the storm's leaf splits and its barrier: 3/100 red under 8-way load); the lane timer-storm test counts spawns instead of seconds (150/150 red under 18-way load); the sync tick tests drive `tick.test_tick_ns` at 100 ms; `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_SUPERVISOR_PROBE_MS` drives the supervisor's cadence for the lazy-umount row | AGENTS.md's gate rules: a test must not depend on an idle host; never loosen an assertion — restate what it proves on an observable that holds under any load |
| AC.6 A per-suspect lookup resolves through an index built once | C2-lost's owner rides the derived census its arm iterates (`Pass.Derived{count, ino, block_index}`: the first owner in census order, as the scan answered); C9's block count probes an ino → bindings index of the census's owners built at the first candidate (`Pass.ownerBlocks`); C10's re-check counts a candidate's distinct `(parent, suffix)` identities off one pass over the fresh dentry pass's collected names (`Pass.dedupedNames`, memoized with the fresh pass); C17's routing check indexes every decoded map's stripes once, sorted by `(stripe, map, place)`, and looks each view up (`stripe.verifyStripes`; a map naming one ino twice routes it by its last place, as the scan did); C11's owners come off ONE paged walk of the map kind (`census.KindPager` over `TREE_BLOCK_MAP`) instead of a probe per ino; the ladder's C2-leaked / C3 / C6 re-checks share one derivation of the fresh census per volume (`Fresh.derived`). The bound: every lookup's work ≤ 3 × its inputs (owners or bindings, names, views + mapped stripes + routed names — plus the suspects), C11's store calls ≤ the entries its walk visits / `SCAN_PAGE` + 2 (+ the owners' own reads). Report fields `c2_owner_steps`, `c9_owner_steps`, `c10_name_steps`, `c11_store_calls`, `c17_route_steps`, `recheck_derived_steps` (SUM-merged; the text form's `lookups:` line). Red rows `fsck/tests.zig` "… is linear (0003 §AC.6)" (400 fill + 60 damaged: 25,830 → 60 / 520 owners examined, 14,340 → 180 names, 6,000 → 234 routes, 24,000 → 400 re-check bindings) and the C11 bound in `fsck/scale.zig` (2,003 → 5 store calls at 2 k flat names) | The owner ruling (§J): a cost superlinear in the damage is a defect. Each lookup scanned a whole census list per suspect — O(suspects × owners), O(candidates × names²), O(striped dirs × views × K) — so a damaged volume (the case fsck exists for) paid the product; C11 descended once per inode where one walk serves every owner. An index costs one pass over what the class already holds |

## AD. A slot lease moves its tree's live images with it (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-tripwire-hunt.md`)

The shared hot directory row fired `extent_grant_closure_violations` and
`meta_kv_replay_extent_violations` in the fsck-scale agent's loops: a joiner that took a slot by
handover recycled the departing holder's tree images into its own grant pool. The spec's T1/T5
custody move (`sym_slot_leases.md` §1 T1/T5, §4.5 "the receiver's install"; the reference's
`transfer_in`/`transfer_out`, `grant_record_minus_images`) was never written in the port.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AD.1 Every live image of a leased slot tree is in its lessee's grant | T1 (the grant): `acquire.Verbs.acquireSlots` walks the granted trees (`Seams.treeImages` — the manager's maintained tree, or the tree at the granted root) and writes the grantee's `extent_grant:` record GAINING them in the grant's ONE control entry (`Manager.rewriteForImages(.gain)`); the joiner's `installWireGrant` claims them as held in its grant (`JoinedSet.transferImages(.gain)`) under its SMO lock BEFORE its table word names it the holder; an in-process region's RAM grant follows (`RegionGrant.transferIn`). T5 (the release): `releaseSlot` walks the released tree (a wire lessee's read FROM THE DEVICE at the released root, the slot's cached nodes dropped first — the cache barrier of §X.5) and writes the departing record LOSING them in the release's entry; the joiner's `releaseDurablyFn` returns them from its grant after the release lands (`transferImages(.lose)`; a failed walk there is loud, never an abort that would reopen the door of a slot it no longer holds). The manager's own lease moves nothing (its images are untracked — the bitmap is its record). The walk reads interior nodes only (a leaf's extent is its parent's pointer). Counters `extent_grant_images_transferred_in/out` | The grant is a POOL (charter §5.5): a retired image recycles into the grant of the appender whose SMO retired it. Without T1 the images of a tree a joiner took by handover were in no record — its mirror marked them claimed (every extent outside the grant is) and its retirements recycled them into its pool anyway: its next claim of one was an `alloc` its recovery's screen refused (`meta_kv_replay_extent_violations` → the recovery rolled back every poll), a surplus withdrawal of one returned an extent the record did not name (`extent_grant_closure_violations`; the manager's return ignored it — leaked). Without T5 a released tree's images stayed in the departing record: a later carve of one the new maintainer retired met `extent_grant_conflicts`. Red row `handover_moves` "THE CUSTODY OF A MOVED TREE'S IMAGES" |
| AD.2 A joiner never recycles an image its grant does not hold | `SmoContext.grant_holds` (the joiner's `JoinedSet.grantHolds`: its grant bits under `bits_lock`, held only across bit operations — never across a wire call — so an SMO's retirement never waits on the manager): a retirement outside the grant fires `extent_grant_foreign_retirements` and keeps the extent allocated | After AD.1 it cannot happen; if it does, a leaked extent is space, an extent two custodians hand out is corruption — the reference's "claimed guard" (`free_pending` drops it) |
| AD.3 A custody move past the record's run cap refuses typed (superseded by §AE.4: the record is a chunk family, the refusal gone) | A record the move would take past `cr.extentGrantMaxRuns(recordValueCap)` (4,111 runs at the default 256 KiB node, 1,039 at 64 KiB) answers `Rejected`, loud, counted `extent_grant_custody_cap_refusals`: the lease does not move | A tree-0 value is bounded by the node's value cap; transferred images are scattered (a fragmented record). The envelope: ≈ 4 k scattered images of trees one joiner leases at the default geometry. A chunked record family is the scaling step past it (0002 row 524) |
| AD.4 A recovery's rewritten record is sized to its runs | `recovery.Driver` encodes the grant minus the kept images into a buffer of `extentGrantEncodedLen(runs)` (the entry enforces the value cap) where a fixed 256-run stack buffer refused a longer record `Corrupt` — every poll | A record holding recycled tree images as single-extent runs passes any fixed count; the bound that exists is the value cap |

## AE. Rung-10 symmetric defects after the tripwire hunt (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-symmetric-defects.md`)

The four items `rung-10-tripwire-hunt.md` §5 and §8 left open on the post-perf3 base.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AE.1 A merged readdir restart re-reads a stripe from D's old cover | `stripe.readdirMerged`: at `D`'s refill every stripe whose loaded page reached past `D`'s old range is read again from THAT RANGE'S END (`Stream.restart(…, old_cover)`), never from its last consumed cookie; the stream keeps no consumed-cookie word | U.3's restart re-read the range `D`'s old page and the stripe snapshots after it had already answered: a name the lazy migration moved there was listed from `D`'s snapshot and again from the stripe's fresh one, behind the reply's frontier — out of cookie order, so a continuation from it re-listed what followed (the shared hot directory row's C listing one name more than its `find`). Every entry of that range the stream held was consumed before `D`'s last (cookie order); a moved name was listed from `D`; a create there landed behind the listing (POSIX: unspecified). The range past the cover, unconsumed, is read again as before — U.3's no-miss argument is unchanged |
| AE.2 The membership cadence never shares a fiber with a pass that waits on a guard | `cli/mount.zig` `MembershipCadence` (a meta fiber of its own on a symmetric set): the cluster wire's tick (evictions, the grace reap, the death ledger's poll and its recoveries) and the parked guard scopes' expiry sweep with the register's publish (`Plane.sweepParked`). The reclaim cadence keeps the corpse reclaim, the cross-owner roll-forward (`Plane.cadenceTick`, no longer sweeping) and the stripe migration — passes that may wait on DLM guards. After a recovery the poll ADOPTS the recovered slots' open intents (`Plane.adoptRecovered`, reads only; `recovery_intents_adopted`) and the reclaim cadence completes them | A served `XvGuards` parks a scope in the holder's table that only the member's release or — for a member that died holding it — the expiry sweep after the owner's eviction frees. With the sweep and the eviction on the fiber whose roll-forward or migration waited on that scope, the wait was for ever: the shared hot directory row's 11 intents never rolled forward after C's death, the publish (the stuck tripwire's caller) stopped with them; storming C through its kill, A never evicted C and its own creates on that stripe wedged to the kernel's request timeout. The membership cadence is what dooms the lease and frees the scope, so it must be the side that never waits |
| AE.3 A token ask from a member whose lease the owner lists expired is a counted refusal, judged at every registration | `dlm_token_nonmember_refusals` is a `ShardedCounter` (a warning names the client); `Holder.serveGrant` judges the lease at the ask and again before each registration (`refuseExpired`); a frame the holder's pass retires at a lease's expiry or past the deadline posts the ack's wake, so a grant parked on that `(client, object)` recall re-judges at once | A member cannot ask past its own `T_self`, so a refused ask is a frame sent while live and served late — the session queued it, typically behind the member's own grant parked on its own recall (charter §5.5 Tokens: "a grant on an object a pass has in flight PARKS"), which completes only at the lease's expiry when the member died. The refusal is the correct answer, so it is a counter (0002 row 526). Judging once let that parked grant register a token for the expired member; no wake at the pass's expiry left it to its own park deadline (`T_owner`) with the session behind it |
| AE.4 The grant record is a family of stamped chunks; a change too large for its entry is staged behind a move marker | `extent_grant:{a}:{id}` (`kind ‖ tag ‖ a u32 BE ‖ id u64 BE`); each chunk one 4 KiB value — `ExtentGrantChunkHead` (48 B: version 2, kind runs/marker, the key's appender and id repeated, a marker's staged range, `checksum.stamp`) ‖ 1 … 253 runs; the record = the union of the effective chunks, pairwise disjoint (`grant_record.Family.load`). A change is a REPLACEMENT: each chunk owns the extent range from its first run to the next chunk's first run, the target is cut at those bounds, and every chunk whose share changed is deleted and its share written under fresh ids (`Family.plan`). A replacement that fits its change's entry rides it; one that does not is STAGED (`Writes`): the marker naming the fresh ids `[lo, hi]` first (a chunk inside a present marker's range counts for no reader), the fresh chunks in entries of half the entry payload, then the change's own entry carries its records, the replaced chunks' deletes and the marker's delete. A marker found at a load never landed: its staged chunks then the marker are deleted before the next change (`extent_grant_markers_swept`). The custody move's run-cap refusal (§AD.3) and the return's run-cap refusal are gone; a carve is cut to `carveExtentsMax()` = one entry's room for its `alloc` frames and at worst one chunk delete each (2,298). fsck's forest census reads every family whole: one that does not assemble is `C14GrantRecordCorrupt` (report-only). The envelope, stated: a change whose own entry cannot carry the deletes of the chunks it replaces (≈ 2,000 chunks, ≈ 500 k runs touched) answers `Rejected` | A record is the manager's attestation of every extent a lessee holds — its pool AND every live image of every tree it leases (§AD.1); a long-lived slot's tree carries thousands of scattered images, so one value (4,111 runs at 256 KiB) refused its handover and one entry (128 KiB) bounds any whole rewrite. 4 KiB is the journal's grain: a carve or a return that changes one run rewrites one page, not the 64 KiB record. The marker makes a multi-entry change atomic for every reader without a second copy of the record: the old chunks stay effective until the entry that deletes them and the marker lands; a crash between leaves only ineffective chunks, never a record naming less than the old or more than the new — the custody law (one custodian per extent) holds at every entry boundary. 0002 row 525 |


## AF. Zero-message opens for regular files (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-dev-perf.md` §10)

§AB.5 measured `FUSE_NO_OPEN_SUPPORT` and left it to the owner; the orchestrator's posture (the
owner chose none; decision 0003's performance-first ruling, every durability law kept): no-open
for regular files, OPENDIR stays, the write-back drain moves from RELEASE to FLUSH. Verified
against the 7.2.6 sources (`fs/fuse/{file,dir,inode}.c`): `fuse_file_open` sets `fc->no_open` at
the first OPEN answered ENOSYS; `fuse_file_put` then ends every `FUSE_RELEASE` locally — for a
file a CREATE opened and for one opened before the latch alike; `fuse_flush` sends FLUSH on every
`filp_close` unless `FOPEN_NOFLUSH` (without the writeback cache) or `fc->no_flush`, which an
ENOSYS sets for good; `fuse_evict_inode` is the only FORGET of a live inode; `fuse_do_setattr`
skips the ATTR_OPEN truncate iff `atomic_o_trunc`; `fuse_vma_close` writes a mapping back at
munmap, after its fd's close.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AF.1 OPEN is answered ENOSYS; the daemon latches the connection at ready | `lifecycle` answers OPEN `ENOSYS` before the filesystem or a guard sees it (`fuse_open_enosys`); CREATE counts no open; OPENDIR / RELEASEDIR are served as before. `mount` opens its own `.stats` once before it reports ready (`latchNoOpen`), so no CREATE-opened file is ever released with a RELEASE. Knob `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_NO_OPEN` (default 1; 0 = the OPEN/RELEASE control arm, tested beside it) | mdtest's census (§AB.5): OPEN 8,000 + RELEASE 16,000 of 88,013 requests. Before the latch a fresh mount's creates still sent RELEASEs (the census's first cut: RELEASE 8,000 in an mdtest started cold). OPENDIR stays: no-opendir lets the kernel cache readdir pages that a peer's change reaches only through the directory's own `INVAL_INODE` (AB.2 names dentries, not pages) — owed, not here |
| AF.2 The write-back drain at close rides FLUSH | A CREATE's reply drops `FOPEN_NOFLUSH`; a FLUSH is never ENOSYS. The drain worker asks the filesystem's try-only `probe_flush` (`DataPlane.hasWriteCustody`: the parked table, the overlay registry, the rewrite epochs — spin-locked sections only, O(1) on an empty set) and answers the close itself when nothing is held (`transport_flush_inline_serves`); custody demotes the FLUSH to the CPU's lane — the data class (`worker.onWorkerLane(.flush, no_open)` is false) — which drains (`data.releaseIno`, the RELEASE drain) before the reply (`fuse_flush_drains`). Charter §6.2's law (flush at close) unchanged; its hook moved | The kernel tells the daemon of a close only through FLUSH under no-open. The synchronous drain is stronger than the control arm's asynchronous RELEASE: close(2) returns after the bytes left RAM. The price the census measured: a FLUSH round trip per close where the control arm's open paid an OPEN round trip — the drain law keeps the synchronous request the mode was to remove (an open/close pair's requests 2 → 1, not 2 → 0) |
| AF.3 A close burst coalesces: one FLUSH-drain per ino per reclaim tick | The kernel sends FLUSH for every close of every copy of an fd — a dup's, a forked child's at its exit, its exec (under `O_CLOEXEC` too). A FLUSH with custody on an ino a FLUSH drained less than one reclaim tick ago (`OpenTable.recentlyDrained` / `markDrained`) notes it owed `deferred` (`deferDrain`) and replies on the worker (`fuse_flush_drains_deferred`); the reclaim cadence drains deferred inos at its next tick | Measured (cache-less set, 2,000 `/bin/echo` children appending through one held fd): draining each close re-uploaded the extending append's whole 4 MiB block — 8.1 GB of device writes for 143 KB (57,000×), 16–21 s; coalesced 4.2–8.4 MB, 0.2–3.7 s (the control arm 4.2 MB, its one RELEASE at the shell's close) |
| AF.4 Custody no close covers is owed and drained once idle | Every WRITE notes its ino owed after the filesystem recorded its custody (`OpenTable.noteWrite`; an IPC host write the same; a W1 direct-drive patch, durable at completion, notes nothing); a FLUSH covers the notes made before it began (`flushBegin` / `flushEnd` — the stripe sequence under the stripe lock, so either the probe sees a write's custody or the write's note follows the FLUSH's begin); the reclaim cadence drains an owed ino whose latest write is `OWED_IDLE_NS` old (the rewrite epoch's TTI/10 = 30 s; seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_FUSE_OWED_IDLE_MS`). A note that cannot be recorded drains before the write's reply | Under no-open nothing tells the daemon a file's last reference went: a held-open writer, a mapping written back after its fd closed, an async write landing after its close leave custody no FLUSH follows. TTI/10 is the charter's own bound for custody nothing closes; long enough that a writer pausing between bursts keeps its parked blocks (the W2 fold "at the last moment"). The control arm's RELEASE drained the post-close classes at once: the bound there is now ≤ TTI/10 + one tick |
| AF.5 O_TRUNC is a SETATTR(size 0) | A no-open session does not echo `FUSE_ATOMIC_O_TRUNC` (`InitWants.atomic_o_trunc`); the kernel sends the O_TRUNC open's SETATTR(size 0), which takes the size setter's exclusive guard (the lifecycle's "SETATTR iff size") and ships to a foreign holder like any truncate | With the flag the kernel skips that SETATTR ("done by OPEN") and no OPEN carries the truncate — red in the session e2e row (zero size-setting SETATTRs reached the filesystem) |
| AF.6 The open count is OPENDIR's; the custody is the kernel's lookup count | `open_count_stranded` now names a lost RELEASE (control arm) or a lost RELEASEDIR (no-open: only OPENDIR counts) — re-defined, not retired. The unlinked-open custody keys on the last FORGET as before | An open file pins its dentry, so its inode cannot be evicted and no FORGET comes while any `struct file` holds it; the corpse destroy never read the count |
| AF.7 The virtual inodes need no open | The kernel's lookup reference pins a generation (no FORGET while a file holds it); the immutable generation's exact size at LOOKUP / GETATTR and TTL 0 make every page-cache read one whole document; a new generation is a new ino, so no `INVAL_INODE` is owed. A write or truncating open no OPEN refused (root's, under `default_permissions`) answers EACCES at the WRITE / SETATTR | The reference itself calls `FOPEN_DIRECT_IO` "a belt" for generation inos (`fuse_client.rs` :27535). Smoke: 200 reads under a create storm, each one parsed document of exactly the fstat size; two sequential opens, two generations |
| AF.8 The shim's handoff is unchanged | The bind travels the control socket with `SCM_RIGHTS`, screened by `fstat` / `F_GETFL`; the bootstrap is a GETXATTR — neither touches OPEN or `fh`. The shim smoke runs in the gate | §AB.5's audit, confirmed by the gate's shim smoke on the no-open default |

0002 rows 528–531.

## AG. Rung-10 coherence defects (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-coherence-defects.md`)

The five items `rung-10-dev-perf.md` §9.4 and §9.8 left open.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AG.1 A transaction has ONE owner at every instant; a full intent register refuses | Every store's `commit(t)` consumes `t` on every path (its refusals included), so a staging `errdefer abandon(t)` closes its block before the commit (`{ errdefer abandon(t); stage… } commit(t)`) or carries the ownership flag the commit clears; the rail `tests/rails.zig` "tx ownership rail" keeps it (33 sites fixed: the writer claim's commit and clear, the fsck CLI seam, the fsck repair engine, the fsck harnesses, a `kv_fs` seam). The cross-owner intent register forgets an intent whose record never became durable (`ops.leaveOpen(…, durable, …)`) and refuses an insert past its reservation (`Register.InsertError.RegisterFull` → `Deferred`, `xv_register_full_refusals`). Red rows: `gate` "the writer claim's commit failing EIO at open …" (ABRT: `Bus error` through the freed guard set), `two_holders` "an intent whose step-0 commit fails EIO … leaves NOTHING on the register" (`expected 0, found 1`) | §9.4's `incorrect alignment` under a device EIO was the claim commit's failed `commit` freeing the transaction and its errdefer deinit-ing the freed memory (the Debug poison in its allocator field); with it fixed, every write failing at the handover step overran the register's 1,024-entry map (`integer overflow` in `putAssumeCapacity`) — one abandoned ghost per move-gate re-issue of an op whose step 0 never landed. Charter §1.8: a device error fails loud and typed, never a panic in a handler |
| AG.2 A kernel entry answered under a token never outlives the token's coherence | Every token serve stamps its entry `kernel_pin_until = now + the mount's longest kernel TTL` (`Divert.setKernelPin(peered_ttls.longestNs())`, set by `mount` before it serves; unbounded until set); room-making DROPS a kernel-pinned entry without a `Release` (`Cache.dropOrRetireLocked`, `dlm_token_kernel_pinned_drops`) and releases only an entry past its pin. A peer's change is visible "at notify speed": once the reader's notifier wrote what its recall queued before the ack (`transport_notify_pending`, the sideband's unanswered fire-and-forget writes). Seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_TOKEN_EVICT_AFTER_SERVE`. Red rows: `tokens` "a reader whose kernel may still cache an answer served under a directory's token keeps hearing the holder's changes after its cache EVICTS the token" (`expected 1, found 0`), `token_cache` "the kernel pin", the smoke "INVALIDATION COHERENCE ACROSS DAEMONS" with B evicting every entry (red at the second step) | AB.2's open question: a holder recalls only the readers its table lists, and a reader's cache RELEASED a token to make room while its kernel still held entries answered from it (a negative `d/y`, a positive, attributes) for their TTL — no recall, a stale kernel view for 1 s peered (60 s in the row). Of the two laws the question named, the parent's token is KEPT at the holder for as long as the kernel may cache what it answered (the token table is the per-parent reader set already; no new structure, no wire change). The recall's `INVAL_ENTRY` cannot be awaited before the ack (its write takes the parent's `i_rwsem`, which a request waiting on that recall may hold), so "at once" means once the reader's notifier drained. 0002 row 527 |
| AG.5 A successor's grace never waits for a member proven dead | The roster a manager's successor arms its shard with (`WirePlane.priorRoster`) applies the clients classifier's dead-pid proof: a same-host `member:` row whose pid is absent or another incarnation is not expected (`MemberRow.expectsReassertion`, `membership_grace_dead_rows`); a live row, another host's and an undecodable one are. Seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_MEMBERSHIP_LEAVE_LOST`. Red rows: the smoke `THE -o ro READER OF A LIVE SET` with B's leave lost (C's remount `grace_refused`), `shard` "a successor's grace expects no same-host predecessor whose pid is proven dead" | A clean leave is best effort on both sides, so a row can outlive its member; the successor expected it, nothing could re-assert it, and the fresh half refused every stranger — C's `-o ro` remount — to the window's deadline. A dead process holds no lease to re-assert: its next mount is a new incarnation recovered through the death ledger |
| AG.4 A kmbuf ENOMEM names the region and its levers, never a contiguous-page shortage | `Refusal.kmbuf_ring_enomem = { pages, buffers, buf_size, memlock_charged }`: one queue's region takes `regionPages(ring_entries, buf_size, page)` pages at once; the kernel falls back to order-0 pages but draws them from the free lists only; the levers `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_MAX_WRITE`, `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_OVER_IO_URING_Q_DEPTH`, `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_BUFFER_MODE=user`, and `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` / `CAP_IPC_LOCK` when `CapEff` bit 14 is clear. No fallback grain: none exists (below). Owed: the kernel patch completing a short bulk fallback with reclaiming per-page allocations. Red row: `session` e2e "the kmbuf ring answering ENOMEM after every retry" | The venue kernel's region allocator (patch 0003 `io_region_allocate_pages_multi_buf`) tries one compound block per buffer and falls back to order-0 pages, so high-order availability never fails a mount and smaller buffers need the same pages; the ENOMEM is the no-reclaim bulk fallback (all or nothing per region) or the memlock charge. The old detail ("order-8 contiguous pages") named a resource that cannot bind and hid the levers that do |
| AG.3 C8's online re-check reads a block's references and its owners' layouts in ONE state | Phase C for `c8_drift` is per block (`Pass.c8Settled`): the candidate owners (the first round's census owners binding the block, plus the owners its durable references name now) locked together under their exclusive 4a leases (`Host.lockInos`), the durable references re-read under them (an owner that appeared since joins the set and the round repeats, bounded `C8_SETTLE_ROUNDS` = 4; past it the suspect clears, counted `c8_owner_set_moving`), every candidate's CURRENT bindings counted under the same leases; an owner set wider than one lock plan or a layout the codec refuses keeps the whole-view comparison. Test hook `Host.c8_hook`. Red row: `fsck` "C8's online re-check reads a block's durable references and its owners' layouts in ONE state" (`[C8] … block 40 — durable refs 0 vs derived 1`).
