Owed: the in-flight registry held from every releasing commit to its terminal free (the `[C2] leaked` window) | The whole-view re-check (a fresh census, then a refs scan: two walks) re-ran the race it was meant to settle: a publish or a destroy moves a block's reference and its layout in one tx under the owner's lease, and a destroy between the walks read `durable 0 vs derived 1` — the macOS gate's finding (11/60 under load on pristine `dev`, 0/60 after) |


## AH. Rung-10 gate blockers: the release window, the ladder's evidence order, rows that asserted a schedule (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-gate-blockers.md`)

The rows that could still redden a gate under load after §AG: the online fsck's `[C2] leaked` window §AG.3 left owed, and three rows that passed alone and failed beside a loaded host.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AH.1 A release is a live owner of its block from before its releasing commit until its free | Every site that commits a reference release holds the released blocks in the in-flight registry (`BlockAllocator.holdInflight / dropInflight`, the list-shaped twin of `InflightAllocGuard` on the same per-cell count; `write_seams.ReleaseHolds`) from BEFORE the commit until AFTER the block's free — dropped on every path that leaves without the free, so a release that never freed is a leak the census sees, never a shield. Sites: the handler's `AfterCommit` flows (corpse destroy, SETATTR truncate, O_TRUNC, the staged write's displaced copy, the promotion's: `holdAfter` before the commit, `dropAfter` deferred), the plane's merges (inline, the crossing, the tree-mapped window — `MergeOutcome.holds`, dropped by the write path's callers after their frees), the extend barrier and the sweep chunks (held before each page's commit, freed then dropped), the packed promotion's displaced copy, the mover's republished source, the fsck repair's destroy. The epoch's save frees its parked keys BEFORE popping its B guards (an intermediate B is an unpublished mint until its free); the own block a promotion mints stays registered until its publish lands (`ownBlockPublished` / `abandonOwnBlock`). A drop with none held is refused and counted (`block_inflight_unheld_drops`); a released binding reaching `DataPlane.freeBindings` unheld fires `release_free_unheld` (both must stay 0). The reclaim's elided debt and its queue need nothing: `beginFree` drops the count before either, and a queued block carries the queue's own registration. Offline nothing is in flight: an offline leak stays a finding | Between a release's commit (the record, the layout and the `−ref` gone in one tx) and its terminal free, the allocator tracks a block no durable reference and no record names — the departed owner cannot be locked, so a census and its re-check that fall inside the window confirm a leak the free resolves a moment later (4/150 under 14 × `yes` on macOS after §AG.3, the pristine storm 6/150 on the loaded venue). Census scoping cannot close it; only the producer can say "mine, in flight". The reference registers mints alone (0002 row 532) |
| AH.2 The ladder reads its fresh evidence after every suspect's registry check, and the tracking arms read the allocator after their derivation | `Pass.recheck` runs Phase A over every suspect, then Phase C over the standing ones: the ONE fresh census a pass memoizes is read after every suspect's registry check (interleaved, the census the first suspect's re-check read predated the publish that ended the next suspect's mint, confirming it `[C2] leaked`). C2-leaked and C3 confirm only while, re-read AFTER the fresh derivation, the block is still tracked (at the judged count for C3), no live owner holds it in flight and no mint under this scan replaced it (`Pass.trackingStands`): registry-absence first, then the references, then the allocator. Phase C iterates the standing list (C6's re-check appends to the suspect list). The ladder's test hook is one face (`Host.recheck_hook`: the C8 points, Phase A's registry boundary, the tracking arms' derivation) | The memoized census is the §AC.6 linearity (one per pass); its read must still follow the registry check it confirms against. A block freed and minted to a new writer between Phase A and the verdict is the writer's (its epoch stamp says so); read before the derivation, the stale tracking confirmed the new owner's block |
| AH.3 C6's in-flight term counts an untracked block no other term counts | The flat arm's `inflight_untracked` skips a free-listed block (`freeBlocksCount`'s) and a trim-windowed one (`trimClaimed`'s) | A release held past its free (AH.1) or a trim claim's guard sits on a block another term already subtracts: counted twice, it read `[C6] … 1 set-but-unreferenced (leak)` (11/150 on the venue storm once the storm held its releases) |
| AH.4 A row asserts what the product guarantees, never the schedule it happened to get | The write pipeline's admission row: the park window is held exactly (`Governor.test_park_window`), the parked case judges `ticks ≤ waits ≤ 1 + ticks`, `quiesce` waits unbounded. The §L window-fill row measures the steady pairs past a rendezvous (the worth gate opened), the slack the row's own restricted to the windows judged. The cache-path smoke counts a big file's promotion by either arm (`fuse_write_promotions_to_striped + layout_promoted_block`) and, on the staging set, judges the rm by the online census once every removed file was destroyed and the reclaim drained (df within the pack census's bound: the dispatch packs small residents at the same pressure); its LOOPED repro writes fresh storm files and judges the identity per pass. The fsck storm row judges runs that overlapped the storm's progress, until its shields engaged | `admission_waits == +1` held only inside the 5 ms tick and after the re-check; the gate's learning instant is the schedule's (a probe meeting a stall keeps it off a period, failing four bounds at once); a staged file leaves the ring by its next write or by the plane's pressure promotion, whichever runs first (the noopen gate: 11 own-block DMAs, fewer than 10 by the write path); on the loaded venue the ten storm runs ended before its threads were scheduled |

## AI. Rung-10 gate blockers, second wave (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-gate-blockers-2.md`)

Four rows the release gate's three-clean-runs law met: the joiner rejoin row, the nvmet sigstop row, the online-volume row's tripwire, the sideband abort.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AI.1 A joiner's returns reach the manager's free heap when their entry lands | `Manager.returnInner` (ReturnExtents, the leave's unclaimed runs, the next join's residue) and `settlePending` (a GrowRing segment no page names) release every returned extent's RAM bit (`releaseUnpublished`) once their control entry landed — the reference's order (`return_extents_inner` / `settle_pending_ring_segment`: `release_unpublished` per landed chunk). The control entry only JOURNALS the kind-4 deltas (`bind.zig` `controlEntryFn`); the RAM bits move by the claim / release seams alone, and the manager's RAM fake now does the same (it applied the deltas to its bitmap and hid the leak). The recovery's return (`volume_bind.returnExtentsFn`) no longer releases a second time. Red rows: `manager` "a joiner's returns reach the manager's FREE heap when their entry lands" and the grants / leave rows under the faithful fake | Every extent a joiner handed back stayed allocated in the manager until its next mount: the rejoin row's B refills its pool at its SMO rate (the derived target, hundreds of extents) and returns it at every clean leave, and the manager's free heap fell 985 → 0 in three cycles of a 990-extent heap — B's fourth join was granted 65 of its 72 floor, then `no heap extent for slot N's first tree (heap full)` or `JoinAppender refused for capacity` (4/14, 2/13 in the symmetric agent's loops). After: 811 free at the row's end |
| AI.2 A row judges two fields of one `.stats` render only when one render can show both | The nvmet sigstop row waits for `writer_guard_fenced` = 1, then judges the counter and `writer_guard_verdict` on a render BEGUN after it: a render writes the volume's verdict (`meta.set`) before the process-wide counter (`meta.guard`), and `FailureLatch.noteFence` latches the verdict before it counts, so the render that first shows the count may have read the verdict ahead of the latch. No product change (one lane, one latch); the guard row pins the order | The gate's "verdict `healthy` beside a fenced count of 1": not another volume's lane (the set has one meta volume), the render's own section order |
| AI.3 A joiner never learns a volume before its holder armed it | The set-config generation the membership grants carry moves at the lattice's step 7 — `World.publishPlacement(vol_tag, state, generation)` after the runtime act (the online add: attach, the grant-plane arm, Active) — never at the record's commit; the word is raised, never lowered (`Gauge.raise`; two ops publishing out of order). `ResolveSetConfig` answers `Deferred` for a record AHEAD of the word (`lattice.recordAhead`; the joiner counts `volume_config_pull_deferrals` and keeps its word). A data-plane verb for a volume the holder has not armed answers `not_holder` when volume 0's record names it ahead of the word (a joiner that mounted inside the window read it off the projection) and when no grant plane is armed yet on the manager (the listener precedes `bindDataPlanes`; a lease refused busy) — `block_grant_unarmed_answers`, the writer asks again at its next mint; it stays the `cluster_wire_service_refusals` refusal only for a volume no such record names, or a verb misrouted to a joiner. The holder's and the writer's rows publish their count after the row (release / acquire). Seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_ONLINE_ARM_HOLD`; red row: the online volume row (A's arm held until a verb named the unarmed d2: refused, the tripwire). 0002 row 533 | The commit of `add-data`'s record published the new generation before A attached and armed d2: B learnt d2 at its next renewal, armed its writer row and its first pull asked A's holder for d2 inside the arm's window — `this mount does not hold the lease of that volume`, the tripwire of the gate's online-volume failure under load |
| AI.4 A pool free list's give-back retries a refused push | The FUSE sideband's two cell pools are one `CellPool` (free list + recycle word) whose `give` counts `transport_sideband_recycle_respins` and spins (`mpmc.pushSpin`) until the taker stores; the reclaimer's cell pool, the NVMe ranged read slab and the zcrx cid pool likewise; the `uring_fs` and journal-lane request-cell pools and the `uring_fs` request channel (several workers pop) wait the same way through `mpmc.pushSpinUnlessFull`, which answers false only on a FULL ring so their must-stay-0 tripwires still name a double give-back (`rung-10-uringcell.md`; oversizing the ring is no fix — the model's lapped weakening). Model `src/sync/mpmc_pool_model.zig` (two takers, a two-cell ring at its population): holds with the retried give-back, the weakening (ONE asserted `tryPush`) fails. Red row: `session` "a sideband cell given back while another taker sits between its dequeue CAS and its sequence store" (SIGABRT at the assert before the fix) | The sideband abort (`completeCell → recycle → assert(free.tryPush)`) was no double recycle: the ring holds the pool's WHOLE population with several takers, and Vyukov's push at position p reads the cell the pop at p − cap releases only after its dequeue CAS — a taker preempted between the two while a second taker's pop and the give-back run past it makes the push answer FULL one cell short (`mpmc.pushSpin`'s documented class; reproduced under 4 × `yes`) |

## AJ. Close is no durability point: the write-back timer (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-writeback-timer.md`)

The owner's ruling (2026-10-14), verbatim: **"Yes: drain on a bounded timer."** As restated with
it: closes become fully local, and acknowledged buffered writes (the accumulation table, the W2
overlays, the staged arm, the write pipeline's custody) become durable within a DERIVED bound (the
flush cadence) or at fsync, fdatasync, syncfs and unmount — like ext4, where close is not a
durability point either; a crash may lose up to that bound of UNSYNCED writes, synced writes are
never lost; measured A/B before it becomes the default. It amends §AF.2–AF.4 and charter §1.10,
§6.2.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AJ.1 The bound | `T_wb = A + τ + D`: `τ` = the timer's period = the flush cadence (`checkpointTickPeriodMs(F)`: 50 ms derived, the strict cadence's 100 ms tick), `A` = the age a custody must reach = the checkpoint's cadence ceiling (`SQUEEZEFS_CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE_MS`, 1 s; `F` is its twentieth), `D` the pass's drain. Shipped: `T_wb = 1,050 ms + D` — with `D ≤ τ`, the landing ceiling a dirty metadata leaf has (`max_age + 2 × tick` = 1,100 ms, KD-SYM-10) (`writeback_bound_ns` = `A + τ`, `writeback_age_ns`; `writeback_residency_ns` measures birth → landed). Under load `D ≤ C ÷ W`: `C` ≤ the budget's write-custody components (`parked_full_buffers` at its cap `max(256, budget/16 ÷ B)` × B, `parked_extents`, the governor's in-flight permits), where a writer past the cap parks at the parked gate or lands the coldest custody itself — memory never grows past the components, and the time bound degrades by `C ÷ W`, never unboundedly | `τ`: the cadence the times drain and the coalesced barrier already ride, O(1) per stripe when nothing is owed. `A`: a block filled within the age writes itself through (`union == whole block`), so only a streamer slower than `B ÷ A` (4 MB/s at 4 MiB) lands a partial block — `A = τ` was measured first and landed IOR's in-progress blocks partial under host load (1.27 device bytes per user byte against the close posture's 1.00); and one ceiling for every acked byte, data and metadata alike |
| AJ.2 Every close is local | A FLUSH answers ENOSYS: the kernel latches `fc->no_flush` for the connection — the daemon's own close of `.stats` at ready is the latch (`latchNoOpen`), so `fuse_flush_enosys` reads 1 per mount. A CREATE (and the control open arm's OPEN) answers `FOPEN_NOFLUSH`; RELEASE drains nothing | 7.2.6 `fuse_file_open`: a zero-message open's `open_flags` are the kernel's own `FOPEN_KEEP_CACHE` — no reply exists to carry `FOPEN_NOFLUSH`, so `no_flush` is the only switch for those closes (`fuse_flush` still runs `write_inode_now` before it, so a shared mapping's dirty pages still go out as WRITEs at close and at munmap — owed custody the timer lands) |
| AJ.3 One timer pass over an age-ordered set | Every WRITE (and an IPC host write) notes its ino with the birth of its oldest custody (the first note since it held none) in a per-stripe min-heap; ONE meta fiber wakes per `τ` and `timerPass` takes every ino born by `now − A`, drains it through `drainAged` — the overlay records and the rewrite epoch whole, the parked blocks born by the cutoff (`flushParkedBlock`, §AK's in-place drain included), the younger blocks kept — and re-owes what it kept at its birth (never earlier than the next cutoff: a failing drain is retried next pass, not spun on). No per-ino timers; the teardown awaits the pass in flight (never cancelled mid-DMA), the dismount pass lands the rest | Per-block age, not per-ino: a continuous writer always holds some custody, and an ino-grained age would land its in-progress block at every pass |
| AJ.4 The crash law of an unsynced write | After a kill -9 each acknowledged FUSE WRITE of an unsynced file is whole or absent — zeros, or past the file's recovered length (an unsynced extension may revert to the last durable length: ext4's `data=ordered`, §AL) — never torn and never another file's bytes; a synced write is always whole. A `write(2)` longer than `max_write` is several FUSE WRITEs the daemon acks one by one and may land in part (POSIX promises no more; ext4 neither). fsync, fdatasync, syncfs and the unmount drain synchronously as before. ONE crash law with §AL.5: the timer moves when DATA lands, and a size follows its data — an extending write's size claim parks and journals once the bytes under it are durable: the timer's pass lands them and the times drain it wakes right after the pass journals the claim behind one data barrier (`afterTimerPass`), so an unsynced extension is durable within `T_wb` + one drain and reverts whole to the last durable size before it; under the close posture (AJ.5) the drained close journals the claim before it answers (`closeDrain`), so §AF's "a kill after the close finds the file whole" keeps holding | The smoke rows judge at that grain: a 1 MiB + 1000 B `pwrite` landed its 1 MiB head (the device overlay publishes at its completion) and lost its parked tail |
| AJ.5 The knob | `SQUEEZEFS_WRITEBACK_POSTURE=timer\|close`, default `timer` (the A-B-B-A, `rung-10-writeback-timer.md`); `close` is §AF's drain at the close the kernel reports, the control arm, its rows kept (`SQUEEZEFS_WRITEBACK_POSTURE=close` in the §AF smoke rows). The seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_FUSE_OWED_IDLE_MS` is the owed age in both postures (a row that must see custody parked holds the timer away) | — |
| AJ.6 The measured price, and what is owed | The A-B-B-A (`rung-10-writeback-timer.md`): closes 34–47× from one thread, 2.5–2.7× from four, mdtest file read 9.7×, the open-append-close loop's device bytes 0.91×; a held-open random 4 KiB rewrite (fio QD32, 15 s) 0.60–0.66× IOPS at 1.32–1.36 device bytes per user byte against the close posture's 0.13. Owed (the data plane's drain cost, not the trigger): a drain of a seeded full buffer lands its dirty pages in place instead of the whole block, and an upload superseded by a newer write publishes its snapshot instead of freeing the DMA — landed as AJ.7 | The close posture's random-rewrite number is RAM residency: fio's close was the first durability point, so 15 s of acknowledged O_DIRECT writes (≤ the parked cap) lived only in daemon memory. Landing them within `T_wb` re-parks each block every second — a seeded 4 MiB upload per drain, ≈ 25 % of them superseded by the next write (latest-wins frees the DMA) — and the W1 patches the drains re-enable go 1:1 to the device |
| AJ.7 A content-valid buffer drains its dirty pages; a superseded upload publishes its snapshot | Every content-valid full buffer tracks `dirty` (the pages written since the cell last matched `clean_base`) over `clean_base`, the binding its clean pages equal: the seed's base (`fillComplementFrom`, `checkoutSeeded`), an escalated overlay's base, a published snapshot's key. `flushParkedBlock` lands it IN PLACE when W1's state half passes (`beginInPlace`: passthrough, undecorated whole-block mapping, sole owner through the fence pair, no epoch binding, no overlay record) and the authoritative map still names `clean_base`: ONE DMA per contiguous dirty run from the cell under (3), the W1 postlude, the entry retires (`drain_inplace_dirty_drains`, its bytes in `drain_inplace_write_bytes`). A write landing during an upload's DMA no longer frees it: the snapshot — one consistent image at its stamp, taken under (3) — publishes (`write_pipeline_superseded_publishes`), the entry stays parked with `dirty_since_snap` (the pages written since the snapshot) over the published key, born at the snapshot; the upload re-drives only for writes its caller owes (`flushParkedBlockBy`: a durable flush owes every acked write, the timer its cutoff, the write-through trigger none). `write_pipeline_supersessions`, `_superseded_bytes` and the `superseded` disposition are retired: nothing abandons a DMA | **Per pass `≤ pages(dirty) × P ≤ Σ_w pages(w) × P`** — device ÷ user ≤ 1 for page-aligned rewrites (each acked page lands at most once per write), never `B` per pass: a random rewriter's stream-adjacent hit parked a deferred full buffer (W1 then declines on RAM custody), its run table overflowed into a whole-block seed, and every timer pass uploaded the block whole — superseded by the next write ≈ 90 % of the time on kamrui, the DMA freed and redone (1.80 device bytes per user byte; the unit row: 65,536 B for five dirty pages). Visibility: the write-side view outranks the map for every byte of a content-valid entry, so the older published image is never served after the newer write was acked; the kept entry's successor lands only what changed after the snapshot (in place once the epoch saved). Crash: a dirty run is one DMA from a cell no writer mutates under (3) and a write's pages are contiguous, so a cut between DMAs leaves every FUSE write whole or absent; a failed DMA leaves the entry parked, loud (the kill rows) |

0002 row 537.

## AK. An extending append's drain lands its touched pages; the transformed tail; ONE `.stats` document (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-append-drain.md`)

The owner's ruling (§J: a measured write amplification is a product defect) met
`rung-10-dev-perf.md` §10.4's owed row: on a set without a cache path an open-append-close
loop drained every append by re-uploading its whole 4 MiB block — 1.74 GB of OST writes for
500 `echo >> f` (36 KB, ×48,351) on the perf container. Symbols as §J: `B` the block, `P` the
device grain (4 KiB), `F` the transform frame (64 KiB), `τ` an AEAD tag, `u` the bytes a block
received since its last landing, `r` the block's live extent.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AK.1 A deferred full buffer drains IN PLACE at the page grain | `flushParkedBlock` (every drain: RELEASE / the no-open FLUSH, fsync's DUR-1, the parked gate's cold flush) takes `drainInPlaceLocked` for a `.full` entry whose complement is still the device's (`seed == .deferred`, not content-valid) over a block W1's state half passes for (`beginInPlace`: passthrough, undecorated whole-block mapping, `refcount == 1 ∧ !SHARED` through the fence pair, no overlay record, no epoch binding): the covered runs widened to pages, each partial page's other bytes read back from the mapping into the cell (≤ 2 pages per run, `seed ≤ written`), ONE DMA per contiguous page run from the cell, the W1 postlude (re-stabilize, purge the four tiers, the touched stamp), the entry retired. Declined (`drain_inplace_declines`) ⇒ the whole-block upload as before | The stream-adjacent append skips the W2 page overlay (arm F's `!stream_adjacent`) and parks a deferred full buffer whose drain seeded the complement with ONE whole-block read and uploaded the whole block to a fresh destination (+ a map merge, a rewrite-epoch shadow, a free): `B` written + `B` read per drain. The bound now: **device per drain = `pages(u, 1) × P`** (1–2 pages for a line), **`≤ pages(e, a) × P / e` per appended byte** (§J.1's in-place law), the `Δ(size)` journal entry per extending write unchanged (§J.2). The crash law is W1's: an edge page's untouched bytes are rewritten with themselves, so a kill at any grain leaves every old byte intact and each appended byte old or new (the crash row injects the torn DMA at every grain); the cell's recycled bytes never reach the device; a failed seed or DMA leaves the entry parked, loud |
| AK.2 A stream-adjacent small piece continues a page overlay over a mapped base | Arm F admits a stream-adjacent small piece when the block's entry is already a W2 overlay deferred over a mapped base (`continues_overlay`): it merges (no I/O at the write) instead of escalating that overlay | A held-open appender's first write after a mount is not stream-adjacent (the per-ino word is 0) and parks a page overlay; every later append is, and escalated it — ONE whole-block seed read into a content-valid full buffer — so the close's single drain uploaded the whole block (the fork appender's control arm: 4.2 MB for 144 KB). Merged, the drain folds the touched pages in place (§J.1 landing 2); the RAM trigger (`B/4P` pages) folds a long stream mid-way and the next append parks a deferred full buffer (AK.1). A hole overlay still escalates to zeros and lands once (write-before-publish); a CoW base still seeds whole |
| AK.3 The append-per-drain bound and its harness verdict | `append_drain_bound` (brk.py, data-append rows): `device ≤ (pages(bs, a) × P / bs + 1) × appended + files × P` — the touched pages per drain, plus each block's ONE whole landing at its first drain (write-before-publish: ≤ one block per block of appended bytes), plus the promotion's own-block DMA of the inline image (≤ a page per file); the row `append-close-1k` (`appends.py`'s open-write-close mode, a set without staging) carries it. `drain_inplace_write_bytes` is a device-write byte arm (brk.py `DEVICE_WRITE_ARMS` = bench.zig `device_write_keys`, rail-pinned); `drain_inplace_dmas` is read BESIDE the request arms | The closure: Σ device-write arms ≡ OST diskstats. The drain's page DMAs ride the block layer's put (`put_obj` counts each — the e2e row pins `put_obj + 1` per drain), so summing `drain_inplace_dmas` into the request ledger would count them twice |
| AK.4 A transformed tail lands its image whole, trimmed to its LIVE bytes | A transformed block cannot be patched in place (a frame rewrite would reuse its nonce — `base counter + frame index` — and the image is self-delimiting), so its drain lands the image whole (CoW); the upload transforms `liveLen(block)` = through its last non-zero byte (a short last frame is the format's own shape). **Per drain `≤ storedImageBound(r) = alignUp(table + r + ⌈r/F⌉ × τ, P)`** — the 64 KiB frame grain prices only the tags (one per frame) and the per-frame crypto/compression: 8,192 B for a 7,880 B file on aes-256-gcm, ≈ the compressed live bytes on lz4/zstd (8 KiB for text). **Per appended byte `storedImageBound(r) / u`** — the CoW price grows with the block's live extent `r` (≤ `B`), stated: a transformed tail has no in-place arm | The untrimmed image transformed the whole block: an encrypted-only volume stored every zero frame raw (`F + τ` each), ≈ 4.2 MB per drain whatever `r`; a compressed volume paid ~270 B per zero frame. A frame-grain trim (the first cut) still stored the live frame's zero tail: 69,632 B per drain for a file under 64 KiB. Every byte past `r` reads back as zeros (AK.5) |
| AK.5 A transformed set's writes and reads past a short image work | The write side's seeds (the seeded checkout's cell, the flush's complement — BLOCK-sized buffers) read through `readMappingPlain`: the whole-block window (the chunk, `B + P`) lands in ONE pooled scratch and decodes straight into the buffer; the codec takes any destination the image's plaintext fits. `frameSpan` ends at `plain_len` (an empty span names no frame; the ranged read zero-fills past `plain_end`) and the whole-block fill zero-completes a short decode to the block | The image seam read the chunk-long window INTO the block-sized buffer (`BufferTooSmall` → EIO): every write into a mapped transformed block of a set without a cache path answered EIO — every `>>` past 4 KiB. A short image (a promoted sub-block image; now every trimmed tail) refused a ranged read past its plaintext (EIO) and its decoded short block panicked a whole-block-walk serve past it (`index 245760, len 65536`; the read LRU cached it) |
| AK.6 The device-write ledger counts the bytes each DMA landed | `write_through_bytes` adds `proof.len` — the stored image's grain-padded length — where it added `B` per upload; `write_pipeline_superseded_bytes` adds the abandoned DMA's `proof.len` (it added `B`) and is a device-write arm (brk.py `DEVICE_WRITE_ARMS` = bench.zig `device_write_keys`); `own_block_dma_bytes` adds the promoted image's padded stored length (it added the plaintext) | On a transformed set the ledger counted the plaintext block for an 8 KiB image, and a superseded upload's DMA landed an image no arm named (the aes close rows: 1.3–8.7 MB of diskstats outside every arm, 19–36 supersessions): Σ arms never closed against diskstats. Passthrough publishes are unchanged (`proof.len = B`) |
| AK.7 `.stats` is ONE document a strict parser accepts | No key repeats inside its object: the counter `meta_kv_appender_fence_breach` that counted the same event as the must-stay-0 tripwire of the same site is gone (the tripwire's count is the key); a budget component name registered more than once (`block_alloc_table` per data volume, any per-member cache) renders as ONE entry — `instances` and current / floor / weight / sheds / trims summed. The render test parses the whole document strictly and names every repeated key by path (`stats.duplicateKeys`); every smoke read of `.stats` (`Rig`/`Peer` `stats`, `statsTry`, the virtual inode row) parses strictly — `use_first` is gone | A strict parser refused the document (`DuplicateField`); the smoke tolerated it with `use_first`, brk.py's basename lookup took the first. A key exists once in its owning family, so every tool reads the same value |

## AL. A write past EOF never exposes a recycled block's bytes: the extension law (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-append-drain.md`)

The append-drain wave's "found, not fixed": a mapped block's device bytes past EOF are not the
file's. A sub-block landing — the inline, staged or packed image a promotion lands on its own block
(`plane.ownBlockStored` DMAs the image's grain) — leaves the tail the block's previous owner wrote:
the block-device free elides its discard (`reclaim.zig`: discard is substrate hygiene) and
write-before-publish guards only the bytes the DMA covered. A write past EOF then exposed that
tail across `[EOF, write)` on every arm: the W2 fold and the §AK drain land only their touched
pages, the overlay seeds its gaps and the CoW upload its complement from the old block. Symbols as
§AK; `e` the EOF, `s` the new size. The kill case of an extending write (AL.5) and the cross-daemon size change (AL.6) are the same wave.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AL.1 The extension law | Growing a file from `e` to `s` — a write past EOF, a striped truncate-up — first writes `[e, min(s, the EOF block's end))` of a MAPPED EOF block as the write path's own zeros (`WritePath.zeroExtension`, the one entry both growers call), admitted through the ladder like any byte; a hole's EOF block and a block-aligned `e` write nothing | Every arm's existing law then covers the gap: the W2 fold and the §AK drain land its pages in place, the overlay and the CoW upload carry it in the block, the write side's view serves it from custody. Zeroing the tail at the mint costs `B − P` per promoted small file (the whole-block amplification §AK removed); bounding every seed by EOF (the reference's `ActiveBlockBuf::seeded`) never reaches the gap pages the in-place arms leave unwritten; the base is the ACCEPTED size — the durable size or an earlier extension's parked claim (AL.5), the mark that never lowers before that claim commits — so a later gap never zeroes an earlier write's bytes (an extending write holds the inode's exclusive op guard from admission through its postlude on the FUSE and IPC paths: `lifecycle.zig`, `ipc/host/service.zig`) |
| AL.2 Its cost | Zeros ≤ `min(s − e, B − e mod B)` per extension, landed with the write that continues them: in place `pages(e, s − e) × P` (the §AK drain of the extension), a CoW landing once with its block | The gap is bytes the user named (a sparse extension inside the block); an adjacent append (`offset = e`) opens no gap and pays nothing; never a second landing |
| AL.3 A truncate-up is crash-safe | setattr's striped grow writes the zeros BEFORE its custody flush, so they land before the size commits: a kill leaves the old size or the zeros | The flush already precedes the size tx (the custody lands before the clip) |
| AL.4 The ledger | `write_gap_zero_bytes` counts the zeros: admission ≡ WRITE payload + these (`stats.closure`, the smoke's ledger rows; until AL.7 the shrink's own zero tail was a third term) | Bytes the filesystem itself admitted |
| AL.7 A shrink writes nothing (2026-10-22) | A striped truncate-down clips the map and commits the size; it does NOT zero the kept block's bytes past the new EOF. The tail is AL.1's: a mapped EOF block's bytes past EOF are never the file's, and the next grow — a truncate-up, a write past EOF — lands them as zeros before the size moves. `fuse_truncate_zero_tail_bytes` and `kv_fs.zeroTailAfterShrink` are gone | The shrink's write was redundant after AL.1 (the grow re-zeroed the same bytes) and the one truncate step that could FAIL for a plane reason: generic/475 (fsstress under dm-error on a set the own-block arm had filled — 2,046/2,046 blocks referenced on both volumes) logged 1,549 `the truncate's tail zero-complete failed (NoSpaceLeft) — bytes past the new end may read back after a grow` error lines, a claim AL.1 had already made false. A shrink now has no device write to refuse (ENOSPC, EIO) and no obligation of its own to record: the obligation is the invariant every grow pays. The owed AL.5 kill window of an extending WRITE over a mapped EOF block now reads the file's own old bytes after a shrink where it read zeros before — the same owed window, never another file's bytes |
| AL.5 THE ORDERED LAW — a size never becomes durable before the bytes it makes readable | An extending striped write parks its size CLAIM with its stamp (`PendingTimes.parkClaim`; fold-visible to every attr reply, the read's EOF and the next write's base — never to a record RMW, so a chmod or a link cannot journal it early). The times drain journals `min(claim, custody floor)` — the floor is the lowest byte the ino's RAM custody covers (`WritePath.custodyFloor`: a parked block's lowest covered byte under its (3), an open overlay's block start, 0 while a rewrite epoch is open) — behind ONE data barrier over the claimants' touched namespaces (`fsync.barrierInos`); a clipped claim stays parked. fsync lands the data, journals the claim, then the meta barrier — never less than the claim it found at its entry: its data leg lands EVERY block parked before it began (`accumulate.flushBornBy`, past any one census buffer), so custody a concurrent write opens under the claim afterwards (an overlay record, a rewrite epoch: the floor drops) never clips an fsynced size (the close posture's drain likewise); syncfs, the dismount and the handover's step 0 land a claim's custody before draining it; the write-back timer's pass wakes the drain once it landed a claimant's custody (§AJ.4); the close posture's drained FLUSH/RELEASE journals the claim after its landing, and the FLUSH's try-only probe declines while a claim is parked; strict mode lands and barriers before it commits; a foreign-slot claim ships after its custody lands. THE ONE CRASH LAW (with §AJ.4 — the write-back timer's "whole or zeros" read over a hole; over a mapped EOF block the unlanded bytes are the block's old tail, so the law is stated on the size): after a kill every fsynced byte is present and the file ends at its last DURABLE size, every byte below it the file's own (written, or the extension law's zeros); an unsynced acknowledged extending write is whole or absent, an overwrite's byte old or new — never another file's bytes | Chosen over clipping the recovered size at replay: an in-place landing journals nothing (§AK), so replay has no durable extent to clip to — the claim's own commit IS that record. Cost: the size lags its acceptance by ≤ `T_wb + τ` (the custody's landing bound, §AJ.1, plus one drain cadence); ≤ 1 journal entry per claimant per drain pass (an appender's per-write Δ becomes one per pass); one data barrier per drain pass that journals a claim; fsync after an extension pays one more meta barrier (the claim's) |
| AL.6 A cross-daemon size change runs the holder's size path | record_ship's served `setattr` with a size calls the filesystem's truncate face (`kv_fs.servedTruncate`: the local setattr — the custody settle, the map clip, the grow's extension zeros landed before the size) and REJECTS it unarmed; the non-holder lands its own custody and runs its barrier before shipping the size | The holder applied the size to the inode alone: a shrink left the bytes past the new EOF to read back after a grow, a grow exposed the EOF block's tail |
| AL.5 Owed: an extending WRITE's bytes after a kill | Not fixed here. The extension's size Δ is a landed journal entry while its bytes and the gap's zeros are daemon memory until the drain, so a kill between leaves `[e, s)` reading the mapped block's device bytes — the recycled tail, not zeros (§AJ.4's "whole or zeros" holds over a hole, not over a mapped EOF block with a sub-block landing) | The fix is a durability-law change, sized here: either the size claim of an extension over a mapped EOF block parks (fold-visible, §L.6's mechanism) and commits after its bytes land, or a per-(ino, EOF block) zero watermark lands a geometric zero run in place before the size commit crosses it (≤ 2× the reach in zeros, ≤ `log₂(B/P)` synchronous DMAs per block per mount; a CoW block needs its own arm) |
| AL.6 Owed: a foreign-slot truncate | Not fixed here. A non-holder's setattr ships the size (`record_ship` `.setattr`) and the holder applies it to the inode alone — no map clip, no shrink zero tail, no extension zeros — so a foreign-slot truncate-up over a mapped EOF block reads its tail | The holder's verb needs the local setattr's data half (the clip, the zero tail, `zeroExtension` before the size), a wire-verb change outside this defect |

## AN. A daemon whose bring-up or teardown fails ends its own connection before it unmounts (2026-10-15; `docs/evidence/rung-10-kamrui-failstop.md`)

Seen on kamrui (7.2.6-sqz with 0027/0028): a host-wide `sync` (Ubuntu 26.04's uutils `sync FILE` is one) met a daemon failing its bring-up after the INIT reply. The sync held the superblock's `s_umount` for read in `fuse_sync_fs`; its FUSE_SYNCFS waited in `fuse_get_req` (`fuse_block_alloc`: `fc->io_uring && !fuse_uring_ready(fc)` — until every queue registers, a request is never allocated). The daemon's `umount2(MNT_DETACH)` dropped the mount's last reference in its own task: `cleanup_mnt` → `deactivate_super` → `s_umount` for write. 16 minutes, until a manual fusectl `abort`. §Z.1's request timeout cannot help: it times requests on the kernel's queues, and this one was never allocated (and a failure before the reply negotiates none).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AN.1 The fail-stop order | A failure path ends its own FUSE connection BEFORE any step that can wait — a worker join, the filesystem's `destroy`, an `umount2`, `fusermount3 -u`: the bring-up after `mount(2)` (every errdefer that can wait calls `failStartup` first: the test hold, then `failStop`), the lazy detach (`unmountDetached` calls `failStop` itself), and the orderly teardown whose drain missed its deadline (`awaitDrains` false). `failStop` writes fusectl's `abort` for the connection id (the mount's minor from `/proc/self/mountinfo`, never a stat — a GETATTR to ourselves); without fusectl, root's detach carries `MNT_FORCE` (`umount_begin` = `fuse_abort_conn` runs before the last reference drops; EPERM detaches plainly), and an unprivileged bring-up failure closes `/dev/fuse` before `fusermount3 -u -z` (the workers and the sideband are gone: our fd is the last device reference) | `fuse_abort_conn` clears `connected` and wakes `blocked_waitq`: the parked FUSE_SYNCFS answers ENOTCONN, the sync releases `s_umount`, the detach's `deactivate_super` proceeds. Ending the connection is the one act that frees every waiter at once; the order is the whole fix (the old code aborted too — after the detach that could not return). Red on kamrui: the daemon's main thread in `deactivate_super` from `task_work_run`, the sync held 15 s with `waiting` 1; green: the sync returns 20 ms after the failure path runs |
| AN.2 Our own end is no kernel verdict | The sideband's disconnect after `failStop` (`self_aborted`) leaves `mounted` set and `lost_while_mounted` clear: the mount is still ours to unmount, and the daemon detaches it | §Z.1's fail-stop is the KERNEL's (a request outlived the lattice): that mount stays in the table, the failure visible. A failing daemon's own end is followed by its own unmount; were `mounted` cleared, `unmount` would skip and leave an ENOTCONN mount behind |
| AN.3 After the arm, the orderly teardown keeps its order | Once every queue registered, `close` drains (every owed slot answered), unmounts, joins; only a drain that missed `DRAIN_WAIT_NS` fail-stops first | An armed session answers every request a waiter can hold `s_umount` around: served, synthesized by the drain, or ENOTCONN in the 30 s residency, and §Z.1's timeout bounds the rest. A group that did not drain may still own such a request: the plain `umount2` would wait on it |
| AN.4 `Session.mount` owns fusermount3's fd and its mount on every path | Before the session holds the fd, a failure closes it (the connection's only reference: its close ends it) and only then runs `fusermount3 -u -z`; the CLI no longer unmounts or closes after a failed `Session.mount` | The CLI ran `fusermount3 -u` (blocking, before any abort) after the session had already detached, and closed the fd a second time — a number another thread may have reused |
| AN.5 Rejected | A bound around `umount2`; a `kill` of the waiter; skipping the unmount on failure | The waits are uninterruptible (`down_write`, a non-killable sync caller); a bounded wait leaves the deadlock behind the daemon's exit; an un-detached mount of a dead daemon is the visible failure §Z keeps for the kernel's own verdict, not for a startup refusal |
| AN.6 A daemon ends only the connection it owns, and the id proves ownership only while its own mount holds the superblock (2026-10-23) | Every fusectl `abort` the daemon writes names the minor the table gave at ITS `mount(2)` (`mount_dev`, never a later lookup by path) and is written only while its own device fd still answers connected (`connectionAlive`: `poll` → no POLLERR; `fuse_dev_poll` reports `!fiq->connected` without a request to ourselves). The held-elsewhere verdict (§S: another mount of the superblock — a bind, a move, a propagated copy — would keep the connection alive past our unmount, so end it now and loud) is read from the table and acted on BEFORE the daemon's own `umount2`, counting the entries with its `major:minor` other than its own (`mountinfo.othersOfSuperblock`: its id at its path) | The anonymous device a FUSE superblock takes is freed inside the `umount2` that drops its last reference and reissued at once to whoever mounts next, anywhere on the host (the lowest free number; tmpfs and fuse draw from one pool). Gate 94 on `fix/suites-symmetric-posture`: the verdict read AFTER the unmount found the daemon's number on shard 8/16's fresh mount at another path and aborted it inside its INIT (`the INIT reply write returned -2`, `InitReplyFailed`); the smoke row "A DAEMON ENDS ONLY THE CONNECTION IT OWNS" pins it with the number reissued deterministically (`SQUEEZEFS_TEST_FUSE_UNMOUNT_HOLD`, every lower number filled with a tmpfs). While its own mount is attached the superblock cannot die, so the number is its own; the fd probe closes the one window left (an external `umount(8)` racing the stop) |

## AO. A tree this mount maintains reaches only allocated extents: the writer mount's claim adoption (2026-10-15; `docs/evidence/rung-10-routing-loop.md`)

The `fsck --offline` refusal after the write-back timer's kill (`tree 0 … { 0, 256, 0, 0, 0 }`) was a
named guest root recovered FREE and overwritten by the mount's first claim. A claim made in RAM with
no journaled delta is named before the C6 bitmap page that makes it durable; the mount reconciles.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AO.1 Every extent a maintained tree reaches is allocated before the mount's first claim | The writer open (`openGuestTrees`, after the named roots open and before its re-mints) walks every opened tree it maintains (`collectTreeExtents`: interior nodes read, leaves named by their parents) and adopts each reachable extent the recovered allocator holds free (`adoptLive`: bit set, page dirty, `meta_kv_replay_root_claims_adopted`, a warn line per tree). A projection open (`-o ro`, a joiner) adopts nothing: it claims nothing and its allocator is not the bitmap | A guest root minted on first touch (one extent, no delta) is named at once — appender 0's page refresh at C4b, C11's page plan, a tree-0 record — and a builder-built tree (`copySlotTree`) claims every node in RAM, its root last, while C6 writes pages in page order and a release mid-build drops the lowest-first cursor below its leaves: a kill between the naming and the page, or inside C6, recovered reachable extents free (the mid-C4b and mid-C6 cuts). Checking only the root's bit missed the builder's leaves. Recovery reconciles the allocator with the roots it names, as the dropped root free does (`meta_kv_replay_root_frees_dropped`); ordering every naming after its page instead would hold a fresh tree's records in the window by another floor, and a journaled claim at mint would leak the extent when the crash precedes the naming |
| AO.2 A lessee's tree is a projection: never walked; its custodian is its grant | A tree tree 0 names `leased` / `releasing` to another appender (`slot_state.GuestRoot.lessee`) is skipped by AO.1's walk. Its extents need no adoption: a lessee's nodes sit in its grant runs, whose bits ride the grant's entry (alloc frames beside the record), and the handover's `cycleOnce` lands the holder's claims on its bitmap before the grant's entry moves the tree's images into the grantee's record (T1, §AD.1); a dead lessee's tree is the recovery's to install and walk under the SMO lock (§U.1) | §U.1's law: its interior may name extents the lessee recycled — a walk from tree 0's grant-time root could refuse the mount on a level mismatch or adopt foreign garbage. The live-lessee row pins both halves: the mount walks the maintained trees only, and B's pooled image (A's RAM claim moved to B) stays allocated on A's reopened bitmap |
| AO.3 The walk's cost is one read per interior node | Measured on kamrui (x86_64, NVMe): 1,000,000 files in 8 directories, 66 trees, 1,026 extents reachable — 0.65 ms per writer mount, clean or after a kill (`meta_kv_mount_reach_walk_extents` / `_ns`); the interior share is < 1 % of the images at the shipped fan-out | Every writer mount walks (no cheaper sound trigger: a page refresh names a root without a journal entry, so an empty window does not prove the bitmap whole) |

## AM. A served verb never parks holding the session's serving capacity; the lease gauge is one exact sum (2026-10-14; `docs/evidence/rung-10-serving-lanes.md`)

The move matrix's offered leg once parked B's unlink 218 s and answered `HolderUnresolved`
(`rung-10-gate-blockers-2.md` §7); A's `slot_leases_held` read −164 wrapped after the rejoin row.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AM.1 A session serves on lanes, never one frame at a time | The listener's connection runs leader/follower lanes: one lane holds the reader role, copies the call it read, hands the role on (to an idle lane, else a new one) and serves on its own buffers; replies are sealed and written under the session's send lock. At most `SERVE_LANES_MAX` = the dialer's `MUX_IN_FLIGHT` lanes per session, spawned only as concurrency asks for them; an idle read timeout ends the session only while no serve is in flight | The dialer multiplexes up to `MUX_IN_FLIGHT` calls of every thread of a daemon onto ONE session per peer, and the listener served them one at a time — so any serve that parks (an `AcquireSlot` accept running the whole transfer: the step-0 times drain's 4a lock, the door's unbounded drain, the checkpoint cycles under a loaded device) held every other call of that peer behind it: the member's renewals, its re-resolutions, its own first touch. A member whose renewals wait behind a serve that waits on the member is a head-of-line cycle the reply bound only turns into retries (§Q's "the served acquisition never parks the session" law, which made the guard ask take the TRY form, covered one verb; the lanes cover every verb). Serving capacity = the peer's in-flight capacity is the derivation: no call can wait behind another's serve, and the cost is bounded by what the peer can have in flight. Bounding every park below the reply bound was rejected (a queue of K bounded parks still sums past it); per-class lanes were rejected (the accept and the unlink's own first touch are one class) |
| AM.2 `slot_leases_held` is SET from the planes' counts | Each plane folds `own leases − its last share` into the process-wide gauge at every lease change it makes (arm, install, release, cutover) and takes its share out at deinit; a plane's share is the leases its OWN regions hold (`own_ids`) — a wire lessee's are its own process's | The gauge added the table's whole count at the manager's arm, nothing for a wire grant at the manager, one per release of ANY holder's slot, and a joiner's close subtracted its table's count including the foreign views it had loaded: two event classes disagreed and it wrapped. A share re-read from the authoritative count cannot drift; owning-process counting sums the fleet's leases exactly once |
## AP. The write side's custody tables follow the custody budget; a full table hands the writer to the parked gate, never a tripwire (2026-10-15; `docs/evidence/rung-10-storm-custody.md`)

The write-back storm (16 writers, `SQUEEZEFS_PARKED_BUFFERS=8`) fired two must-stay-0 tripwires on
every run: the device overlay registry, sized verbatim by the parked cap, met 16 writers' heads
full (`overlay_registry_full`); and W2 page overlays held every active-block table entry without a
pool cell, so a full checkout holding a fresh cell found no entry (`active_block_table_full`). Both
tables were sized as if one kind of custody filled them.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AP.1 The overlay registry's records | `max(parked_cap, ⌈capBytes(budget) ÷ B⌉)` (`device_overlay.registryCapacity`): 256 at a 4 GiB budget, 4,096 at 64 GiB, 256 for the storm's explicit 8 at 4 GiB | An open record is write custody the timer lands — at most one block (its gap seed writes the complement), the parked buffer's unit and drain cost — so the parked cap is its floor; every overlay store in flight holds a record, so the write pipeline's byte cap in blocks keeps the governor, not the registry, the bound on a streaming writer. A record holds no payload (a RAM struct and its reserved destination). The reference's registry was an unbounded map (0002 row 539) |
| AP.2 A fresh install reserves its record before it mints | `Registry.reserve` under the registry's lock (`reserved ≤ free`), spent by the install; no room ⇒ the piece declines to the accumulation arm with nothing minted (`overlay_registry_declines`) — there the parked-buffer gate parks the writer at the pool cap. `overlay_registry_full` (must-stay-0) now asserts the reservation law: an install holding a reservation found no record | The install minted its destination before it looked for a record, then abandoned it and tripped: a full registry is a capacity state, not an impossible one. The decline is never a refusal — the next arm acks the piece — and the parked gate is the one backpressure station (its cold flush is the price an overlay settle would pay: the partial block's gaps) |
| AP.3 The active-block table holds the parked buffers AND the extents beside them | Entries = `activeBlockEntries(cap) = cap + extentEntryCap(cap)`, `extentEntryCap(cap) = cap`; a new W2 extent is admitted only below its cap (`Table.extents`, raised under the table lock, lowered by an escalation after its cell is taken and by a retire); a first small piece past it takes arm G's full buffer (`extent_cap_fallbacks`), the gate enforcing the pool cap. `active_block_table_full` is unreachable by construction: a full checkout holding a cell sees at most `cap − 1` other full entries (each holds its own cell) and `cap` extents | An extent holds at most a quarter block of page RAM before it folds (`foldPagesDerived`), so `cap` extents hold ≤ `cap × B/4` — a quarter of the parked set's RAM, charged as `parked_extents`. The parked RAM bound is unchanged (`parked_full_buffer_bytes ≤ cap × B`); the `parked_buffers` gauge counts both kinds (≤ 2 cap) |
| AP.4 §AJ.1's `C` | `C` ≤ the parked buffers × B + `parked_extents` + the governor's in-flight permits + the open overlay records × B (≤ `registryCapacity` × B) | Each open record's drain lands at most one block, like a parked buffer's |
| AP.5 Rejected | A registry gate of its own (settle the coldest record to make room); a registry sized only by the parked cap with the tripwire removed | A settle of a partial record lands its gaps — the parked gate's cold-flush price, which the decline already reaches without a second wake protocol; and a tripwire on a reachable capacity state is noise that hides the impossible one it should guard |

## AQ. A late shipped free meets the successor's derivation, never the untracked refusal; the term's moves supersede the derivation (2026-10-15; `docs/evidence/rung-10-kvda-late-free.md`)

The KVDA page sink's fallback row fired the must-stay-0 `block_untracked_free_refusals` once in a
kamrui gate. A writer's terminal free sits in its shipper's batch (consumed, unpublished as a free,
unanswered) or on the wire across the holder's death: it is in neither the grant window nor the
in-flight registrations, so no renewal declaration names it, and a same-node successor whose
derivation (the refs census, the predecessor's pages, the kept window, the live declarations) never
carried it reads the block nobody's — bit CLEAR. The batch then flushes to the successor, which
had no answer for a free of a block it never tracked but the double-release refusal. The pages-held
twin: a deferred candidate (carried by the pages, declared by no one) a late free cleared and the
successor re-carved is RELEASED by the sole writer's exact census — a live grant's bit cleared.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AQ.1 The term's moves | The holder's bitmap keeps one bit per block, under its ordering lock, of what THIS term moved since its arm: every carve, re-learn, free CLEAR, return and holding take that flips a bit (`DataBitmap.moved`). The arm's census, page load and kept-window fold are the derivation, not moves; the arm SEALS the derivation as its last step (`sealDerivation` — a re-hold's replay runs through the journaled set/clear); a trim window's transient take moves nothing | The derivation speaks for a block only until the term touches it: from then on the term's own moves are the record. One bit per block (the bitmap's own size) and a store under the lock every mutation already holds |
| AQ.2 The successor answers a late free of a block its derivation returned | On a holder that SUCCEEDED a predecessor (a later lease term, or its own replayed one — `term > 1 ∨ already`), a shipped free of an untracked, unreferenced block whose bit is CLEAR (or trim-windowed) and UNMOVED since the arm is answered `Freed` with no bit flipped (`free_derivation_settled`): the writer's word retires, and the settle is the term's act on the block — it is marked moved, so a declaration built before the answer (naming the block as an in-flight registration the writer then abandoned) never re-learns it and a second free of it is the double release. A CLEAR bit the term moved stays the double-release lineage (row 6, `block_untracked_free_refusals`, must-stay-0); the volume's first holder carved every block a writer can free, so it keeps the refusal whole (no probe) | The derivation already made the block nobody's — the free is the writer's last word on a custody the successor never saw, not a second release. Nothing moves, so no answer here can free a block anyone holds; a genuine double ship still meets the writer's own allocator at its retire (its `beginFree` on an untracked word) |
| AQ.3 A declaration and the census never undo the term's moves | A declared block the term CLEARED (a free answered, a return, a release) while the word was in flight is a stale word's — never re-learnt (`block_grant_stale_declared_blocks`); a deferred candidate the term moved before the exact census is never released (`data_alloc_bitmap_leaks_settled`; closure `deferred ≡ released + adopted + referenced + settled + pending`) | The re-learn and the release were the derivation's tools; applied to a block the term already moved they leak a block the holder just freed (the declaration in flight across the free's answer) or clear a live grant (a candidate freed late and re-carved) |
| AQ.4 Rejected | (a) Declaring the shipper's batch as in-flight (`heldBlockRanges`); (b) a separate pending-free declaration class; (c) screening the free's `lease_epoch` | (a) Without AQ.3 the fold re-learns a CLEAR declared block for the holder's lifetime (a free answered while the word was in flight would be SET again and leak), and it would enter the writer's ledger slots, which its leave clears. (b) The flush cadence and the renewal beat are independent: a free can reach the successor before the writer's first declaration, so the successor must answer the block it never saw without the class anyway. (c) The writer knows no successor term on this wave (`WriterPlane.leaseEpoch` answers term 1), and an epoch stamped at enqueue is not what the successor's derivation saw (a block batched after the writer learnt the new term but before its first declaration is still unknown there); the unmoved bit is the fact the epoch would approximate |

## AR. A dead ring submits nothing; RingFailed on a run the kernel took is an indeterminate EIO; an op the kernel took stays owned until its CQE (2026-10-15; `docs/evidence/rung-10-deadring.md`, `docs/evidence/rung-10-ringdeadread.md`, `docs/evidence/rung-10-uringfsdead.md`)

Seen on kamrui (`baf17ef`): replay applied a journal window whose ticket had answered `RingFailed` and whose entries the lane had rolled back. The exec lane's pre-park `flush` entered a ring whose owner had already declared it dead.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AR.1 The dead ring's SQ | `ParkedRing.flush` enters nothing once the ring is dead. An SQE prepared before the verdict, or left in the SQ by a failed enter, never reaches the kernel | Every owner fails its prepared ops at the verdict (`failAll`, `onRingDead`) and releases their buffers. A later enter would run ops that were already answered, against memory that was already freed |
| AR.2 A journal run the kernel already took | Its owner still answers RingFailed at once. The EIO is indeterminate: the entry may stand on the device and replay at the next mount. It was never acked, and the volume is FAILED. Its late CQE is dropped by the lane | An enter failure does not cancel ops already in the kernel. A WRITE or FSYNC only READS memory, so a released bounce at worst puts garbage in an un-acked window, which replay's checksum drops. The ops that WRITE memory are AR.4's |
| AR.3 Rejected | Holding the ticket until the ring's teardown reaps the run | The teardown happens at unmount. Clients of a FAILED volume would hang instead of getting EIO |
| AR.4 An op the kernel took that writes memory | It stays OWNED (its buffer, its cell or slot, its waiter) until its CQE, and is answered RingFailed then. This covers an exec fiber's ring op (READV, POLL_ADD, ...; the fiber stays parked) and an NVMe request (`readInto` DMAs into the caller's buffer, `readBlock` into a pool slot). At the verdict `Parked.untaken` finds the SQEs the kernel never took, between its SQ head and the owner's tail; those are answered at once (AR.1). The dead ring keeps reaping on the owner's backstop tick through `Parked.enterDead`: an enter with `to_submit = 0`, GETEVENTS and no wait, which submits nothing and runs the completions `DEFER_TASKRUN` defers, plus the CQ memory. A cancel request on a dead ring goes through `IORING_REGISTER_SYNC_CANCEL`, a register and not an SQE | An enter failure cancels nothing in the kernel. The kernel writes the buffer whenever the op completes (an io-wq copy, a device DMA, task work), and by then the woken fiber's caller or the next request owns that memory. The wait is the one the op had on a live ring: device I/O completes or the block layer times it out, and a pollable READ ends on data or on its fiber's cancel |
| AR.5 An op whose CQE never comes | It stays held for the owner's life, and nothing reuses it: the fiber is never resumed, and the cell and buffer are never returned. `exec_ring_dead_held_ops`, `dev_ring_dead_held_ops` and `uring_fs_ring_dead_held_ops` gauge the held ops; each falls at its CQE. No timer gives up | The buffer's owner (the fiber's caller, `readInto`'s caller) cannot be answered without handing back memory the kernel may still write, so a timeout could only move a counter. The volume is FAILED: a leak is safe, a reuse is not |
| AR.6 Safe by teardown order, so no hold is needed | The parked ring's own wake READ, because `wake_buf` is freed after the ring's close. The transport's SQE128 ring, because its headers, payload arena and kmbuf are freed in `Group.deinit` after the ring's close, and FUSE's copies into them run as task work that a closing ring terminates. The zcrx lane, because its refill region is unmapped after its ring | The close kills the ring's context before any of that memory goes back. Task work of a dying context completes canceled, without a copy |
| AR.7 Rejected | (a) Answering at once and quarantining the buffer; (b) a SYNC_CANCEL of every held op at the verdict. (The uring-fs pool and the sideband, open here when AR.4 landed, are AR.8 and AR.9) | (a) The buffer is often the caller's (a fiber's readv target, `readInto`'s destination) and cannot be swapped out. (b) A device op cannot be cancelled anyway. A pollable op keeps the semantics it had on a live ring: it completes, or its fiber's own cancel request ends it |
| AR.8 The uring-fs pool (`io/uring_fs.zig`) | `Reactor.failAll` answers only the slots whose SQE the kernel never took (`ring_park.untakenIn`, the body `Parked.untaken` calls). Every slot the kernel took (READ, WRITE, FSYNC) keeps its buffer, its file reference and its request cell. The dead worker (`drainDead`) answers every later request with RingFailed. It reaps on the dead ring's tick (`wake_unarmed_backstop_ns`) through `ring_park.enterDeadFd`, the body `Parked.enterDead` calls, and each held op answers RingFailed at its CQE. The worker exits once the queue is closed and nothing is held. `uring_fs_ring_dead_held_ops` gauges the held ops | `failAll` used to free a READ's buffer and answer its cell at the verdict, so the kernel's late copy landed in reused heap. Every op the kernel took is held, as on the NVMe lanes: a WRITE that reads a freed bounce puts reused memory on the device. A held cell is never handed back: its caller has no answer yet, so `freeCell` (`pushSpinUnlessFull`) never sees it |
| AR.9 The sideband's `/dev/fuse` READ on a dead ring | No hold: the window AR.7 noted is not real. `Sideband.deinit` frees `buf` only after the join (`assert(self.thread == null)`). The READ belongs to the sideband thread, which arms and submits it. A thread's `do_exit` runs `io_uring_files_cancel` before `exit_mm` wakes the joiner, and that tears down the thread's io-wq (`io_uring_clean_tctx` → `io_wq_put_and_exit`): a blocked worker's read is interrupted by a notify signal, queued work is cancelled, and the exit waits for every worker. A poll-armed READ that fires after the exit queues task work on a dead task. That work falls back to the ring's kworker, where `io_should_terminate_tw` fails the READ without issuing it | The submitter's exit ends the read, not the ring's close. The exit is synchronous and comes before the free. The ring's exit work comes later and only reaps |

## AS. The writer claim is a tree-0 control record; a member's own guard never meets the commit door (2026-10-15; `docs/evidence/rung-10-umountclaim.md`)

Seen on kamrui (perf mounts, 2 MDT + 2 OST): mdt1's claim refresh failed `SlotBusy` on every beat, and the clean dismount could not delete it, in every session.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AS.1 Where the claim lives | Tree 0 (`TREE_CONTROL ‖ tag 15`, `control_records.writerClaimKey`), value = the 88-byte `WriterClaimV1` alone. The mount, the heartbeat, the dismount's delete, `claim clear` and the off-device probe all read and write that key | At local ino 1 the key routed to slot 0, which only member 0 hosts. On an armed set member ≥ 1's door answered `moved` (`SlotBusy`) to every refresh and to the delete. The claim went stale 45 s after the mount under a live writer, which on a PR substrate makes a live holder preemptable, and it outlived the clean unmount. A control record has no slot, so it takes no door token, never migrates and is outside every digested kind (`dynamic_routing.md` Q4 had already ruled this) |
| AS.2 What went with it | The FUSE screen's `isGuardXattr` row (the allowlist already refuses the name), the digest walk's `writer_claim` exclusion, and the ino-1 xattr chain scan | Nothing writes a `writer_claim` xattr any more |
| AS.3 Rejected | `door_bypass` on the claim's transactions | Member 0's native slot can be leased by another appender in the N-daemon posture. A bypassing write would mutate a slot tree whose lease another appender holds |
| AS.4 Found beside it | The migration's bulk copy writes images outside the node cache. An extent an earlier SMO retired and the allocator handed back stayed in `retired`, so the copied tree's open loaded `RetiredExtent`. `NodeCache.unretire` clears the mark as a publish does | The tree-0 claim writes changed the target's allocation pattern, and the migrate crash-table test hit it deterministically |

## AT. A write that parks its stamp holds its file's `I{ino}` guard shared (2026-10-16; `docs/evidence/rung-10-rangelock.md`)

Seen on kamrui (RAM-backed NVMe): on 1M seq write ×4, 51 % of the writes waited on `I{ino}` in the striped write's postlude, 10.8 ms on average, while the exclusive section was held 69 µs on average. On 4k randwrite QD32×4 the section was held 3.3 µs and 11.8 % of the writes waited 790 µs. Every one of these writes was Shared at level 1.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AT.1 The park's section | A §L.6 park (the coalesced cadence, no KILLPRIV) takes `I{ino}` SHARED with no transaction. Under the guard it reads the inode, folds the claim, and parks the stamp and the claim. The strict-cadence and KILLPRIV commits keep the exclusive guard and their tx | Two parks commute: `PendingTimes.parkClaim` takes a max per field under its stripe lock. Every guarded RMW of the parked entry or of the stored record takes the guard exclusive: the times drain, setattr and truncate, absorb, and the commit arms. So a park still never interleaves with them |
| AT.2 Rejected: range-granular admission (a per-file range lock) | Not built | The postlude runs after the write's data has landed, so this guard never ordered data. The plane's per-(inode, block) locks (3) order overlapping writes. Level 1 orders an extending write against truncate, because an extending write is never Shared there. Nothing here needs ranges, and a range structure would be a new lock-free core (a model and its weakening) for semantics shared mode already gives |
| AT.3 Rejected: only shrinking the exclusive section | Not enough | Held 3.3 µs, the guard still cost 790 µs per wait. If handoffs were free, 31 holds ahead would cost about 100 µs. The time is the handoff: a release wakes one waiter, and the lock stays free until that waiter runs, because a fresh taker yields to a queued one (charter §9, bounded barging). A shorter hold leaves the handoff as it is |
| AT.4 Unchanged | The level-1 classes. The cross-daemon tokens: `I{ino}` is local, and a foreign-slot file's claim is still one shipped verb (`shipSizeClaim`) | — |

## AU. A joiner's shipped-free verdict retires only a count it holds (2026-10-16; `docs/evidence/rung-10-matrixwedge.md`)

The reclaimer ships a non-holder's last reference, a SHARED block and an UNTRACKED one (porting spec §4.1). An untracked block reaches a joiner's reclaimer when the holder minted it for a write into the joiner's file and a later served merge displaced it. The joiner's `Freed` step ran its local `begin_free` for every verdict, so that block met row 6 and fired `block_untracked_free_refusals` on a correct free (THE TWO-DAEMON MATRIX, block 129, every run).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AU.1 The tracking steps | `Freed` (retire) and `NonTerminal` (release) touch the joiner's allocator only when it holds a RAM count for the block; `freed_since_word` still counts every `Freed` | The holder's verdict is the authority on the double-release lineage (it runs row 6 with its own state); a missing count here means this mount never counted the block, not a second release |
| AU.2 Rejected: carrying a tracked flag per shipped block | Not built | The count is 0 or 1 at the ship and nothing but this mount's own ladder moves it in between; the flag would widen the batch and the `FreeTarget` seam for no case the read misses |

## AV. The parent's stamp on a create or unlink is a parked refinement (2026-10-20; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path.md`)

`mdtest -np 32 -n 10000 -u` on the RAM substrate: 41 k creates/s against ext4's 711 k. The daemon ran 2.5 cores of 32; the journal lane of the one volume the tree lives on ran 95 % of one core, and 55 % of it was the pass's RAM apply. Every create staged three records, and the third — the parent's Δ(mtime, ctime) merge record (§5.2) — was the one that landed on a leaf shared by every child of the directory: the 32 rank directories' inodes sit in one leaf, 43 k Δs/s arrived in it between two writebacks (mean 230 records at apply, max 4,483), and every apply re-merged the overlay (`remergeStable`, O(overlay) per 8 applies, the array past 64 KiB mmap'd and unmapped with a TLB shootdown across the daemon's threads).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AV.1 The park | Under the coalesced cadence (`times_park`) a create, symlink or unlink of a NON-directory child stages no parent record: the stamp is parked (`PendingTimes.park`, `kv_fs.parkParentTimes`) under the op's SHARED `I{parent}` guard before its entry commits; `economy.ParentRecord.parked_times`; counted `fuse_parent_times_parked` | §L.6's law applied to the parent: the Δ changed only two time fields, it is fold-visible on every read, the batched drain journals it within one flush interval, `fsyncdir` and the dismount drain it ahead of their barriers. A create's entry is two records on fresh, exclusively-guarded keys; the shared leaf sees one `Put` per parent per drain instead of one Δ per create |
| AV.2 What stays | A directory child's nlink shift (the exclusive `Put`), `link` and `rename` (their shapes are the exclusive RMW), the cross-owner intents (the holder's served step stages the Δ), strict mode (`META_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS=0`) | nlink is structural (fsck's census; the kill rows' "clean" would lose it); the other arms are not on a hot path; strict mode's ack means durable |
| AV.3 Durability | A crash inside the interval leaves the parent's `mtime`/`ctime` ≤ 50 ms behind its newest child — ext4's own post-crash shape at its 5 s commit interval; `fsync(parent)` closes it; `fsync(child)` covers the child's entry (the name and the inode), as POSIX scopes it | The same bound §L.6 gives a write's stamp; the entry carrying the name is unchanged |
| AV.4 Coherence | The dentry record keys the parent, so the conveyor's token recall still names the parent object to every reader of the directory; the kernel's `fuse_dir_changed` drops the parent's attrs on its own create | Nothing new to recall |

Measured (this box, the perf mount, `-np 32 -n 10000 -u`, one session each): file creation 43.7 k → 61.9 k/s, file removal 38.2 k → 53.3 k/s; journal bytes per create 225 → 182; the lane's CPU per create 21.8 → 15.1 µs; `fsck --offline` clean.

## AW. The conveyor's RAM apply leaves the journal lane: the pass reserves, an applier applies and submits (2026-10-20; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path.md`)

After §AV the journal lane of the one volume an mdtest tree lives on still ran 93 % of one core at 62 k creates/s: 15 µs per tx, of which the pass's RAM apply (resolve, the union lock, the pre-images, the apply with its O(overlay) re-merge, the encode) was ≈ 11 and the reservation, the submit and the acks the rest. The charter's "both stages on ONE OS thread" made that thread the engine's ceiling whatever the committers' parallelism; the appliers' work is parallel by nature (disjoint leaves) and nothing in the durability law needs it on the ring's thread.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AW.1 The split | The pass (the lane's fiber) drains, recalls tokens, admits the ring, allocates the window, RESERVES and stamps the seqs, tracks the window and hands it to an applier (`ApplySeam.spawn`); the applier — `Conveyor.applyWindow`, a fiber per window — resolves, locks the union, revalidates (re-resolving on a moved leaf: the seqs are the window's already), captures the pre-images, applies in queue order, kicks the threshold, encodes and submits. The portable contracts' applier is inline (the pass's thread: stage A whole, as before) | The reservation ahead of the apply keeps every durability witness: the registry's open entry holds the checkpoint tail back until the lane completes it after the landing, which is after the apply (`journal_core.InflightRegistry`'s law); a failed member still rolls out in-window and leaves an unwritten hole; a refusal before any apply abandons the window as the pass did |
| AW.2 The heap admission moves to the committer | `admitHeapPreQueue`: the committer resolves, locks, runs `heap_law.admitHeapLocked` for its tx alone — the space retry's checkpoint cycles included — and unlocks, BEFORE the enqueue | A refusal after the reservation would be a hole; a checkpoint cycle run behind an open reservation of the committer's own would wait on the completed prefix that waits on it |
| AW.3 The venue and its width | The backend spawns appliers on a FIXED set of handler lanes (`sqz-fuse-ln<i>`, idle while the drain workers run handlers on their own lanes), `applierParallelism(cpus) = clamp(cpus/8, 2, 4)` wide; the pass holds a drained batch while that many windows are in apply (`meta_conveyor_holds_pacing`) | Measured on mdtest -np 32 creates: the committers themselves as appliers (one per window, on the drain workers' own lanes) held their leaves 114 µs per 1.4-entry window — 32 concurrent appliers contending for the rotor slot trees' leaves, every handoff a cross-thread wake — and stalled the workers' FUSE queues; the `sqz-meta` pool (2 lanes) shared with the reclaim's destroy batches halved the unlink rate; a rotation over all 32 handler lanes doubled the apply's CPU (cold caches); four pinned lanes: creates 61.9 k → 68.4 k/s, unlinks 53.3 k → 63.1 k/s, the lane's CPU per create 15 → 6 µs |
| AW.4 Out-of-order applies | Two windows may apply in either order; a record lands at its `(key, seq)` place in the overlay run whatever the order (since the persistent chunked run of wave 2 — `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path.md` — there is no stable/tail split and no re-merge: the former "tail ≥ stable per key" law and its `max_applied_seq` guard are gone) | Two in-flight windows share a key only under a shared guard (a Δ merge); the inversion guard on the heads above an older record stands |
| AW.5 The hold law (§L) | A pad-majority batch holds only with a window ON THE DEVICE (one in apply is the appliers' to overlap with), ends at the NEXT landing (the flight's emptying in the paced regime), and the worth gate's line scales with the batch (`c ≥ n/3`: a quarter of the pad, §L's ½ at its n̄ of 1.56) | With the pass at 3 µs and the window's lifetime 100+ µs the empty-flight wait held the pass — the one serial stage — for the whole pipeline: 62 % of the passes, 141–170 µs each, the closed loop at the hold's pace. §L's probe of the unpaced regime predates §L.6, which parks that shape's stamps |
| AW.6 Residual | The rollback's pre-image of a key two in-flight windows share is each applier's at its own apply; the earlier window failing while the later landed compensates with a value that folds the later's Δ — the post-state without the failed window, which is correct; both failing (two consecutive write failures) is the failure latch's remount | Noted, not built around: `divergedFromReplay` already treats a rollback it cannot make exact as terminal |

What it did not buy yet: the apply's intrinsic cost (15–17 µs of CPU per create on the appliers: `remergeStable` and the snapshot's destroy are O(overlay) per 8 applies on leaves whose overlays reach thousands of records between writebacks, the pre-image lookups, the per-record allocations) and the closed loop's latency (lookup 41 µs + create 375 µs at 32 ranks) bound mdtest at ≈ 68 k creates/s; the lane is no longer the ceiling (`sqz-jrnl` 2 s of a 7 s phase). The path to 200 k and beyond is in the evidence note.

## AX. The client bypass's DIRECT ARM: a trusted client reads the data device itself under a layout lease (2026-10-20; `docs/evidence/rung-10-client-direct.md`)

The bypass measured at 39–54 % of ext4/xfs on 4k random I/O in memory (`rung-10-client-bypass-rows.md`) with a structural cap: client → service thread → the daemon's own `io_uring_enter` → the CQE → `futex_wake` → client, two thread hops and an arena copy around the one syscall an in-kernel filesystem pays. The owner's bar — fastest or on par on every served row — is reachable only by the client issuing the device I/O itself. DAOS's libioil does not do this (its engine does the I/O on dedicated cores); it is the step beyond.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AX.1 Opt-in and the trust boundary | `-o interception=direct` (the mount owner's act). At HELLO the daemon grants the arm iff the peer's uid/gid could open EVERY data device node for reading (`rendezvous.deviceReadable`: root, or the owner/group/other read bit that is theirs — `SessionOk.features & FEATURE_DIRECT`); the client then opens the nodes itself (`O_RDONLY | O_DIRECT`) and the kernel's permission check is the final word. A refusal is counted (`ipc_direct_refused_access`), said once per peer, and leaves a ring session | A direct client reads ANY block of the volume — the trust a FUSE mount's root already holds over the device, now extended to clients the owner names by device permissions. It is for the owner's own HPC clients; the default `-o interception` grants nothing of it |
| AX.2 The layout lease | `LeaseReq{binding}` → `LeaseOk{ino, size, epoch, block_size, entries[block] = {volume ordinal, device offset}}` for a RESIDENT STRIPED PASSTHROUGH file with no write-side custody (else `LeaseRefused{kind | custody | moved | binding | disabled}` and the ring serves; a refused binding backs off 200 ms). The daemon reads the stripe's epoch BEFORE the map and refuses `moved` if it changed after | The map is the KV truth at the lease; everything that could make it wrong afterwards bumps the epoch (AX.3). Holes, tenants, inline and staged files, transformed volumes, multi-block and unaligned requests are the ring's |
| AX.3 The lease epochs | `data/lease_epoch.zig`: one monotonic word per inode stripe (the shape table's stripes and mix) in a sealed memfd page every direct session maps read-only; bumped (a) by the conveyor at every member's terminal outcome for every inode whose record or xattrs it changed — after the RAM apply, before the ack, every origin — and (b) at `op_guard.admitWrite` for the inode.
