The client's recheck after its `pread`: the stripe still reads the lease's epoch, else the lease is dropped and asked again; past two attempts the ring serves | (a) is the FUSE direct leg's "incarnation still ∧ the map still binds" (charter §6.3) made cheap enough for a client: the RAM apply precedes the bump, so a lease built over the new map never carries the old epoch; the ack follows the bump, so a read issued after an acked change sees it. (b) covers RAM custody the device does not hold yet (a parked write): every lease of the inode dies at the write's admission — a mixed read/write workload on one file re-asks per write (owed: bump only when custody is taken). Stripes, not inodes: a collision is a spurious re-ask, never a stale serve |
| AX.4 Death | A direct-only client never parks on a slot, so the heartbeat ladder runs on the direct path: a daemon that stopped stamping (≤ 1 s) poisons the session before any read, and the op falls through to the kernel — loud, since the mount went with its daemon | A dead daemon can no longer move the epochs the leases rest on; a surviving client must not keep serving under them across a remount |
| AX.5 Writes | Not in this wave. The W1 patch of a resident block within size (the lock-free Shared class) can be client-issued the same way — the postlude (the parked stamp, the touched word) asked of the daemon asynchronously — with the law: a client killed mid-write loses nothing acked (its write was not acked), a daemon killed with client writes in flight leaves the device holding only acked bytes (the client fails the op loud by AX.4). The ring's dd inline write serves W1 today | The read arm had to stand first: the lease and its invalidation are the whole mechanism, and the bar's gap is widest on reads |
| AX.6 Rejected: passing the device fds over `SCM_RIGHTS` | Not built | The client's own `open(2)` IS the permission check the owner set on the node; an fd passed by root would bypass it |

## AY. The direct arm's WRITES: a client patches a resident block in place under a RECALLABLE write lease (2026-10-20; `docs/evidence/rung-10-client-direct-writes.md`)

§AX put reads at parity; writes stayed on the ring at 39–51 % of ext4. The W1 patch (`sole_owner_patch.zig`: one aligned DMA into a block the file owns alone, no RMW, no journal entry) is a shape a client can issue itself — if the daemon can promise that nothing of its own shadows the device for that inode while the client writes, and that it never changes the inode's layout under a write in flight.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AY.1 The WRITE lease | `LeaseReq{LEASE_WRITE}` on a `FEATURE_DIRECT_WRITE` session (every data device node WRITABLE by the peer: `rendezvous.deviceWritable`, the same screen as reads with the write bit) for a binding with write rights; granted iff every mapped block passes W1's own state half (`WritePath.probeDirectWriteLease`: custody, the authoritative map, passthrough, whole and undamaged, the durable sole-owner verdict and the fence pair, under a try-locked block guard; the word re-stabilized) — else `LeaseRefused{write}` and the client holds a READ lease (its writes ride the ring; never re-asked per write). The grant takes a slot in the session's LEDGER (`write_lease_core.Words{tag, inflight, recall}` in the client stats page, its one protocol region) with a fresh tag | The W1 eligibility IS the in-place law; the daemon decides it once at the grant and defends it by recall thereafter. A per-session ledger keeps the words client-writable and the daemon's `recall` store on the same cache line the client reads |
| AY.2 Device-true while leased | At the grant the inode's and every block key's stripe is marked (`lease_epoch.noteWriteLease`) and every key is purged from the four read tiers; while marked, `read_path.depositable` refuses the keys (no hot/LRU/hold/NVMe deposit) and `plane.moverQuiescent` answers false (no copy under a client's pen). The lease refuses `custody` (a parked write, a staged resident, an open overlay) | Nothing of the daemon's may answer a read of the inode from anywhere but the device, and nothing may copy a block the client is rewriting |
| AY.3 The recall (the token the daemon takes back) | Every mutation chokepoint recalls the inode's write leases BEFORE it holds anything: `op_guard.admitWrite` (every kernel/ring write — a Shared-class write may still land an overlay), `op_guard.Table.acquire(.exclusive)` (truncate, setattr, unlink, an extending or CoW write), `plane.lockLayout` (every layout RMW, any origin), `forgetShape`. The recall is Dekker's: the daemon stores `recall := 1` (SeqCst) then loads `inflight`; the client stores `inflight += 1` (SeqCst) then loads `recall` — a client that reads LIVE has its count visible to the daemon, a daemon that reads 0 has its recall visible to every later client entry. `inflight == 0` ⇒ the daemon proceeds AT ONCE (no round trip); else it polls the word through its `Io` (a fiber parks; never a kernel futex wait on a lane's thread) up to `RECALL_BOUND_NS` = 1 s, then FORCES (`recall := 2`, `ipc_write_leases_forced` fires, the session is poisoned). `WriteLeaseModel` (explorer, TSO store buffers) proves: never a mutation with a write in flight, every client write landed-before or refused; dropping either side's fence breaks it | A client write that completed can never have landed on a block the layout no longer names: the daemon did not mutate until the count read 0 with the recall visible. A client's `pwrite` syscall completes within the device's latency even if the process dies mid-call, so a count stuck past a second is a wedged or hostile client, not a slow device |
| AY.4 The client's write | Under the lease: aligned (4 KiB), in size (size-neutral: an extending write is the ring's), one mapped block, `enterWrite` → `pwrite` on the device → `exitWrite` (a wake if a recaller waits) → counted. A recall seen at entry drops the lease (the next attempt re-asks; past two the ring). A landed write under a FORCED lease answers EIO — the one case the bytes may be lost, and it is loud on both sides. The epoch is checked BEFORE any size-derived answer (a grow since the grant) and the device write is never "completed" from a stale size | The epoch guards the map and the size; the recall guards the in-flight window; neither fabricates |
| AY.5 The postlude | A batched `WriteNote{binding, max_end, count, vols_mask}` every 64 writes or 10 ms (checked at the next write), at the lease's drop (unbind) and — ACKED — at `fsync`/`fdatasync`, which the shim now interposes: the note acked, then `fdatasync` on every device written since the last, then the real call (the kernel's FSYNC finds the dirty mark owed and the bytes flushed). The daemon's note handler: `opens.noteDurableWrite` (the stamp at the open's flush/release — §L.6's parked Δ(mtime, ctime)), `inval.onWrite` (KD-11), the fsync touched word per volume | The data is on the device at the ack; only the stamp and the kernel's cache are owed, and §L.6 already bounds a write's stamp by the flush interval. A client killed before its last partial batch loses that batch's dirty mark, never a byte |
| AY.6 Crash atomicity | A direct write is an in-place patch of any aligned size within the block: its atomicity under a cut is the device's sector atomicity — ext4's O_DIRECT shape — and differs from the daemon's own overlay arm above `PATCH_MAX` (a CoW destination) | The bar is ext4; the client chose the device |
| AY.7 Security | Unchanged in kind: the device-permission screen (now the write bit too), the client's own `open(O_RDWR)` the final word. A direct client can already read any block of the volume; writing extends the same whole-device trust. `-o interception=direct` remains the owner's opt-in for clients the device permissions already name | Nothing new is trusted; the same thing is trusted for more |

Measured rows and the kill/cut rows: the evidence note. Owed: a bump of the read-lease epoch only when custody is taken (today: any arm but the in-place patch); a direct client's unsent partial note at `close` under a held ctl lock (the stamp of ≤ 63 writes).

## AZ. The §AG.2 kernel pin is the holder's too: a recalled grant stays registered for the pin (2026-10-20; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path.md` "wave 2")

The full gate's INVALIDATION COHERENCE row read `d/x` on B stale once after A's unlink (B's kernel kept the positive dentry for the row's 60 s TTL). §AG.2 pinned the token at the READER — a kernel-pinned entry is dropped without a `Release`, so the holder keeps recalling the reader — but a recall's ack RETIRED the grant at the holder (`retireFrameLocked` → `unregisterLocked`), so after any change to `d` that named no dentry (since §AV the drain of the parent stamp parked by a create, on its 50 ms cadence; a `chmod d`) the reader held no grant while its kernel still served every dentry the token had answered (`PRUNE` cannot drop a directory with cached children; `INVAL_INODE` drops its attrs and pages). The next walk through `d` re-registers the reader (its stale attrs cost a GETATTR under a fresh token), which is why the hole bit only when the next name change came first — the gate's race between the drain's cadence and the row's shells. Both applier arms lose it (3/8 red with a 300 ms pause before the unlink, either arm); the arm only re-phases the drain tick.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AZ.1 The pin at the holder | `Holder.setKernelPin(ns)` (the mount sets it beside the divert's, from the one peered-TTL posture): a recall ACKED keeps `(object, client)` registered until `ack + pin` (`Grant.recalled_until_ns`, `dlm_token_recall_pins`); the next pass recalls it again (the reader's service writes the names with no entry, as it already did under the reader's pin); a fresh ask makes it live again; a frame that expired or a swept member unregisters as before. Expiry: the FIFO of pinned acks swept at every pass (one pin length ⇒ time order) and, exactly, an object's expired pins dropped as its recall begins (`dlm_token_recall_pins_expired`) | The law "a kernel entry answered under a token never outlives the token's coherence" needs the registration to last as long as the answers may; the reader's drop-without-release gave that only until the first recall. The holder knows its own longest kernel TTL; the set's mounts read the same TTL posture (a per-mount knob is the owner's) |
| AZ.2 Rejected: an `INC_EPOCH` on every directory recall | Not built | Correct and blind: every dentry of the reader's mount stale at its next walk for each change to one directory it had read — the lookup storm §AB.2's names exist to avoid |
| AZ.3 Rejected: the reader re-registers on its own after a recall | Not built | A wire round trip per recall per reader (the listen-only grant does not exist in the verb set); the holder's registration costs a few words |
| AZ.4 The row | The coherence smoke pins the window without a timer: B's walk, then A's `chmod d && rm d/x` in one shell (the name-less recall, the unlink before B's kernel walks `d` again); red 3/3 without the holder's pin. The harness forwards `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLIERS` and `SQUEEZEFS_META_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` to the rows' daemons as it does the rings pin | A deterministic shape of the gate's race; the arm pinnable from the harness, as the orchestrator's A/B asked |

## BA. The create's chain, wave 3: the §L pad hold derives from the device grain; the applier's resolve is seeded; the pass venue is a lever (2026-10-20; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-3.md`)

At 32 ranks the create is 300 µs inside the commit call (`SQUEEZEFS_OP_PROFILE`: handler 18 µs before, 1 µs after): queue wait 36, the pass's hold 42 µs on 56 % of passes (22 % the §L pad hold, 33 % the §AW pacing), the applier's window 55 (its `leaf_lock_hold` 100 µs per 4.4-tx window, ≈ 60 of it the four appliers' waits for each other's leaves), the lane 55, the hops. The owner's direction: performance first.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BA.1 The pad hold by grain | `conveyor.padHoldFor(grain) = grain ≥ 4096`: the §L pad-majority hold stays on a device whose grain is a page (a pad-majority window wastes one per round trip) and is off on a finer grain; `SQUEEZEFS_META_WINDOW_HOLD` = `derived` (shipped) / `both` (§L and §AW.5 as they were — the control arm) / `pacing` / `none`. The §AW pacing hold stays in every arm but `none` | Measured on the 512-B RAM namespace, `-np 32 -u`: the pad hold saved 22 B of pad per create and held 16 k of 73 k passes ≈ 40 µs each — every member of a held batch waited a landing; off, the create's queue wait 36 → 25 µs, file creation 88.5 → 96.9 k, removal 89.2 → 100.4 k, directory creation 58.8 → 64.0 k, removal 51.3 → 58.8 k, the shared-directory shapes inside their spread, the ring +9 % of device bytes; `none` (the pacing hold off too) pays 2.3× the windows and 38 % more ring for the same rate. §L's premise — "the hold's cost is its members' wait of ≤ one round trip" — is the create's whole critical path when the lane is no longer the ceiling; its gain is a page of ring only at a page grain |
| BA.2 The seeded resolve | The committer's heap admission (§AW.2) resolves the tx's leaves; their references now ride the queue entry (`Queued.seeds`) and the applier's A3 starts from them, descending only for a leaf superseded, split or moved (`stale`'s predicate; `meta_conveyor_resolve_seeded` / `_descended`) | 100 % seeded on mdtest; the applier lanes' CPU −11 %. The resolve precedes the lock, so the lock hold did not move — a CPU win |
| BA.3 The pass venue, measured and not shipped | `SQUEEZEFS_META_PASS_VENUE=committer`: the election winner runs the pass AND the apply on its own thread (the election serializes passes whatever thread hosts them; only the submit and the ack need the lane's ring); no pass fiber on the lane (one that re-ran `runScheduledPass` on its tick while a committer held leadership mid-pass drained a batch twice — the one defect the arm met). `lane` ships | Latency-neutral (the create 327 vs 319 µs): the leader applies a 10-tx batch serially under the leaves where four appliers overlap theirs; the lane's CPU 2.2 → 0.9 s per 320 k creates is the arm's real gain, and a leader that keeps leadership while committers keep arriving is a convoy on its FUSE lane |
| BA.4 The structure that stands | Per-tx reservations cannot replace the pass's batch: every reservation pads to the device grain (the pad law makes each window's run grain-aligned, which is what lets windows share no sector), so a reservation per committer costs a grain per create (2.3× the ring at 512 B, 20× at 4 KiB). The seq is the journal position (the §4.6 pt 2 floors and the tail law read it as one), so the apply follows the reservation. The ack follows the landing (charter §5.2) | The remaining chain — enqueue, the pass, the applier, the lane's write and ack — is four thread hops around a device round trip, and ext4's 711 k acks a create from RAM. A lane that coalesces byte-granular per-committer reservations into grain writes (the pad law moved from the reservation to the write), or an ack-after-apply posture (ext4's own, but a daemon crash is a power cut for its metadata — the kill rows' law), is each the owner's: a §5.2 amendment with the crash matrix re-judged, not a lever |

## BB. Metadata takes the Lustre/ext4 posture: the ack after the RAM apply, the journal tail in a memfd the supervisor replays (2026-10-20; the owner's ruling; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-4.md`)

§BA.4 left the create's chain at four thread hops around a device round trip because charter §5.2 acked after the landing; ext4 acks a create from RAM and makes it durable at its commit (5 s) or at `fsync`. The owner ruled on 2026-10-20: SqueezeFS takes the Lustre/ext4 posture for metadata — acked once applied in RAM, durable at the next commit, `fsync`/`fsyncdir` durable on return, as close to ext4 as we can get — under two conditions the orchestrator set: a DAEMON crash stays lossless (the journal tail outlives the daemon in a kernel-held memfd its supervisor replays before anything else touches the volume; only a true power cut has the flush-window exposure), and group commit (byte-granular reservations the lane coalesces).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BB.1 The ack point | Under the coalesced cadence `Conveyor.applyWindow` answers the members (guards drop, kernel invalidation, lease epochs, the oneshots) right after the apply, the encode, the tail copy and the lane handoff — in that order: the handoff before the ack so an `fsync` issued after the ack finds the window in the lane's queue; the lane's verdict at the landing frees the window and settles the token union (`finishWindow`) without answering again. ONLY a window whose every member is this mount's kernel's own op (`tx.kernel_origin`) takes it: a wire-served step, a control record (a lease, a grant, the claim, a membership page), a job's or a tool's commit is answered at the landing as before. Strict mode (`SQUEEZEFS_META_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS=0`) answers at the lane's ack for every window — the durable-at-ack lever. The A1½ recall (§AG.2) precedes the reservation, so it precedes the new ack point as it did the old | The committer's wait no longer spans the device round trip and the lane's hops; what the law gives up (durability at the ack) is exactly what ext4 gives up, and the tail (BB.2) keeps a daemon crash lossless. The kernel-origin bound is the symmetric protocol's: §5.5 reads every verb as idempotent against DURABLE state, so a reply to a peer must mean the record landed — the first gate of the wave had a manager ack a joiner's slot leases at the apply, die unsupervised, and come back without them: the joiner's reclaim met `UnknownLease` ("the prior lease is not custody"), the one verdict that poisons a joined mount, its storm's creates parked at the move gate and the row wedged (`docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-4.md`). The registry's prefix law is untouched: an acked window is still an OPEN entry holding the checkpoint tail back until the lane completes it after the landing, so no ledger record ever names an entry the device lacks |
| BB.2 The journal tail | `meta/journal_tail.zig`: per writable volume a memfd (`sqz-jrnl-tail`, size-sealed: `F_SEAL_SHRINK\|GROW\|SEAL`) holding a header (magic, version, the device path, the ring's device offset and length, the grain), a slot table (`inboundCap(pages)` slots: `{state free\|written\|landed, seq_base, up to two device-relative runs}`) and a MIRROR of the ring at the ring's own offsets. The applier copies the window's image ops into the mirror at `op.offset − ring_base` and publishes the slot `written` (release) before the handoff; the lane's terminal outcome marks it `landed` and frees it. The daemon sends the memfd and the writer guard's fd to its supervisor over a `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socketpair the intermediate created before the second fork (`SCM_RIGHTS`: the fds are the kernel's once sent — a daemon dying before the supervisor's `recvmsg` loses nothing) | A mirror at the ring's offsets is the staging ring's trick: the bytes the lane writes ARE the replay's bytes (one memcpy per window, no second codec, byte-identical to the device image), the slot names what to write, and a landed window rewritten is idempotent by construction. The guard fd over the same socket shares its OPEN FILE DESCRIPTION with the supervisor, so the `flock` — Layer A of the D0 guard — outlives the daemon until the supervisor closes it |
| BB.3 The supervisor's replay, then the release | At the daemon's death (reaped, or its own ladder) `supervisor.linux_loop` drains the socket, maps each tail read-only, writes every `written` slot's runs from the mirror to the device in `seq_base` order (`pwrite`, then `fsync`: the same bytes the lane would have written), logs the count, and only then closes the guard fds. A successor's `acquireWriterFlock` meets the dead daemon's lock held by a pid that is gone and waits it out inside `guard.proc`'s bound (5 s — the replay of a 32 MiB ring is milliseconds on RAM, under a second on NVMe); past the bound the refusal stands, loud. A mount without a supervisor (foreground; `--daemon` without `--supervise`) and a supervisor that is itself gone have no replay: the next mount finds the device alone and the power-cut window applies — documented in charter §5.2 and said at mount under the coalesced cadence without a supervisor | "Before anything else reads or writes the volume" is the claim: as long as the supervisor holds the guard's OFD no writer opens the volume (the reader re-point reads under its own law). The PR registration is the host's, not the fd's: it outlives the daemon until a peer's death ladder preempts it at `T_owner` = 45 s, three orders past the replay, so a symmetric successor also sees the device complete; the ladder's own wait for the replay is owed (the risk the evidence note names) |
| BB.4 `fsync` covers the pending windows | A barrier request (`JournalLane.requestBarrier`) records the position of the last window admitted before it (`cover_upto`) and the fiber starts its barrier only once every pending window below that position has landed; the coalesced cadence's own barrier starts as before (what it misses rides the next) | Under the old law an fsync caller's own ops had landed before it asked; now they may be in the lane's queue or on the device's wire — the FSYNC must follow their CQEs on the same ring (io_uring orders nothing between SQEs) |
| BB.5 A write that fails after an ack | The lane's rollback hook rolls back nothing for an answered window (RAM is what was acked); the volume latches FAILED at once (`failStop`, one error line) and the hole is checkpointed past as before; `kill -9` the mount and the supervisor's replay retries the tail's bytes against the device | A rollback would make RAM contradict an ack the application already acted on; ext4 aborts its journal and goes read-only on the same event. The 3-count latch of the old law was for failures a committer could still be told about |
| BB.6 Group commit | Not built this wave. The pass's batch is the group: one window per pass drains every committer queued since the last, and the ack-after-apply lets the committers leave before the landing, so the lane's write count per entry is the pass's batch width (measured: the evidence note). Byte-granular per-committer reservations coalesced by the lane need two laws this wave did not have room for: two writes to one grain must be serialized on the lane (a run's tail grain shared with the next run — io_uring orders nothing), and the tail's replay must rebuild a shared grain from its entries, where today it writes window images | The structure that stands is §BA.4's; the measurement row is "entries per lane write" (`meta_conveyor_lane_handoffs` against `meta_journal_lane_writes` and the ring stats' entries) |
| BB.7 The kill rows re-judged | THE KILL MATRIX and the mid-tar `kill -9` rows stand as written (fsync'd bytes exact; an in-flight file exact or a hole — unchanged under the new law since the data's T_wb bounds already allowed loss of the unsynced). A supervised `kill -9` row (`--daemon --supervise`) is the replay's proof: acked creates present after the remount with no fsync; the same row on a foreground mount is the window's: acked creates may be absent, fsync'd ones never. dm-log-writes rows (the power cut) prove the window; the flakey rows of xfstests stay the data oracle | The matrix's rows were written against durability-at-ack; each is re-read against the posture it now proves |

## BC. The committer applies its own transaction; the pass reserves, stamps and serializes (2026-10-21; the owner's ruling "go for ext4's class"; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-5.md`)

With §BB the ack left the landing, and the create's chain was still enqueue → the pass → an applier fiber (the window's union lock, 95 µs per 4-tx window) → the committer: two hops and the appliers' serialization. The reference's own engine ran ≈ 300 µs per create per thread and never attacked that floor; the owner ruled it ours to remove.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BC.1 Self-apply | `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY=self` is the lever; `venue` (§AW's applier) ships this wave — `self` measured +22 % file creation and +38 % removal at 32 ranks but 0.87×/0.78× on directories and a convoy on the hot leaves at 64 ranks (0.6× creation, 0.3× removal), so the arm that scales ships until the leaf is spread (BC.6). The pass drains, recalls (A1½), admits, reserves ONE window and stamps the seqs — then hands every member its TURN (`Done.Signal.turn`) and drains the next batch. The member, on its committer's thread: resolve (seeded), lock ITS leaves ascending, revalidate (retry on an SMO race), its pre-images, apply, the threshold kick, unlock, encode its own entry (`journal.partOps`), copy it into the journal tail, and — this mount's kernel's own op (§BB.1) — answer itself. The last member to finish builds the window's image from the members' ops (`journal.buildWindowFromOps`) and hands it to the lane; a served verb, a control record or a tool waits for the landing (`Done.landing`). A `commitGroup`'s members keep the venue (one thread parks on them in turn) | No window apply, no applier hop on the critical path; the leaf locks are one transaction's. The seq is still the journal position, assigned at the reservation before any apply, so the tail law and the §4.6 floors stand; the window is still ONE reservation, so the pad law and the ring's accounting stand |
| BC.2 A rolled-out member is a PAD | A member whose apply fails (or whose entry cannot be encoded: its records leave seq-conditionally) becomes a PAD entry over its exact range (`BatchPart.pad`) — a chain link, never a hole; `applyHoles()` is false for a self-applied window, no hole checkpoint runs | Its window's survivors may already have answered themselves; a hole ahead of them would make them unreachable at replay until a checkpoint |
| BC.3 The turn spin | A committer waiting for its turn spins for it up to twice the turn's predicted latency (an RFC 6298 mean of enqueue → turn), capped at twice the §AB.4 wake (35 µs), then parks; `turnSpinBound` tie-tested | A send to a spinning receiver wakes nobody: the pass's fan-out of N turns costs no syscall and the committer no park/resume. Measured: the queue wait 17.7 → 7.9 µs at `-np 32 -u`; three in four turns found by the spin on creates |
| BC.4 The fsync cover | The lane's barrier request covers the conveyor's reservation high-water mark (`Hooks.cover_upto`) and starts once the registry's completed prefix passes it | A self-applied window is reserved and acked before the lane sees it; the lane's own pending set would miss it |
| BC.5 A failed write's tail slots | Stay written at the lane's verdict; only a landed window's are freed | The supervisor's replay is the retry (§BB.5) |
| BC.6 The lane's inode window — measured, not shipped | The rotor's tie-break by the committer's thread, and the cursor's own tree under the ceiling, both measured WORSE (`leaf_wait_other` 41 → 63–65 µs per take, file creation 119 → 85 k): the first funnels every lane into the smallest tree (the headroom law), the second shows the contended leaves are not the rotor trees' edges. Reverted; the per-tree leaf-wait phases stay | Each create takes 3.9 leaf locks and waits ≈ 41 µs for each at 32 ranks — the serializer once the hops are gone, and its identity (which tree, which leaf) is the next wave's first measurement |

## BD. The create's leaf set: two leaves, taken once — the heap admission's lock-free fast path (2026-10-21; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-6.md`)

Wave 5 counted 3.9 leaf write locks per `mdtest -u` create. Named: a regular create stages TWO records — the child's inode `Put` and the parent's dentry `Put`; the parent's stamp is parked (§AV) and its nlink does not change, so no third record exists — and each of the two leaves was taken TWICE: once by the committer's heap admission (§AW moved it before the enqueue, under the leaves' write locks) and once by the apply. The leaves are slot trees' (every take was in `leaf_wait_other`): the child's rotor slot and the parent's.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BD.1 The lock-free admission | On a volume with room (`heap_law.fastPathRoom`: free extents ≥ 2 × the compaction reserve + one per transaction the conveyor can hold) and no heap-full posture, a leaf whose published log end (`CachedNode.log_hint`, stored at every write unlock) plus the bytes admitted onto it without the lock (`inflight_admitted`) plus this transaction's fits in place (`heap_law.fastPathFits`) is admitted with NO lock: the bytes are charged to the leaf's in-flight word and released after the apply's unlock published them (or at the teardown of a member never applied). A leaf that does not fit, or a volume near its reserve, takes the locked law as before | The admission's purpose is the ENOSPC promise; a leaf with room needs none, and an over-admission (a hint read before a concurrent apply) only means a leaf splits at writeback from extents the room guarantees exist. The charge keeps `hint + inflight ≥ the truth` at every instant (the release follows the publish). Red test: a kernel create takes ≤ 2 leaves at the apply and none at the admission; charged ≡ released |
| BD.2 The lane's ino window — measured, reverted | One window of `node_size / 128` inos per lane per slot (one leaf's worth), cut with one `fetch_add`; consecutive mints of a lane adjacent | File creation unchanged (96.5 k venue, 119.4 k self), and the watermark-derived used-inode count (`statfs`) inflated by the windows' gaps (102 912 for 200 creates in the two-member row). The inode leaf is not the serializer |
| BD.3 What remains | Measured with BD.1: 55 % of admissions lock-free, the rest on leaves at capacity (a 256 KiB node fills with 4 KiB appended frames and is rewritten — 198 MB rewritten against 80 MB appended per 320 k creates); the apply's hold is 116 µs per 4-tx window on the venue of which 24 µs is lock wait: the lock is held by WORK — the apply's `O(overlay)` remerge and the checkpointer's freeze and rewrite under the leaf's lock (`sqz-ckpt` 2–3 s of CPU per phase). The serializer is the leaf's work under its lock, not its sharing | The next lever is the writeback's lock discipline (an O(1) freeze with the remerge and the rewrite off the lock) and the fold-forward overlay (the reference's D7) |

## BE. The FUSE transport speaks mainline's buffer-pool UAPI; the kmbuf rungs are transitional; zero-copy per open file is taken by serving opens (2026-10-21; the owner's ruling "we will not keep the fork"; `docs/evidence/rung-10-fuse-mainline-uapi.md`, `docs/evidence/rung-10-kernel-mainline.md`)

The daemon's fast buffer modes (`BufRing`, `ZeroCopy`) speak the kmbuf buffer-ring + zero-copy UAPI of patches 0001–0021, which upstream rejected; Linux 7.3 shipped buffer pools (`FUSE_IO_URING_CMD_ADD_QUEUE` = 3, `ADD_BUFPOOL` = 4 — colliding with the series' `RELEASE_PAYLOAD`/`FEATURES`), user-memory pools, per-entry zero-copy slots, and zero-copy granted per open file by `FOPEN_IO_URING_ZERO_COPY` — unreachable under zero-message opens. The owner ruled the fork gone: the transport is ported to mainline's UAPI, the series shrinks to real fixes and proposable patches (`scripts/kernel/patches-mainline/`).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BE.1 The MainlinePool rung is the primary contract | `geometry.BufferMode.mainline_pool`: one session pool (`worker.Pool`, `depth × payload_sz` per queue), the queue's region handed over by `ADD_QUEUE` (zero-copy iff the zc surface reads present) + a REGISTERED `ADD_BUFPOOL` from the queue's first ring, answered inline and checked before any REGISTER; registered at fixed index 0 on every ring of the queue; `IORING_URING_CMD_FIXED` + `buf_index` 0 on every REGISTER (header iovec + `{NULL, 0}`, `ent_zero_copy_buf_index = 1 + ent` on a zc queue) and COMMIT; the payload window at the header's `offset` (bounded by the region — a breach fails the session — and tripwired off the `payload_sz` grid); the direction from `FUSE_URING_ENT_ZERO_COPY` for READ (DEST) and WRITE (SOURCE) alone; the zc slots at `1 + ent`; the reply legs, the held write, the bounce slab unchanged over the shifted slot | The kernel's own design for the same feature; the pool is user memory the kernel keys by offset, so N rings per queue need no patch (the derived 3 carries over, to be re-measured); the direction bit retires quirk 3's opcode mirror and the readdir latch on this rung |
| BE.2 The probe is INIT bit 43 and ranks above the ladder | `probes.bufpoolVerdict` (`FUSE_HAS_IO_URING_BUFPOOL`); `auto` ⇒ MainlinePool wherever offered, else the kmbuf rungs, else UserEnts; `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_BUFFER_MODE=pool` the explicit pin, refused loud without the offer; `bufring`/`zc` refused on a kernel offering the bit (kmbuf absent there). The daemon writes `fuse.enable_uring=Y` BEFORE `mount(2)` so the offer carries both bits on the first mount after boot. The legacy command probes (`RELEASE_PAYLOAD` for retention, `FEATURES` for rings per queue) run only on a legacy rung (`probes.legacyProbesApply`) | No command probe is side-effect free on both strata: opcode 3 creates queue 0 (without zero-copy) on mainline and looks up a retention on the series; the INIT bit is the kernel's advertisement, side-effect free, and `ringAtReply`'s first-mount hole is closed at the source. Seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_MAINLINE_POOLS` answers the probe on a legacy kernel: the first ADD_QUEUE meets `RELEASE_PAYLOAD` (-ENOTCONN) and the mount fails with the typed `MainlinePoolRefused` — the collision guard's red test, green on 7.2.8-9.33 |
| BE.3 Zero-copy per open file: serve opens | Mainline grants zero-copy by the OPEN/CREATE reply's flag; a zero-message open carries the kernel's `FOPEN_KEEP_CACHE` alone. On a MainlinePool zero-copy session `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC_OPEN=1` (default) SERVES regular-file opens — the control open arm of §AF whatever `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_NO_OPEN` read (the lifecycle flips its posture at INIT, `atomic_o_trunc` returns, `FOPEN_NOFLUSH` on clean handles, the `.stats` latch is skipped) — and marks every OPEN/CREATE reply; `=0` keeps zero-message opens and marks CREATE alone (a created file's first open is zero-copy, a re-open copies through the pool). Pure table `session.openPosture`; the posture reaches the filesystem as `InitCaps.zc_open` / `zc_create_only` | The numbers both ways (the same daemon, 7.2.6 + the series): zero-message opens bought open/close ×4 6.6×, +read 5.6×, one thread 1.2–1.4×, mdtest creation 1.18×, the data rows unchanged (§10.5 of `rung-10-dev-perf.md`); zero-copy bought 1M seq write 1.9–2.5× (io-wq 26 % → 2.6 %) and 4k randread 1.04–1.08× (`rung-10-zero-copy-writes.md`). A data mount's per-op gain outweighs one 13 µs OPEN per open; the metadata workloads keep the lever. Decided by reasoning from those measurements; the 7.3 A/B (the plan in the evidence note) is owed before the default is called shipped. Option (c), an INIT flag granting zero-copy to zero-message opens, is written as an RFC with a sketch patch (`scripts/kernel/upstream/fuse-zc-no-open/`, checkpatch and W=1 clean on v7.3) for the owner to post |
| BE.4 The pooled held write | A WRITE the kernel copied into the pool (no per-file grant, or a copying queue) is HELD in its pool buffer when `zc.holdShape` and the filesystem's gate admit it (`worker.tryHoldPooled`): the lease carries the bytes in hand AND the slot coordinates (`PayloadLease.acquireHeldPooled`), the direct store is `WRITE_FIXED(device ← pool @ user address)` on `POOL_INDEX` (`prepPoolStore`), a reader of the bytes pays no bridge. Counted `transport_pool_write_holds` | The pool is an ordinary user-registered buffer (`imu->dir` both ways, no FUSE-side lock; the buffer is the entry's until its COMMIT's fetch), so lever (b) applies to a copied payload too: the kernel's one copy into the pool, then DMA — the reference's UserEnts arena never had a registered source |
| BE.5 The legacy rungs stay until matched | `BufRing`/`ZeroCopy` keep their probes, registration, retention and tests; `transport_buffer_mode` 3 names the new rung; the deletion of the legacy modes, of `kmbuf.zig` and of patches 0001–0021/0024/0026/0030/0031 is a later owner-ruled commit after the 7.3 matrix matches the 9.33 numbers | Dual-mode is the transition; no regression on the 7.2.8-9.33 venue kernel is admissible (the full unit suite and the lattice/zc e2e rows are green on it) |
| BE.6 Live validation owed | `linux-mainline-sqz 7.3.0-1.7` is installed below the default Limine entry; the owner boots it. Then: the e2e row "e2e mainline pools" (skips where INIT offers no bit 43) — ADD_QUEUE/ADD_BUFPOOL on every queue, reads and writes byte-exact through the pool, zc'd READ/WRITE flagged, the held slot store and the pooled hold, the laws closed; the smoke's mount/kill rows on `auto`; then the measurement plan of the evidence note. **Ran 2026-10-22** (evidence §9): the bring-up's success arm and the gate's rows hold on 7.3; the ten reds of the first gate were the §AF rows judging zero-message opens on a mount that serves them (now pinned to the posture they test) and one defect the posture exposed — an O_TRUNC OPEN of a foreign-slot file ran the local size path (`efaaf1d`: shipped like SETATTR); the measurement plan stays owed | Developed against the kernel source and the fake-kernel seams; the first ADD_QUEUE on a real pool kernel has not run |
## BF. The read cache: one LRU cache core, used forward for reading and reversed for writing; 25 % of system RAM each (2026-10-21; the owner's rulings; `docs/evidence/rung-10-read-cache.md`)

The 4 KiB random read's ranged window was "never published" (charter §6.3 as of 10-20): the second read of a window paid the device leg again (1.7 µs of a 4.6 µs read), and the reuse the ghost's second touch bought was a 4 MiB fetch for a 4 KiB request under a 5 % waste bound. The owner ruled (2026-10-21): two caches, `SQUEEZEFS_READ_CACHE_PCT` and `SQUEEZEFS_WRITE_CACHE_PCT`, each 25 % of SYSTEM RAM by default, both under the memory budget; the read cache evicts on plain LRU; and ONE cache model serves both directions.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BF.1 The core | `mem/cache.zig` `Cache(Entry, Sink)`: a striped exact LRU (intrusive list per shard, a spin lock per shard, O(1) touch, nothing allocated per op), the bound `pct % × min(RAM, budget ÷ 0.7)`, epoch validation on lookup, insert / lookup / lookupMany / invalidateKey(s) / invalidateIno / evictUntil / shed / component, a per-instance Stats family; the Sink is the direction: `runs_under_lock = true` DROPS under the lock, `false` runs OUTSIDE it under the eviction protocol (the victim stays indexed and servable while the sink runs; `.kept` relinks it at the head and the pass backs off) | The read direction's sink is a slot release that never blocks; the write direction's is a promotion that may park and must never lose bytes. One core, two sinks: the recency order, the bound and the pressure response are written once |
| BF.2 LRU, exact within a shard | No CLOCK (the hot tier's), no ARC, no frequency: the owner's ruling. The approximation is stated and tie-tested: `S` shards split the bound evenly and a victim is the oldest of a 1/S slice | O(1) touch under a striped lock with no global order is the only LRU that costs tens of ns on a 1.9 M IOPS path; a global list would serialize every hit |
| BF.3 The grain | `GRAIN = 4 KiB` (the LBA grain every ranged window is a multiple of); windows ≤ 64 KiB (the ranged bounce slot: 16 lookups bound a hit, and past it the ghost already escalates to the hot tier) | A 4 KiB read is one lookup and stores exactly its device bytes; a 64 KiB slot would hold 4 KiB until neighbours arrived (16× until then); a 4 MiB slot is the hot tier |
| BF.4 One shard per block | `GrainKey.shardHash()` hashes `(vol_tag, block offset)` — the core's shard; `hash()` and `eql` are the grain's own — the index. One hash for both made a block's grains one probe cluster (the first A/B's lanes at 96 %) | A window serve, a deposit and a purge each take ONE lock; the ONE purge reaches the tier with the block key alone; the index stays O(1) |
| BF.5 The ranged purge | `PurgeSeam.purgeRange`, `purge.purgeBlockRange`: W1's `finishInPlace` purges `[rel, rel + len)` — the whole-block tiers drop the block, the grains exactly the window; the scattered in-place arms (fold, drain) purge the block | A 4 KiB write under a random reader costs the reader one grain, not the block's thousand |
| BF.6 The coherence law | (a) the incarnation at the insert, (b) the binding after the copy, (c) the filling inode's lease epoch read BEFORE the DMA still current at the lookup; the reader's recall sink now bumps the recalled object's lease epoch (`wire_plane.RecallSink.noteRecalled`) beside its purge | (a) ∧ (b) is every tier's existing proof; (c) is the read view's / direct arm's law extended to the tier, so a foreign write reaches it through the recall as a bump, not only through the key purge (belt and braces, and the direct arm's leases on a foreign inode drop at the recall too) |
| BF.7 Where the fill lands | `CompletionHook.run(ok, landed)`: the IPC direct-drive passes its arena and the hook deposits BEFORE its recheck (purging on a failed one); the FUSE zero-copy leg passes nothing — its DMA landed in the app's pages (the dest slot; the worker's `window` is the bounce), which THE READ VIEW row caught as 4 KiB of zeros served from a deposit of the bounce. With the cache on, `directLeg` declines a grain-eligible cold read so the lane's ranged read fills (`SQUEEZEFS_READ_CACHE_FILL=lane`; `leg` the control arm) | A cache a cold FUSE read never fills is no cache; the lane hop and one copy on the cold read buy every later serve. Owed: the leg into the cache's own memfd slab, replied from it by `READ_FIXED` — no lane hop cold, zero passes warm |
| BF.8 Stated, not solved | A foreign in-place patch (W1 on the holder: zero journal, size-neutral) reaches a reader's tiers — and its kernel page cache — when the parked stamp drains at the flush cadence; a per-write token recall would put the holder's mutex on the 4 KiB write path | The posture pre-exists for the hot tier and the kernel's cache; the read cache rides the same event, no worse. A lock-free "tokened inode" predicate for W1 is the owed item |
| BF.9 Not built | A unified LRU across the hot tier and the grain cache (one order over 4 MiB slots and 4 KiB grains); an fd-source arm for grain serves | The hot tier's CLOCK is coupled to the prefetch pipeline's probation/consume-behind law; at 4–64 KiB a memcpy beats a bridge op |

## BG. The write cache's member scope is the PR registrant: (mount slot, fabric host identity) — a second identity on one host is another member (2026-10-21; the owner's judgement; `docs/evidence/rung-10-write-cache.md`)

The first gate of the write cache branch went red in ONE place: `ops.nvmet.fidelity` "sigstop-preempt". A is SIGSTOPped past T_owner; B, under a second fabric host identity on the SAME host, preempts A's PR key (the recovery ladder's device-fenced takeover) — and died at its mount: `StagingOwnerLive`. Both present the manager's slot 0 of one set, so §T.1's member scope `(set, mount slot)` named ONE claim (the abstract socket), A's process still held it (stopped, not dead: nothing listens, nothing hands over), and B was refused as "a second mount of this member". The owner judged the law: the ring belongs to a MEMBER — its acked custody is that member's; B cannot adopt A's residents (A is alive and fenced, its residents are its flush-window exposure by §BB/§6.1, loud and counted), and a fenced or stopped holder's claim must never block a different identity's mount.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BG.1 The member | `(mount slot, member identity)`, the member identity `staging.memberIdentity(hostnqn, hostid)` = `hash64(hostnqn ‖ 0 ‖ hostid)` of the fabric host identity the mount presents (`mount_fabric.Plan.memberIdentity`): the explicit pair, `/etc/nvme`'s, or the machine-id derivation salted by the mount point; 0 on a set with no fabric record (a local device: one member per host). The marker is v4 (88 B) and carries it; the claim's name is `squeezefs-staging-<set identity>-<slot>-<member>`; `bindVerdict` reads another member under the same slot as `foreign_owner` (never adopted, never wiped) | The PR registrant IS the fencing unit: the device fences between host identities, so two identities on one host are two writers in every law that matters, and their rings are two custodies. The identity is stable across a member's incarnations (its successor remounts under the same pair — the derived one by the same mount point), so the kill -9 handover is unchanged; the node token stays boot-scoped and out of the scope (§T.1) |
| BG.2 The stopped holder | A keeps its ring and its claim (its process lives); resumed, its next beat reads the usurpation (`writer_guard_fenced`, the latch FAILED), its writes answer EIO and its residents can never be promoted — the exposure of an acked unsynced write under a fence, counted at its death as the supervisor's held ring (no deadline: acked custody; the log names the pid whose `kill` discards it) | B adopting A's ring would be two writers over one segment with A alive — the §T.1 defect; wiping it would lose A's acked bytes under A's feet. The exposure is the law §6.1 already states for a fenced writer |
| BG.3 Red tests | the marker/claim unit test (a second member under the same slot: `foreign_owner`; its claim name differs; the pair hashes unambiguously), the write-cache bind test (the same member live: `OwnerLive`; a second member: its own fresh ring beside it, no refusal counted), `mount_fabric`'s identity test (no plan ⇒ 0; two mount points ⇒ two members; a remount at one ⇒ the same), and the fidelity leg as the oracle | the leg is the production shape: one host, two identities, a stopped holder preempted |

## BH. fallocate reserves: the UNWRITTEN binding, written in place by the landing that fills it (2026-10-23; the owner's ruling "implement it"; `docs/evidence/rung-10-fallocate.md`)

The adjudication audit's largest gap (F1): every `fallocate(2)` mode answered EOPNOTSUPP, LTP fallocate03 and mremap06 sat in the ledger for it, and 31 xfstests of the auto group `_notrun` behind `_require_xfs_io_command falloc`. The reference served the call but reserved nothing (0002 row 554). The owner's bar: a reservation must be real — a later write inside it cannot ENOSPC — and a 1 GiB fallocate must not write 1 GiB.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BH.1 The representation | A flag on the 40 B mapping wire, `M_UNWRITTEN` (bit 2): the block allocated and bound to the file at its index — the `+ref` rides the layout's transaction, the allocator recovers it from the refs ledger, `df` and fsck's census count it — its bytes not the file's. Whole-block only, never DAMAGED; the tree-mapped map carries it as a kind-2 record (`Rec.point`'s special form, as a quarantine marker). A publish naming it needs no DMA proof (`publishLayout`: the proof law exists so a type flip cannot precede its bytes; an unwritten binding names none); its flip to WRITTEN is the same reference — no `±ref`, no proof (`Mapping.sameRef`) | The alternatives: zero-filled blocks written at fallocate (the owner's bar refuses: 1 GiB of DMA for a 1 GiB reservation) or a reservation COUNT the admission subtracts (ext4's delayed-allocation reservation): durable only through a per-inode range list every publish must split, re-derived at mount by a full layout scan, and not a reference the allocator or fsck knows. The flag costs no bytes, rides every existing law (the refs ledger, the census, the mover, the recovery seed) and leaves the kernel's `statfs` honest at once |
| BH.2 One hide, two consumers | `MapSeam.lookup` (the write side) and the read path's `Meta.binding`/`file_at` (`plane.written`) answer an unwritten binding as a HOLE: every read serves zeros, W1 and the overlay decline, every seed is zeros, `zeroExtension` writes nothing over it. `MapSeam.reserved` answers it: the admission's custody probe reads it as custody the file holds (`writeWouldCreateFreshCustody`: a sole-owned reservation admits on an exhausted store) and the landing's destination choice takes it over (BH.3). The read choke point refuses one that reaches it (`unwritten_mapping_served`, must-stay-0) | A binding hidden at the two resolve seams is a hole to every arm that exists and every arm that will — the alternative, "every consumer checks the flag", is one forgotten check from serving a block's recycled bytes as a file's zeros |
| BH.3 The landing in place | `accumulate.pickDest`: the whole-block upload of `(ino, block)` whose map holds a reservation this mount owns alone (`beginPatchSoleOwner`: refcount 1, not SHARED, under W1's fence pair; no epoch binds the block, no overlay record covers it, the volume active) DMAs the whole image INTO the reserved block, re-stabilizes the word and publishes the WRITTEN twin durably at once — never through the rewrite epoch (a shadowed takeover would be freed as "the epoch's mint" on the destroy's `NotFound`, while the layout names it: the one double-free shape). Declined (a clone's share, an epoch's binding, an open overlay) ⇒ the fresh-mint path; its publish displaces the reservation (a different reference → `−ref`, freed after the commit) and the rewrite epoch parks it as the displaced A key (`record`'s `prev` = `lookup orelse reserved`; the overlay's Hole record feeds the reservation the same way), so the save's fold meets a parked key and the drift tripwire stays silent. The device overlay never takes a reservation over: its destination is always a fresh mint, and on a full store its mint declines to accumulation — which takes the reservation over | Space-neutral in every arm (a takeover mints and frees nothing; a displacement frees one block for the one it minted); on a FULL store the takeover is the only arm that can land — which is what the admission promised |
| BH.4 The verbs | `WritePath.reserveRange` (holes → fresh unwritten mints, `ZERO_RANGE`'s written blocks → their unwritten twins, one `merge` per `max_displaced` inserts, the flipped keys purged, the mints returned on a failed chunk — fallocate is idempotent); `MapSeam.punch` (the inline map republished without up to `max_displaced` entries of the range, their `−ref` in the diff; a tree-mapped file's `kvmap.stagePunch` loads the straddling window as `stageWindow` does, clears the range's cells and re-canonicalizes — a run keeps its head and its tail, a superseding point frees its own binding); `MapSeam.shift` (BH.5). The handler settles the write custody before a punch or a shift (the map it edits is whole), zeroes a partial edge through the write admission only inside a WRITTEN block, and commits the size last (SIZE NEVER LEADS DATA) | A punch's whole-block free is the truncate's clip at an interior range; its edge is a write of zeros like any other (W1 in place for an aligned sub-cap piece) |
| BH.5 COLLAPSE / INSERT | At the BLOCK grain (EINVAL below it — fallocate(2)'s alignment law names the filesystem block, which for the data plane is the stripe block): the inline map re-indexed in ONE transaction (`Publish.shift`: every re-keyed reference the old map named is proof-exempt; `shiftFits`: the record at its new length + a `−ref`/`+ref` pair per moved reference + a `−ref` per removed one under the journal's whole-entry cap), a collapse's removed bindings freed after the commit, then the size commit without the truncate's clip or extension law (a shift's bytes past its new EOF are the moved blocks' own). A tree-mapped head or a shift past the one-entry budget: EOPNOTSUPP, counted (`layout_shift_refusals`), the map standing. **OWNER-CALL — the kernel's FUSE client forwards neither mode** (`fs/fuse/file.c` `fuse_file_fallocate` refuses `mode & ~(KEEP_SIZE | PUNCH_HOLE | ZERO_RANGE)` before any request, 7.3 and 9.33 alike; `UNSHARE_RANGE` too): the daemon's arms are reached through the dispatcher (the unit tests) and nothing else until a kernel patch forwards them — a UAPI-semantic change (page-cache truncation and the i_size move on the kernel side) that belongs upstream, not in the series alone. The smoke row pins the kernel's law | A multi-transaction shift has a torn intermediate state (a gap of `k` blocks between the moved and the unmoved part) with no durable progress marker; the refusal is honest and counted. xfstests' `_require_xfs_io_command fcollapse` probe meets the kernel's EOPNOTSUPP and `_notrun`s the fcollapse/finsert rows exactly as before |
| BH.6 The guard and the size | The lifecycle takes the EXCLUSIVE level-1 guard for FALLOCATE as for SETATTR-size (taker list: "SETATTR iff size, FALLOCATE"); a size rise goes through `setattr`'s truncate-up path (the extension law's zeros on a mapped EOF block — a hole's or a reservation's writes nothing —, the size epoch, mtime/ctime); a collapse's or insert's size through `commitSize` (the epoch, the record, the claim's retire, no clip); mtime/ctime stamp when the file changed, never on an idempotent re-reservation | A reservation is a layout change: the truncate's class |
| BH.7 Red tests | layout: the wire pin, the law (whole-block only, never DAMAGED), the choke point's refusal, the kvmap round trip, the publish's proof exemptions and the shift arm's; write path: `reserveRange` over the RAM world (3 holes reserved, the written block untouched, no device byte, charged, idempotent; the write inside lands in place — same reference, no mint, no free, W1 takes the next rewrite; ZERO_RANGE flips with no I/O; a clone-shared reservation declines and is displaced without the drift tripwire) and the FULL store (a hole's write refused, the reservation's admitted and landed in place, a refused reservation returns its mints); kvmap: `stagePunch`'s head/tail/superseding-point/width law; kv_fs: the mode law and the inline arms on a volume-less set, every striped arm through the recording Data face; the smoke row FALLOCATE — EVERY MODE'S LAW on the real daemon (xfs_io, `df`, hexdumps, the kernel's EOPNOTSUPP for the three unforwarded modes, the full store, kill -9 → fsck → remount → the laws re-read) | the pinned ENOSYS retired (the "rung-4 ops default to ENOSYS" test: fallocate is in the must set) |

## BI. The read path's lifetime law: a device-true serve is judged by the block's lifetime and its binding, the seqlock generation is the tiers' (2026-10-23; `docs/evidence/rung-10-read-lifetime-law.md`)

The first full xfstests run after fallocate landed stopped at generic/095 (45 mixed writers — buffered, aio-dio, mmap, splice — and 512 B direct readers on two 1 MiB files): fio read EIO four times, the scratch daemon fired `read_settle_lost_serialized` once and logged `did not settle after 4 serialized settle attempts` ×4. The test had passed before only because a 1 MiB file was STAGED (the write cache, RAM, no device word); fio's `fallocate=native` now makes it striped (the reservation promotes), and the same job with `fallocate=none` on a cache-less mount (striped from its first write, no reservation anywhere) fails the same way — the defect is the read path's, latent for every striped block under a sustained in-place storm.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BI.1 What the seqlock word protects | The block's incarnation word (`gen << 1 | stable`) is the TIERS' law: a whole-block fill or a ranged grain is deposited only under a stable pre-DMA snapshot that still reads after the bytes landed, so a cached copy is of one generation, and an in-place write's `retire → DMA → publish` (W1, the in-place drains, a reservation's landing) moves the generation exactly to make the copies of the rewritten page stale | A serve that deposits nothing (the ranged window, the escalated device-true leg, the settle) was judged by the same word — and lost whenever any in-place write of the block was in flight, whatever page it rewrote. Under generic/095's storm the word is unstable more often than stable: the ranged ladder (8), the whole-block walk (8) and the settle (4) all lost, with 15 ms of backoff sleeps per read, then EIO |
| BI.2 The lifetime law | `ReadPath.lifetime(key)`: the allocator cell's lifetime STAMP while the cell is allocated (`dead` in any other state; `untracked` for a key no allocator here tracks, which keeps the seqlock law — the token's domain). `deviceServeValid`: the lifetime read before the DMA stands after it AND the map still binds the key (the recheck every serve makes). A block freed and re-minted under the read — to another file, or to the same index by a later mint — carries a new stamp: a loss, re-resolved; a key whose mapping names a dead cell is refused before the DMA. The ranged read DMAs under an unstable word too (it is not a dead lifetime) and deposits only under a stable one | The bytes a device-true read lands are the device's at the DMA's instant: torn at the device's sector grain against a concurrent in-place write of the SAME file at most — the O_DIRECT law ext4 and XFS give a direct read racing a direct overwrite (no lock between them) — and never another lifetime's, which is what the stamp guards. The seqlock gave the read a guarantee POSIX does not ask for and the storm could not grant |
| BI.3 The settle's stripe | `ReadPath.settle` is the plane's own (3.5) `MetaStripes` (`plane.locks.stripes`, the table `MapSeam.merge` runs under), passed at `init`; the port had given the read path a table of its own, which serialized settling readers against each other and excluded no publish — the tripwire's premise ("under the settle's stripe no mutator runs") held only by the settle's rarity | With the real stripe and the lifetime law a loss under the settle means a binding or a lifetime moved under the publish lock: the tripwire names a true anomaly again. Readers park behind a commit only in the settle (after 16 losses); no new lock order (the same level) |
| BI.4 Red | `data.read_path` "the lifetime law": a storm hook retires and republishes the word under EVERY DMA — on dev the 512 B direct read lost its ladder, its walk and the settle (the tripwire, EIO); now the ranged leg serves on its first window with nothing deposited, the whole-block read serves through the device-true leg without a settle, an unstable word at the resolve is read all the same, and a free + re-mint under the read stays a loss. By hand: generic/095's job with `fallocate=native` (write cache on) and with `fallocate=none` on a cache-less mount — 15 and 2 EIOs with the tripwire before, 0 errors and 0 alerts after. The fixture's bindings carry the cell's real stamp (a minted binding's) | the two shapes 095 takes on this product |

## BJ. fallocate's edges name the size as it stands after the settle; a reservation leaves the write side's custody alone (2026-10-23; `docs/evidence/rung-10-fallocate-fsx.md`)

The full xfstests run after the lifetime law stopped at generic/127 (fsx `-l 262144 -N 100000 -R -W`): READ BAD DATA, zeros where data was written. 127 passed alone and in the hour-old subset; the TEST set's daemon had fired `rewrite_shadow_displaced_drift` twice 114 tests earlier under generic/013's fsstress. Two defects, both fallocate's, neither the epoch's save nor the lifetime law's:

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BJ.1 The op sequence (127) | fsx `-X` (verify after every op) by hand, seed 21: `TRUNCATE DOWN → 0xbe99`, COPYs and writes extend the file again (their size claims PARKED, the durable size still 0xbe99), `fzero 0x3bbdb..0x3e29e` past EOF → the handler's striped arm settles the custody — the settle journals the parked claim and retires it — then computes the edge's size from the ENTRY probe plus the claim (now 0): `0xbe99`; the edge's zero write `[0x3bbdb, 0x3bf12)` carried `file.size = 0xbe99`, the admission ran the extension law (`req.offset > file.size`) and zeroed `[0xbe99, 0x3bbdb)`: every byte written since the truncate. The daemon's trace: `fallocate mode 10 [244699, 254623) size 245522` → `zeroExtension [48793, 244699)` | The law: a size read before a wait is stale by whatever the wait journaled. The striped arm reads the record after the settle (`claimedSize(local, readInode)`); `setattr`'s size path was already ordered right (the claim read before its settle, the record re-read in its tx) |
| BJ.2 The op sequence (the drift) | fsstress `-p 20 -r` by hand, round 1: the save of ino X displaced `vol … unwritten true` — a RESERVATION — with 0 or 1 parked key. A whole-block write into a hole writes through and rides the rewrite epoch (its shadow is the write-side authority; the map has no binding at the block until the save); a later `fallocate` of the range saw the map's hole and minted a reservation there; the save's fold displaced it unparked (and freed it, correctly by accident). The in-place takeover cannot follow (`pickDest` declines a block the epoch binds), so no double free — but the law was broken | **A reservation fills holes of the WRITE-SIDE AUTHORITY, not of the map alone**: a block a parked buffer holds, an open overlay record covers or a rewrite epoch shadows is bound by its landing — `reserveRange` leaves it (`fallocate_custody_skips`; the admission already admits a write there on a full store, `writeWouldCreateFreshCustody`). ZERO_RANGE over such a block zeroes it through the admission (the custody's bytes, which every arm orders after; the handler settled first — this is a transient close's residue), never a flip the map cannot see. The custody check precedes the reservation check: a parked buffer over a reservation is zeroed too |
| BJ.3 Red | kv_fs "a PUNCH/ZERO edge carries the file's size as it stands AFTER the settle": the data face's settle hook journals and retires the parked claim as the drain does — on dev the edge's write named size 0 (the durable), with the fix 100 000. write_admit "a reservation leaves the write side's custody alone": an epoch-shadowed hole and a parked hole are not reserved (dev reserved all four), the save displaces nothing unparked, the bytes are the writes', ZERO_RANGE over a parked hole lands zeros | fsx `-X` seeds 21 12 31–36 pass on the fix (21 and 12 failed before); fsstress `-p 20 -r` ×4 rounds: no tripwire, fsck clean |
| BJ.4 Not the epoch's, not the lifetime law's | The save's parked set is matched by `Mapping.eql` and the reservation's flip is the same reference: a flip under a parked key would drift too — but the handler settles (`flushIno` closes the epoch) before every flip, and `reserveRange` now skips a shadowed block, so no flip meets a shadow. The lifetime law serves bytes; it moves no binding | the drift's two sightings and 127's zeros both trace to the handler's fallocate paths |

### BD, amended 2026-10-21 (wave 7; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-7.md`)

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BD.4 The admission on a volume with room takes no lock at all | The fit check of BD.1 goes: on a volume with room (`fastPathRoom`, now `free ≥ 2 × reserve + SPLIT_EXTENTS_PER_TX × caps.txs` — two leaves per transaction, two extents per split) the admission resolves the leaves (the seeds) and admits; the locked law only near the reserve or in the heap-full posture. The log-end hint and the in-flight charge are gone (no reader) | Measured: 45 % of admissions met a leaf at capacity (a 256 KiB node fills with 4 KiB frames between rewrites) and took the locked law's exact walk under the leaf lock — **15.1 s of admission lock waits per 320 k creates** against **0.7 s of freezes** (`meta_kv_freeze_locked_ns`, 28 k freezes at 25 µs): the hold was the admission's walk, not the freeze. A leaf that does not fit in place on a volume with room splits at writeback from extents the room guarantees; no promise is owed |
| BD.5 The turns fan out as a tree | The pass wakes member 0; each member wakes 2i+1 and 2i+2 before its own apply (§BC) | At 64 ranks a serial fan-out on the lane fiber took 254 µs of every member's queue wait and parked every spinner past its bound; the tree: 116 µs, file creation 57 → 97 k under `self` |
| BD.6 What the hold is now | With the admission lock-free the apply's hold is 109 µs per 4.5-tx window on the venue: 62 µs waiting for leaves other APPLIERS hold (7.5 takes at 8.3 µs), 47 µs of work (≈ 10 µs per transaction); the freeze 0.6 s per phase. `self` at 32 ranks: file creation **140 k** (venue 96 k, dev 100 k), removal 81–84 k (venue 107 k); at 64: creation 97 k, removal 20 k | The venue's four appliers contend with each other over the rotor's leaves; `self` removes them for creates and convoys on removals (the reclaim's grouped destroy windows take the venue and hold leaves across the self-appliers). `venue` still ships; `self` is one law away |

### BD, amended 2026-10-22 (the train's red on wave 7; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-7.md` "The train's red")

The train ran wave 7 five times and went red twice where the single gate was green: the applier storm's unit test died at `record.zig:1310 assert(prev_seq >= r.seq)` in `foldNewestFirst`, and THE STRIPED DIRECTORY's daemon died (ENOTCONN). The cause predates the wave and is §AW.4's: two in-flight windows apply in either order, and the checkpointer's freeze between the two applies leaves the LATER window's record in the base above the EARLIER window's seq — the earlier record then lands in the overlay BELOW a base record of its key. Every fold read the overlay group then the base group as one newest-first sequence.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BD.7 A key's records fold by SEQ across both sources, never by source | Every walk over a key — `NodeSnapshot.lookup`'s cold fold and `gather`, `deltaChainProbe`, `CachedNode.foldBelowLocked` — MERGES the overlay group (seq ascending, read from its end) and the base group (seq descending); a materialized head is the fold up to ITS record's seq and serves only while the base holds nothing newer for the key (`baseAbove`: one compare on the base group the lookup already has); the previous overlay record's head stands in for the Δ apply's fold-below only when no base record lies between the two seqs (`baseBetweenLocked`); the memo's base fold only when the whole base group is below the record. Counted: `meta_kv_fold_base_above`, `meta_kv_apply_base_above` / `_between` (the inversion's rate; small by nature, never a fault). No record class folds by source: the charter's per-key law is last-writer-wins by record seq, and the on-disk side (`bset.lookup`'s sort, `RecordIndex`'s `(key asc, seq desc)`, the K-way compact) already ordered by seq alone | `prev_seq >= r.seq` holds by construction: the fold's input is a merge of two seq-sorted sequences. The alternative — a freeze floor that moves only records below every in-flight window's seq_base — needed the conveyor's in-flight set inside the node cache, a partial freeze, and every future apply path to register; the merge costs one compare per lookup of an overlay-resident key and one base binary search per Δ apply that would take the head shortcut. Red: the node-level test (W2's Δ frozen, W1's older Δ lands: the next read's gather and the next Δ's fold-below panic; a straggler's head shortcut skips a base record and serves a stale ctime), the backend test under the seam `hold_next_apply` (W1 held before its apply, W2 applied and acked, the parent's leaf frozen, W1 lands below: the parent's mtime must be W2's — found 1001 for 2002 on 5b4e210), the property test's straggler axis |
| BD.8 The record-seq space is monotone across the manager's INCARNATIONS | The manager's open recovers ring 0's seq offset from appender 0's page (`bind.AppenderSet.page0SeqOffset`, max with the replay window's stamps) BEFORE its first reservation (the guard's claim, the bring-up cover) | The seq-honest fold found it at once: a manager whose offset was raised during its life (a departing member's slot floor, charter §5.5 "monotone across rings") and unmounted cleanly came back from the empty window at offset 0, stamping below its old frontier — the term-3 alloc-lease Put (seq 2907408) under the term-1 release's Delete (2944057): `publishBitmap` busy, the data planes could not arm, THE `-o ro` READER and TWO MOUNTS OF ONE SET red at A's remount. On the concatenating fold the same mount had been one freeze (the index's seq order) or one crash (the replay gate skipping the new incarnation's records as "already folded") away from the same outcome. Red: a raised offset, a Put under it, a clean close; the reopen's frontier ≥ the old one and a new Put of the key outranks the old before and after a freeze and across another reopen |
| BD.9 BD.4 and BD.5 judged | Both stay. BD.5 is `self`-only (`handToMembers`/`applyOwn`) and not on the venue path the train crashed; BD.4 takes no part in the apply order or the freeze — it removed the committers' admission locks, which never ordered two windows' applies (the admission precedes the enqueue and the seq). The inversion's reach: the applier storm test 100 runs under the full smoke's load (50 with BD.4, 50 without, the counters at 0 in every run) — the train's 1-in-2 was the gate's own saturation, not a shape the test reproduces at will; the deterministic red tests are the law's witnesses | Wave 7's levers are measured and sound; what they changed is how often the gate's load reached a latent §AW.4 interleaving |

## BK. The daemon owns the setid strip of every data modification: fallocate applies the kernel's `setattr_should_drop_suidgid` from the caller's standing; the shipped setattr carries `kill_suidgid` (2026-10-23; `docs/evidence/rung-10-fallocate-privs.md`)

The full xfstests run on `d5a8e45` stopped at generic/683 (`perms prealloc`): `xfs_io -c 'falloc 64k 64k'` by `qa_user` over root's 6666/6676/6766/6777/2676/2777 file must leave 666/676/766/777/676/777; ours left every bit. The sibling from create 7b's report (handoff 2026-10-21): a foreign-slot truncate did not strip at the holder.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BK.1 What the kernel sends | Nothing. `fs/fuse/file.c` `fuse_file_fallocate` (7.3) runs `file_modified` → `file_remove_privs` → `__remove_privs` → `notify_change(ATTR_FORCE \| ATTR_KILL_SUID \| ATTR_KILL_SGID)`; `fs/fuse/dir.c` `fuse_setattr` DROPS `ATTR_KILL_SUID \| ATTR_KILL_SGID \| ATTR_MODE` whenever the daemon handles killpriv (v1 or v2) — "the only sane way to reliably kill suid/sgid is to do it in the userspace filesystem" — so what reaches the daemon is at most a SETATTR whose `valid` names no mode and no kill bit, then `FUSE_FALLOCATE {fh, offset, length, mode}`. `FUSE_HANDLE_KILLPRIV_V2` tells the daemon only on WRITE (`FUSE_WRITE_KILL_SUIDGID`), on SETATTR-size without CAP_FSETID and on chown (`FATTR_KILL_SUIDGID`), on O_TRUNC opens (`FUSE_OPEN_KILL_SUIDGID`). The capability xattr is the kernel's own: `ATTR_KILL_PRIV` → `security_inode_killpriv` → a REMOVEXATTR `security.capability` the daemon serves (generic/688, root included) | Read on the tree; the reference (`fuse_client.rs` `strip_setid_after_unprivileged_datamod`) strips both bits for every uid ≠ 0 — a quirk (a member's non-exec sgid survives on every local filesystem) |
| BK.2 The law (`economy.fallocateStripMode`) | `fs/attr.c` `setattr_should_drop_suidgid` exactly: kill S_ISUID when set; kill S_ISGID when set and (group-exec OR the caller is outside the file's group); nothing when the caller has CAP_FSETID. The caller's standing (`callerModifier`): `/proc/<pid>/status` of the request header's pid — `CapEff` bit 4, the effective `Gid`, `Groups` — falling back to the header's uid 0 as the capability and its gid as the one group when the pid is gone. Applied after the fallocate's own commit as a mode setattr through `setattrJudged` (a foreign slot ships it); counted `fuse_fallocate_setid_strips` | The golden's 6766 → 766 and 6666 → 666 for a non-member are the `!in_group` clause (`setattr_should_drop_sgid`, 6.2+), not `setattr_prepare`'s group rule — that one is skipped under ATTR_FORCE. The first draft read the rule as "sgid iff group-exec" plus a group rule on the result and kept a non-member's 2666; the smoke row's matrix and the kernel source corrected it |
| BK.3 The shipped setattr's kill bit | `SetattrWire.valid` gains `SETATTR_KILL_SUIDGID` (bit 8; `SETATTR_KNOWN` 0x1FF); `record_ship.Setattr.kill_suidgid` rides `toWire`/`fromWire`; the holder's `.setattr` arm applies the local law — `killSuidgidMode` (suid always, sgid iff group-exec: FATTR_KILL_SUIDGID's documented meaning), deletes `security.capability`, counts `record_killpriv_strips`. `kv_fs.setattr`'s ship passes `a.attr.kill_suidgid` | The local truncate stripped and the foreign-slot one did not: one law, two outcomes by slot |
| BK.4 Red | smoke "FALLOCATE STRIPS THE SETID BITS" over dev's product: every mode kept (`6666 196608` for `666 196608`, line 1). kv_fs "fallocate strips the setid bits through the dispatcher" without the strip call: `expected 438, found 3510` (0o666 / 0o6666). two_holders "a shipped setattr CARRIES the kernel's kill_suidgid" with the holder arm disabled: `expected 34230, found 36278` (0o2666 / 0o6666) | Each test first red against the old code, then green |
| BK.5 Seen, not fixed | The WRITE/SETATTR kill law (`killSuidgidMode`) ignores the caller's group: a non-member's truncate over 6766 leaves 2766 where ext4 leaves 766 (`setattr_should_drop_sgid`'s `!in_group` clause). The daemon has the writer's standing at the WRITE/SETATTR site (`callerModifier`) but the SHIPPED verb would have to carry the computed mode or the standing; no fstests row names it | A follow-up when a golden does |

## BL. A daemon never unmounts what stands on its tree: a coherence notify expires a name, the §AB.2 substitution waits, a whole-view epoch is withheld while a mount stands on the connection, and the held-elsewhere verdict counts only the survivors of its own unmount (2026-10-23; `docs/evidence/rung-10-coherence-never-unmounts.md`)

xfstests generic/410 (run 4, "after make-shared run on private") lost `mpB`, `mpB/dir`, `mpC` and `mpA` with no daemon stopping anything. The kernel's `d_invalidate` of a dentry detaches every mount on it, lazily and in every namespace: `fuse_reverse_inval_entry` runs it at once unless the notify says `FUSE_EXPIRE_ONLY` (dir.c:1628), and `fuse_dentry_revalidate` answers invalid for a dentry older than the connection's epoch (dir.c:402, before any LOOKUP), so `lookup_fast` runs it at the next walk. The head's daemon (its dentries carry `mpA`/`mpB`/`mpC`) had bumped its epoch; each later walk threw one mount off.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BL.1 A named invalidation expires | Every `INVAL_ENTRY` carries `FUSE_EXPIRE_ONLY` when the kernel offers `FUSE_HAS_EXPIRE_ONLY` at INIT (`Session.entry_flags`, uapi 7.38) | The name's next walk revalidates by LOOKUP: one that still names the same node keeps every mount stacked on its dentry; one that changed is invalidated by the kernel then — the VFS's own law for a mountpoint whose name went away remotely. Coherence is §AB.2's: the stale name is never served past the notify |
| BL.2 The §AB.2 substitution waits | An `INVAL_ENTRY` whose parent the in-flight registry names stays in the sideband (`Sideband.deferred`, re-judged every `ENTRY_DEFER_RECHECK_NS` = 1 ms, counted `transport_notify_entries_deferred`) and is written as itself once that request is answered; never an `INC_EPOCH` in its place. Supersedes §AB.2's "rewrites the frame in place as an INC_EPOCH" | The sideband still never takes a parent's `i_rwsem` behind its own daemon's op (the sideband-law smoke row: 0 samples in `fuse_reverse_inval_entry`). The epoch it replaced was the generic/410 trigger: measured, every epoch every joiner wrote was a substitution (5–27 per fsstress run), each invalidating the whole view — a cost of its own besides the unmounts |
| BL.3 An owed epoch is withheld while a mount stands on the connection | `writeOwedEpoch` asks the table (`mountinfo.mountsOnSuperblock`: an entry whose parent is a mount of this superblock); while one stands the epoch is not written (`transport_notify_epochs_withheld`, warned once per session) | The epoch has no expire-only form: every older dentry is invalid at its next walk, the mountpoint's included. The remaining owed epochs (no free cell, an unnamed recall or tx, the first join's whole-view flush) then age out within the TTL classes — the charter's 1 s on a peered mount — while every named change still reaches the kernel by BL.1. Owed: a kernel patch letting an epoch-stale mountpoint revalidate by LOOKUP would make the epoch itself safe |
| BL.4 The held-elsewhere verdict counts the survivors of our own `umount2` (refines §AN.6) | `close`: a detached clone of our mount (`open_tree(OPEN_TREE_CLONE)`, `holdSuperblock`) → `umount2` → the verdict over what still carries the superblock → the clone's release. Without a hold (no CAP_SYS_ADMIN: fusermount3) the verdict stays before the `umount2` | 1fcef90's law (end the connection only when mounts still hold it after our unmount — propagation has done its work) and §AN.6's (the id proves ownership only while something of ours holds the superblock) together: the clone keeps the superblock and its anonymous device, never makes our mount busy and is in no table. Read before the `umount2`, the verdict counted the copies that `umount2`'s propagation removes and aborted under them (generic/409/410: ~90 warnings a run). The `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_FUSE_UNMOUNT_HOLD` seam sits after the release |
| BL.5 Red | dev 7a6e224: session e2e probes (a file bound onto `/hello` read EchoFs's own `hello` after an `INVAL_ENTRY`, after a substituted one, after an epoch); smoke "A BIND OF THE MOUNT…" (d) `cat: sub/x: No such file or directory` at the first walk after a member joined; (e) "1 other mount(s) of this superblock stand beside … (…-peer)" | Each green on the branch; "A DAEMON ENDS ONLY THE CONNECTION IT OWNS" stays green (A's id reissued to B, A touched nothing) |
| BL.6 Seen, not fixed | A member cannot join a set whose manager remounted once (mount, write, umount, mount; then a joiner: `cannot open metadata set … device I/O failed (IO, terminal)`, dev and branch, file-backed volumes). The bind row formats afresh before (d) | Not this law; owed to its owner — fixed by §BM (the bind row's reformat removed) |

## BM. A joiner reads the manager's listener as a hint: the page names who manages, never where it listens (2026-10-23; `docs/evidence/rung-10-join-after-manager-remount.md`)

BL.6's defect: after any manager remount every join failed `device I/O failed (IO, terminal)`. The joiner dialled `endpoint:{0}` as tree 0's projection named it: the PREVIOUS incarnation's ephemeral listener (its successor's publish was a ring-0 entry no checkpoint covered yet). The refused connect was reported as a device error.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BM.1 What a joiner may rely on across the manager's incarnations | Appender 0's page: WHO manages (rewritten at every incarnation's bind — term + 1, its writer id — before its listener exists) and that it is live. Never WHERE it listens: `endpoint:{0}` comes off the projection, which names the listener whose publish the newest checkpoint covers | A joiner never replays ring 0 (backend `openJoined` step 2); the page and the record are written at different points of the bring-up |
| BM.2 The manager lands a moved listener before its plane arms | `publishEndpoints` cycles one checkpoint after putting a changed record (none when the endpoint is unchanged) | From the manager's `ready` on, every projection reader (a joiner, a `-o ro` reader, `squeezefs job`) reads the live listener.
