The cost is one cycle per mount whose endpoint moved |
| BM.3 The projected endpoint is a hint until a dial proves it | `JoinedSet.awaitManagerListener` before `JoinAppender`: `HolderUnreachable` / `ListenerRefused` re-resolve (the resolver re-reads the record at every newer checkpoint) and redial every `ENDPOINT_POLL_STEP_MS`, bounded by `ENDPOINT_POLL_MAX_MS` (`joined_endpoint_polls`). Past the bound: `ManagerUnreachable`, logged with the endpoint and the page's term. Other dial refusals (a TLS handshake) map as before | This covers a join that races the manager's bring-up, which BM.2 cannot: the page is already Live under the new term while the publish has not landed. A refused TCP connect to a peer is not a device error |
| BM.4 Red | dev `c79ecb1`: smoke "A MEMBER JOINS A SET WHOSE MANAGER HAS REMOUNTED" (once, twice, after kill -9 + the supervisor's replay) 3/3 `device I/O failed`; unit "THE JOIN AFTER A MANAGER REMOUNT" `Io` ← `HolderUnreachable` ← SO_ERROR; unit "A JOIN RACING THE MANAGER'S BRING-UP" red without BM.3 | Each green on the branch; the new rows 40/40 under load |

## BN. A window holds a lane cell before the pass reserves it: the window gate (2026-10-24; `docs/evidence/rung-10-conveyor-window-gate.md`)

THE TWO-DAEMON MATRIX under the 4-wide load fired `meta_conveyor_pass_panics` on daemon B, the joiner: "ConveyorFull, 1 members" during `tar -x` into A's directory, and tar's chmod got EAGAIN. A window's handoff found the journal lane with no free cell (`LaneQueueFull`). The lane's cells (`inboundCap(pages)`) and the conveyor's in-flight map were sized at spawn as one per ring page, on the reasoning that "on a padded ring every window occupies a page, so the ring's admission bounds the windows". That reasoning holds only for an ungrown ring at a 4 KiB grain with no other cell takers. A joined ring grows past the pages its lane was spawned for (the floor ring's 128 cells against the 1 MiB ring's ~189 page-sized windows). A sub-page grain packs several windows into each page (the buffered posture, a 512 B device). Barriers and structural entries drew from the same pool. Acks at the apply (§BB.1) let one op's window follow another before either lands. 948a324's "a throughput cap, not a correctness matter" was wrong.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BN.1 The windows own their cells | The lane splits its cells. `journal_lane.windowCells(pages) = inboundCap(pages) / 2` serve windows on a free list of their own (`free_windows`, cells `[0, W)`). Barriers and structural entries take the rest (`free_cells`) and wait or refuse retryably as before | A storm of fsyncs held behind an un-landed window took every cell (the lane red: 255 barriers). That refusal is retryable for a barrier and a panic for a window. With the same cell count, memory is unchanged. The floor ring's user slice (≤ 61 page-sized windows) stays under the share's 63, and the requests keep 64 |
| BN.2 The window gate | The pass reserves a window only while fewer than `windowCap = window_cells − 1` are tracked (`Conveyor.awaitWindowCell`, A1¼, before the token union, the ring budget and the reservation). A full flight parks the pass: register on `landed`, re-read the count, wait. `inflight_cap` is the same bound | The pass is the only tracker, so the count it read can only fall before its own `track`. Each tracked window holds at most one cell. One untracked window may still hold its cell, because the lane runs the terminal hook (`finishWindow` untracks) before `freeCell`. So a handoff always finds a cell. The park holds nothing anyone waits on, and every tracked window reaches a terminal outcome, so the park always ends |
| BN.3 What the ring no longer promises | Nothing in the window path relies on the ring's geometry, grain or growth to bound windows in flight. `swapRing` leaves the gate as it was | A grown joined ring keeps 63 windows in flight on its floor-sized lane. A burst past that parks the pass, and the next window batches what queued meanwhile (`meta_conveyor_window_gate_parks`) |
| BN.4 Model | `conveyor_core_model.WindowCells`: a gate at `W − 1`, registered before it reads, with untrack before notify and requests on their own cells, holds. The weakenings each fail: a gate at `W`, no gate, and a barrier on a window cell meet a full lane; check-before-register and notify-before-untrack leave the parked pass with room and nobody to wake it | AGENTS.md's model rule: every weakening is proven to fail |
| BN.5 Red | conveyor "THE WINDOW GATE" (RamLane holding 4 window cells): the 5th window, `INVARIANT TRIPWIRE 'meta_conveyor_pass_panics' … (ConveyorFull, 1 members)`. backend "THE WINDOW GATE on the device lane" (BUFFERED, the landing-hold seam, 257 kernel creates): the pass panic. journal_lane "the windows' cells are their own": `expected 128, found 255` | Each is green on the branch |
| BN.6 Seen, not fixed | `SQUEEZEFS_META_PASS_VENUE=committer` together with `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY=self` (two non-shipped A/B levers): a committer-run pass that parks cannot take its own member's turn, and every later window's terminal outcome waits behind that member's window (the completed prefix). It parks at ring admission today, which escalates after `PARK_ESCALATION_NS`, and now also at the gate after 63 windows | Owed to the levers' owner: either a committer-run pass applies its own turn in place, or the startup gate refuses the pair. The shipped pair (`lane`, `venue`) and the other two are safe, because the pass never applies a member's turn and every in-flight window lands without it. Closed by §BO.3 (the refusal) |

## BO. No metadata operation answers EAGAIN under load: an fsync waits on the lane's barrier sequence, a committer waits for queue room, and the startup gate refuses the lever pair that wedges (2026-10-24; `docs/evidence/rung-10-no-eagain.md`)

The window gate's report (§BN) left three items. A barrier request took a cell from the lane's request half, and past 64 on a floor-ring lane it was refused `LaneQueueFull`, which reached the application's fsync(2) as EAGAIN. A committer queues BEFORE ring admission, so `queueCapacity(pages)` bounds nothing upstream, and the 129th simultaneous committer got `ConveyorFull` (EAGAIN) for a create, an mkdir or an unlink. And the lever pair of §BN.6 can wedge. EAGAIN is not an errno POSIX gives create, mkdir, unlink, rename, setattr or fsync; applications do not retry it.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BO.1 An fsync coalesces onto the lane's barrier sequence | An fsync is a `journal_lane.BarrierWaiter` on its caller's frame, never a cell. `requestBarrier` pushes it on an unbounded Treiber inbox (one release CAS). The stage-B scan takes the inbox whole (acquire swap) BEFORE it drains the inbound windows and admits the fsyncs AFTER, so a window its submitter pushed before its fsync is in that fsync's cover (§BB.4). All the fsyncs one scan admits share one ticket (`barriers_started + 1`) and one cover, and the first barrier that satisfies them answers them all. The answer reads the node before it publishes. Refusals at the request: `VolumeFailed`, `LaneStopped`, never a full lane. The request half of the cells now serves structural entries only (§BN.1's split is unchanged). `journal_lane_fsync_waiters_hwm` | An fsync needs a barrier that covers its landing, not a barrier of its own. Waiting for a cell would still have meant one cell per fsync and a barrier per 64 of them. With coalescing, nothing bounded is taken, so nothing refuses, and a storm costs one FSYNC per barrier interval (the red: 192 fsyncs, ONE barrier). The completion's wake after the publish only names the futex word's address, so a waiter that already returned costs at most a spurious wake, which every futex waiter re-checks |
| BO.2 A committer waits for queue room | `Conveyor.enqueueParking`: after a failed fast path the committer counts itself (`room_waiters`), registers on `room`, re-tries under the queue mutex and parks. `drain` notifies `room` after a non-empty drain while the count is non-zero (one relaxed load otherwise). A closing or FAILED volume answers its own error. A group wider than the whole queue is still refused (no product path commits one). `quiesce` waits out room waiters. `meta_conveyor_room_parks` | A full queue holds entries, so a leader owns them (`conveyor_core`'s theorem) and its next drain frees room. The wait is the pass's progress, which already bounds every queued committer (the window gate ends at a landing; ring admission escalates to FAILED after `park_escalation_ns`). Waiting for room adds no dependency that waiting for the outcome did not already have. The queue mutex orders the count against the drain's read (the committer's RMW precedes its failed attempt's unlock; the drain locks before its read). `queueCapacity` is a batching depth, not an errno |
| BO.3 The startup gate refuses `SQUEEZEFS_META_PASS_VENUE=committer` with `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY=self` | `knobs.exclusions`: pairs of admissible values refused together, comptime-checked against their rows. `validateVars` reports a set pair as one error line naming both settings and why. The process refuses at startup before anything opens | §BN.6: a pass run on a committer's thread cannot take its own member's turn while it parks (the window gate, ring admission), and every later window's verdict waits behind that member's window. An in-place apply would mean tracking the pass thread's own pending turn across every park point of the pass. The refusal is one table row for two non-shipped A/B levers |
| BO.4 Models | `conveyor_core_model.RoomPark` (55 states, depth 16) holds; counting after the re-try, re-trying before registering, and the pass reading the count before its drain each strand the committer (`FinalViolated`). `conveyor_core_model.FsyncInbox` (172 states, depth 14) holds; a load-then-store push loses an fsync (`FinalViolated`), and draining the inbound windows before taking the inbox admits an fsync without its window (`InvariantViolated`) | AGENTS.md's model rule |
| BO.5 Red | journal_lane "THE FSYNC STORM" (192 fsyncs from 192 threads behind an un-landed window on a 256-cell lane): fsync 128 answered `LaneQueueFull`, 64 refused. conveyor "THE COMMITTER BURST" (the pass parked at the window gate, 128 queue cells taken, 16 more committers): committer 130 answered `ConveyorFull`, 16 refused. knobs "gate: … committer … self": no report | Each is green on the branch. The smoke row "300 CONCURRENT FSYNCS AND 300 CONCURRENT CREATES ON ONE MOUNT NEVER ANSWER EAGAIN" runs on a joined member's floor ring over a dm-delay device and holds 300 fsyncs on one lane at once |


## BP. A PUNCH or ZERO range is tiled once by its head edge, its whole blocks and its tail edge; a truncate at or below the size clips every binding past EOF (2026-10-24; `docs/evidence/rung-10-punch-edge.md`)

generic/695 read 0xab back at `[8M, 10M)` after `fpunch 2M 2M` + `fpunch 8M 2M` + fsync and a dm-flakey power cut. The fsync was not at fault: on 4 MiB blocks `[8M, 10M)` starts ON a block boundary and ends inside that block, and `kv_fs.fallocateZero`'s striped arm had no piece for it — the head edge needed an unaligned start, the tail edge a block past the start's, and no whole block fit — so the punch (and `fzero 8M 2M` likewise) answered 0 with the bytes standing, before any cut.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BP.1 The tiling | `RangeTiling.of(off, upto, bs)`: the head `[off, min(upto, its block end))` iff `off` is unaligned; the whole blocks `[ceil(off/bs), floor(upto/bs))`; the tail `[floor(upto/bs)·bs, upto)` iff `upto` is unaligned and its block is not the head's (`floor(upto/bs) ≥ ceil(off/bs)`). A range inside one block is one edge: the head when `off` is unaligned, else the tail `[off, upto)` | The old tail test (`upto/bs > off/bs`) assumed an unaligned start; the tie test checks every `[off, upto)` over five blocks: each byte in exactly one piece, each edge a partial block |
| BP.2 What an fsync owes a punch | Nothing new: the edge is a write of zeros through the admission (W1 in place or accumulation) and lands under fsync's data leg and barrier; the whole-block frees ride `MapSeam.punch`'s tx, which the meta barrier covers. The power-cut row proves both after a real cut (fsck replayed the journal: no clean-unmount mark reached the device) | The red was the missing write; with it the existing ladder holds |
| BP.3 The same-size truncate | `stageTruncate`'s striped arm returns only on a GROW (`size > old size`): at the same size every binding at or past `ceil(size/bs)` leaves the map (its `−ref` in the truncate's tx, freed after it); with nothing past EOF the map is not republished; a tree-mapped file's head re-arms its sweep cursor at the same size | ext4's `ext4_setattr` runs `ext4_truncate` at `ia_size <= oldsize` "even if i_size didn't change to truncate possible preallocated blocks" (`fs/ext4/inode.c:6219`, 7.3); generic/092 pins it. Past EOF only reservations can be bound (every write past EOF claims its size), so the clip frees exactly the reservations |
| BP.4 Red | kv_fs "a PUNCH/ZERO range that STARTS on a block boundary": 1 zero write for 4 asked on dev. kv_fs "a truncate to the SAME size": 3 map entries for 2 on dev. smoke "A PUNCH, A ZERO RANGE AND A SAME-SIZE TRUNCATE SURVIVE A POWER CUT": `df` 12 for 8 MiB after the same-size truncate, and `p`/`z` differing at byte 8388609 before the cut | Each is green on the branch |
## BQ. The data plane answers, never refuses or holds: a full device lane parks its caller, an exhausted bounce pool overflows, a write held behind a stalled plane answers before the kernel's deadline, a joiner takes its upload's refusal, a staged read never serves zeros for bytes it cannot find, and arm G never acks a placed write without its bytes (2026-10-24; `docs/evidence/rung-10-data-plane-answers.md`)

§BO closed the metadata engine's EAGAIN. Its report left the data plane's: a device lane refused a request past its 4,096 cells (`QueueFull`, EAGAIN to a file's write, read or fsync) and the read path's bounce acquire refused the same way. generic/347 showed the other half of "the daemon answers": a write parked at the gate behind a thin pool refusing every DMA sat there until the KERNEL's request timeout aborted the connection — the mount fail-stopped for one write never acked. And generic/475 on a FAILED volume served zeros for a staged file's acked bytes.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BQ.1 A full lane parks its caller | `nvme_dev.Lane.acquireCell`: an empty free list counts `dev_cell_waits`, loads the door's word, counts itself (`cell_waiters`, SeqCst RMW), re-pops, and parks on the word through the device's wait route (a fiber parks alone); `freeCell` pushes, then reads the count with a SeqCst RMW and wakes one. A closing lane wakes every parked caller, and it and every later submit answer `WorkerShutdown` (EIO), never backpressure. The data plane's narrow error sets (`layout.DmaError`, `write_seams.ReadError`, `pack.ReadError`, the overlay's, the patch's, `write_admit.Error`) no longer name `QueueFull` | Every held cell belongs to a request in flight that returns it at its CQE: the wait is the device's progress, and a device that stops answering is the watchdog's (`overdueCensus` names the held cells), never a per-op timeout (charter §4). The geometry admits 512 queues × depth 32 handler fibers — past 4,096 on a large host, all on lane 0 until the write lanes fan out |
| BQ.2 An exhausted bounce pool overflows | `ReadPath.acquireBounce`: a pool with every slot checked out hands the read an over-capacity backing of the slot's size, served once and freed (`read_fill_pool_exhausted` counts it) | The pool's own law (`read_fill_pool.zig`: "the caller fetches into an over-capacity backing it serves ONCE"); `fetchIntoFresh` already did it. The fill pool's in-flight headroom (`max(cores × 16, 64)`) is below this venue's 1,024 handler slots |
| BQ.3 The stall answer | `WritePath.awaitParkedCapacity`: a write held behind refused cold flushes for `stall_answer_ns` since its arrival at the write path answers ENOSPC when a flush since then met the store out of space (`enospc.spaceRefusedSince`) or the device's own ENOSPC (`Table.deviceSpaceRefusedSince`, noted by the upload's DMA), else EIO; no byte of it recorded; `parked_gate_stall_answers`, one error line per episode. The episode (`stall_answered`) answers every later held write at its first refused flush; the gate's next capacity closes it. The bound: `fuse/geometry.stallAnswerNs` = the timeout the kernel applies (else the one asked) less `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_CHECK_S`, set by the mount after INIT | The kernel aborts the WHOLE connection for one request older than its timeout (`fs/fuse/req_timeout.c` `fuse_check_timeout`, every 15 s, the age from `req->create_time` at allocation — the kernel's queue counts). On 7.3 the timeout is this mount's own INIT reply (`T_owner + T_park_max` = 135 s, §Z), capped by `fs.fuse.max_request_timeout` (0 here), floored at 15. One check period ahead of the earliest abort covers the time before the gate; the episode's at-once answer bounds a request the kernel queued behind the held ones to the first wave's bound |
| BQ.4 A joiner takes its upload's refusal | `accumulate.uploadParkedBlock`: an upload stamps the entry (`upload_seq`, process-unique); the task that set `upload_inflight` clears it on every exit and stamps a refusal (`refused_seq`, `refused_fenced`) unless its upload landed; a joiner whose joined upload was refused answers that verdict (`upload_join_refusals_taken`) instead of re-driving; a superseded publish still re-drives for the newer writes. A joiner never clears another task's flag | A device that holds a DMA then refuses it (dm-thin's `no_space_timeout`, a per-IO timeout) cost the joiner one wait; the re-drive paid a second toward the kernel's deadline (the parked-gate agent's finding) |
| BQ.5 A staged read never serves zeros it cannot justify | `kv_fs.writeStaged`: a failed commit releases a whole image only under a FRESH identity; under the file's own identity the re-stage replaced the record the committed layout names, so it stays (the old bytes with the unacked write overlaid). `ReadPath.readStaged`: a stable identity with no ring copy and no durable copy on a FAILED (or fenced) volume answers EIO (`staged_failed_reads`; `StagedVerdict.absent.volume_failed` from the plane's fence probe) | generic/475's zeros: `Segment.put` retires the old copy on a same-key re-stage, and the failed commit's release removed the only copy while the layout still named it. A promotion's release follows its commit and `rollbackHook` keeps an acked window's RAM (an unacked window's rollback answers the commit an error before any release), so the promotion path keeps layout and ring consistent in RAM |
| BQ.6 Arm G never acks without its bytes | `accumulate.Table.reserveEntry` before `PlacedSeam.take`: the adopting checkout lands in the reserved slot (it cannot refuse); a full table (`active_block_table_full`) seals nothing and the write takes the copy path's ladder; an unused slot returns (`unreserveEntry`) | A sealed assembly's token answers every later placed write of it as a no-op ack: an adoption that met a full table after the seal acked those writes with no entry. A tripwire site must still be safe (the interception host agent's finding) |
| BQ.7 Model | `io/lane_door_model.Door` (two holders, two callers, store buffering explicit) holds; a plain load of the count while the give-back is buffered, counting after the re-pop, and loading the word after the re-pop each strand a caller (`FinalViolated`) | AGENTS.md's model rule |
| BQ.8 Red | nvme_dev "a lane past its depth" (4,176 requests on a 4,096-cell lane held mid-pass): 64 `QueueFull`. read_path "BOTH bounce pools checked out": `QueueFull`. accumulate "a joiner whose upload the device HOLDS and then REFUSES": 2 device attempts. kv_fs "a same-identity re-stage whose commit FAILS": the ring empty under the staged layout. write_admit "arm G at a full entry table": acked with no entry. Smoke "A WRITE HELD BEHIND A DEVICE THAT REFUSES EVERY DMA" (dm-thin, error_if_no_space, request timeout 30 s): ENOTCONN at 30.7 s, the daemon fail-stopped | Each is green on the branch; the smoke answers ENOSPC at 16.2 s (bound 15 s), a second writer in 6 ms, the mount serves, fsck clean after the pool grows |


## BR. The supervisor is the mount's custodian: a journal tail whose replay failed is held for the next writer, and a mount without a supervisor runs no write cache (2026-10-24; the owner's rulings 4 and 6; `docs/evidence/rung-10-supervisor-custody.md`)

Two exposures were left by §BB and §BG. When the metadata device failed mid-flush, the supervisor's replay of the journal tail failed and the tail was discarded: acked metadata of the last flush window was lost even after the device recovered (generic/753, `rung-10-dm-error-unmount.md` (c)). A mount without a supervisor ran the write cache with nothing to hold its ring: after a crash a staged file read zeros under a size that had reached the disk before its bytes (`rung-10-kill-matrix-write-cache-posture.md`).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BR.1 A refused replay is held, never discarded | `supervisor.Tails.replayAndRelease`: a tail whose replay fails is copied (`journal_tail.persistHeld`) BEFORE the writer guards drop; the copy is released only after a replay landed and barriered | The guard drop is what lets the next writer in. That writer's lock waits for the drop, so it always finds the copy |
| BR.2 Where it is held | A file of the same image, sparse (the header, the slot table, each written slot's runs at their mirror offsets), at `/run/squeezefs/journal-tails/<volume uuid>.jtail` (root; `/run/user/<uid>/…` otherwise; the seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_JOURNAL_TAIL_DIR`). It is renamed in without replacing; there is one copy per volume | The owner asked what happens when no successor comes. A memfd dies with its last holder, and the supervisor can be killed. `/run` is RAM: the copy outlives the supervisor (SIGKILL included) and dies with the host, whose power cut is §5.2's window. The write cache's claim channel (`d0fcaf8`) would need the supervisor alive, and the supervisor must not write the device once it has dropped the guard. A file needs neither: the writer lock arbitrates between the supervisor and the next writer |
| BR.3 Identity and staleness | Tail image v2: the volume's uuid and set uuid and the ring's kind in the header, and the reservation's position (`pos_base`) in every slot. The next writer replays only a copy whose identity matches its superblock. Replay starts at the ledger's `journal_tail_seq` (a window behind it is skipped). A copy whose every window is behind the ledger's tail is STALE: it is removed unwritten, logged as an error and counted (`held_tail_stale`) | The dead writer's checkpoint never passes an un-landed window: the registry's open entry holds it back. A window behind the tail either landed (its slot was not yet freed at the death) or was overwritten by a writer that ran without the copy, and its bytes are no longer the ring's. A foreign copy (named for this volume but naming another) is left in place |
| BR.3½ The prefix law (found by xfstests generic/475 on the first cut) | Every replay of a held copy is JUDGED (`journal_tail.linux_tail.judgedReplay`). It reads the device's superblock, its newest ledger record and its ring. `Image.planReplay` overlays the held windows in seq order and keeps each only while `journal.replayScanImage` confirms no drop the device did not already have. The first window that would add one, and every later window, are counted `held_tail_past_hole`, logged and never written | The first cut replayed copies whole, and 475 ended with fsck C10 `nlink 1 BELOW 2 name(s)`. A held (acked-at-apply) window can lie past a window the tail does not hold: one acked at its landing whose write failed and was rolled back, while later windows had already been applied on top of it in RAM. Written past that hole, the journal's resync applied a transaction after a missing one. Keeping the chain a prefix trades that metadata, which a rollback had already made inconsistent in RAM, for the volume's consistency. The death replay (§BB.3, `replayFd`) is still unjudged (seen, not fixed: BQ.10 (c)) |
| BR.4 Who replays | Two replayers. (a) The supervisor, every tick (`retryHeld`): it takes the writer lock NON-BLOCKING on a fresh descriptor of the device (busy means a writer holds it, so it is that writer's to replay), re-checks the copy under the lock, reads the device's superblock for the identity, replays, and unlinks the copy before the lock drops. (b) The next writer (`KvBackend.open` → `consumeHeld`): under its own lock, after the superblock and the ledger and before the ring read, it replays to ITS device path; while the device refuses, the open is refused (`held_tail_replays` counts the landings). The supervisor exits once it holds nothing. A device that now names another volume ends its retries, and the copy is left for that volume's next writer | The owner's law: replayed by the next mount or by the supervisor's retry once the device answers, before that mount admits writes. The lock makes the two exclusive. Removing the copy by hand is the operator's way to mount without it, and that loses the metadata |
| BR.5 What is not held | A JOINED ring's failed replay is logged and dropped as before (its death replay is judged since BS.16; holding it stays infeasible there). If the copy cannot be written, the memfd AND the writer guards stay with the supervisor, which retries with the guards held and blocks successors | A joined ring has no lock of its own, and the manager's ladder may re-lease it, so no later replay is safe without the membership path (the in-place re-join agent's). Keeping the guard is the only safe custody when no copy exists |
| BR.6 No write cache without a supervisor (the owner's choice (c)) | `mount.run`: the write cache is bound only when the supervisor's channel exists (`journal_tail.hasSupervisor`). Otherwise the plane logs once "no supervisor holds a write cache's ring: the write cache is off" and sets `write_cache_off_unsupervised` 1, and writes take the `SQUEEZEFS_WRITE_CACHE_PCT=0` path. The knob > 0 named on an unsupervised mount is refused before the fork (exit 2, with the reason). A `-o ro` reader is exempt. A mount without a write cache whose member claim a supervisor still holds is refused (`StagingHeldForSuccessor`). The probe binds and drops the claim and never connects | Without a supervisor the ring is custody nobody keeps across a crash. The red row read 0 of 8 closed 3 MiB files whole (768 zero pages under each durable size). Serving a crashed predecessor's residents' files without the ring would read zeros, and a connect would take the ring from its holder |
| BR.7 A supervisor holding custody is never the daemon | The supervisor names itself `sqz-supervise` (`supervisor.thread_comm`). `umount.daemonByProcess` drops a candidate with that comm, so a second `squeezefs umount` of a path whose daemon is gone answers `not mounted` and signals nobody. Custody leaves a supervisor only to a successor mount (the ring's handover, the copy's replay) or by the operator's explicit `kill <pid>`, which the hold lines name. A held tail's copy survives even that | The latch agent's finding: once the supervisor reaped its daemon it was a childless `squeezefs mount` process naming the path, so `pickDaemon` took it for the daemon and TERM ended the hold. The red row: the ring died, 0 of 4 staged files were exact, 4 staged reads were lost |
| BR.8 The rows re-judged | THE KILL MATRIX's foreground arm judges the cache-less law: no staged read lost, and no page under a durable size a hole (§AL.5). The supervised arm remounts supervised. Every smoke row written for the write cache (staged, packed, promoted, the handover, the recall — and THE INTERCEPTION HOST's first row) mounts `--daemon --supervise` (`Rig.spawnSupervised`, `mountAsSet`, `Peer.spawnAsSet`). The bracket probes mount supervised. The perf rigs, the suites and dev_mount already did | Each row now runs the posture it was written for. A foreground row that never relied on the cache runs cache-less |
| BR.9 Red | On origin/dev a0664fa: the held-tail row read the acked creates `lost` after `replay FAILED (DeviceWriteFailed) — acked metadata of the flush window may be lost`. The unsupervised row read `data_staging_on 1`, 0 of 8 files whole, and both unsupervised mounts with `SQUEEZEFS_WRITE_CACHE_PCT=25` came up. The custody row's second umount exited 0, the supervisor was gone, and 0 of 4 files were exact | Each is green on the branch (the evidence note's counts) |
| BR.10 Seen, not fixed | (a) In the cache-less posture a joined peer reads the holder's DURABLE size while the holder's parked write custody is un-landed: the O_TRUNC rows on foreground mounts read 0 for A's fresh 3 MB file on B. A foreign read recalls staged residents (§6.1) but not parked blocks. That was latent under `SQUEEZEFS_WRITE_CACHE_PCT=0` and is now the posture of every unsupervised multi-mount set. (b) `squeezefs format` of a device does not remove the held copy of the volume it overwrites: a stale copy stays in `/run` until reboot, its supervisor's retries ended by the identity check  (c) The death replay (`replayFd`, §BB.3) still writes every held window, including one past a hole of a failed, rolled-back landing-acked window. The same inconsistency is possible whenever the device answers at the death. The root is the pipeline's rollback of a landing-acked window under later acked-at-apply windows | (a) is owed to the data plane's coherence (the recall's sink should land the ino's write custody too). (b) is harmless (the uuid never recurs) and is logged. (c) is owed: judge the death replay too (`judgedReplay` needs only the device), or have a landing-acked window's failure latch and keep RAM as §BB.5 does |
| BR.11 The follow-ups (2026-10-24, `docs/evidence/rung-10-custody-followups.md`) | (a) A foreign reader's grant lands the holder's write custody first — ONE hook, `KvFs.landForGrant`, landed with the foreign-append fix (`a81c2b7`, `docs/evidence/rung-10-foreign-append.md`) for the same law: the prepare seam runs over the filesystem — the write cache's recall, then the ino's custody lands and its parked refinements (the size claim, the stamps) journal behind the data barrier — counted `fuse_grant_landings` / `fuse_grant_landing_drain_failures`. It closes the cache-less gap too: the row A JOINED PEER READS THE HOLDER'S UNLANDED WRITES pins it on two foreground mounts. A member whose custody a grant landed holds nothing un-landed, so its later re-join in place is lawful (§BS.3): a row that wants the fail-stop keeps its custody in a directory the member holds and no peer asks about (a child created in a FOREIGN directory is witnessed at its holder by that directory's holder: a grant). (b) `squeezefs format` keeps each member's prior identity from its preflight and, once the member is overwritten, removes the held copy whose header names that volume (`removeHeld`), logged; a copy named for it that names another identity is left. (c) The death replay (`replayFd`) of a fixed, unsegmented ring holding a window is `judgedReplay` (a tail holding none reads nothing of the device: the supervisor names the exit after the replay): the device must name the tail's volume (else `DeviceForeign`, and the tail is held), windows past a hole are counted and logged as lost, never written; a joined ring is still written whole (BR.5). (d) Owed: a cache-less mount does not pack small files — each takes a whole block (1,000 × 64 KiB filled a 4 GiB set: ENOSPC). Its landing cannot flip a striped file to packed: a striped writer holds no (3.7) and a within-EOF writer drops the level-1 guard before its data phase, so a flip between a writer's kind read and its record leaves its buffer under a packed layout, which the merge discards as a dead generation. The arm needs a writer/flip exclusion on the ino (register-then-check, model-checked) | (a) The grant carries the holder's records and the reader reads the device, so both must cover the holder's acked writes before the grant: the write cache's recall had no cache-less twin, and since BR.6 every unsupervised multi-mount set ran without it (red: B stat'ed 0 for A's fresh 3 MB file). (b) The uuid never recurs, so nothing else ever removes the copy. (c) The same hole hazard as BR.3½ whenever the device answers at the death (red: the device's scan confirmed a drop after the replay). (d) Packing is a data-plane law, not a cache feature, but the promotion's flip is safe only because staged writers hold (3.7) and the ring holds the whole image; the cache-less arm without the exclusion would lose acked writes |

| BN.6 Seen, not fixed | `SQUEEZEFS_META_PASS_VENUE=committer` together with `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY=self` (two non-shipped A/B levers): a committer-run pass that parks cannot take its own member's turn, and every later window's terminal outcome waits behind that member's window (the completed prefix). It parks at ring admission today, which escalates after `PARK_ESCALATION_NS`, and now also at the gate after 63 windows | Owed to the levers' owner: either a committer-run pass applies its own turn in place, or the startup gate refuses the pair. The shipped pair (`lane`, `venue`) and the other two are safe, because the pass never applies a member's turn and every in-flight window lands without it |

## BS. A live member its home shard evicted RE-JOINS IN PLACE when it holds nothing un-landed; otherwise the fail-stop (2026-10-24; the owner's ruling (b); `docs/evidence/rung-10-rejoin-in-place.md`)

A joined mount whose beat stalled past `T_owner` (a partition, a frozen daemon) was evicted, its region recovered, and its late reclaim answered `UnknownLease`: poisoned, EIO until an operator remounted. The member-beat fix (`2fbc3ec`) removed the false evictions; a real one still poisoned a mount that had nothing to lose.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BS.1 The trigger | `shard.Member.tick` answers `evicted` for the owner's `UnknownLease` — at the reclaim under the park (expiry judged first: a park past `T_park_max` stays the terminal verdict) and at a renewal — on a member armed to re-join (every joiner); nothing touches the gate, the words or the poison there. The beat runs `WirePlane.rejoinInPlace`; the follower ends first and is re-spawned after | The owner's verdict is the only word that the lease is gone; the beat is the only thread that already acts on it. The follower reads the incarnation's words |
| BS.2 The retirement (`JoinedSet.retireIncarnation`) | The latch's `retire` (every new mutation `Retired` → the conveyor's `Deferred`); the park gate's fourth word RETIRED (one CAS off RUNNING or PARKED, exclusive with release and expiry; a parked pass wakes and refuses its batch `Deferred`); the growth gate closed and drained — passes, THEN windows — within one landing ceiling; the latch's `failStop` under the SMO lock (no cycle, page or landing writes again); the gate re-opened onto the latch. The writer's block-grant windows forgotten locally (`WriterPlane.forgetWindows`), never returned | `Deferred` is a move class: nothing written, the move gate re-issues the op on the successor, never EIO. `park_core_model` invariant 2 races release, expiry and retirement (the load-then-store weakening lets two act); invariant 4 (`RetireModel`): a pass that read RUNNING is inside the growth gate until its window is tracked, so the retirer that waits the passes and then the windows judges a final ring — the pass's fence removed, or the window count read first, lets a window past the judgement (both fail). The holder revoked the windows at the death; a return could clear a bit it re-granted |
| BS.3 The eligibility ("nothing un-landed", `judgeUnlanded` + `RejoinHost.unlanded`) | Every window landed and no reservation open; the old ring's head EQUALS the head the successor's recovery read (`readOwnPage`: this appender's newest valid page over its four slots, `Recovered{ledger_tail_seq}`, polled within `T_park_max`); no cross-owner intent open; no block in the writer's in-flight registry; no staged resident; no write-side custody (`WritePath.custodyInos`: parked blocks, open overlays, open or saving epochs); no packed tenant in flight; no parked time refinement. Any word ⇒ the park's expiry, the poison — exactly as before, the refusal naming the word | An acked entry the recovery's scan did not reach is replayed by no one (a `Recovered` ring is never replayed): serving on would fork. Custody and residents are acked bytes whose layout no recovery publishes; an intent's roll-forward is the recovery's while the op would re-issue against the successor; a parked refinement was never journaled |
| BS.4 The re-join | `MetaSet.openSuccessors`: every volume `KvBackend.openJoined` through the mount's own door (census, endpoint poll, `awaitManagerListener`, `JoinAppender` — a new appender id and region —, `AcquireSlots(M)`, the first checkpoint); `Divert.dropAll` (every token: the death fan-out swept them at every holder); `MetaSet.publishSuccessors` (the bindings re-applied — move epoch, parent memo, times drain, kernel view, token recall, tail hand-off —, one Release pointer store per member, the retired incarnation kept mapped until `close`); the member re-pointed and joined afresh (a new epoch; `PublishEndpoint` under the new appender id; the gate's `T_park_max` and `SelfFence` from the grant); `RejoinHost.rebound` (the successor's node caches into the budget, the read cache purged, the kernel's whole view owed); the move epoch bumped | The same join a fresh mount makes — no second protocol to keep coherent with the manager. A retired object stays valid for a reader that loaded it before the store (its reads are the recovered state; its mutations refuse `Deferred`), so no per-op gate is needed |
| BS.5 What an op in flight gets | A read completes off the retired incarnation's RAM; a commit answers `Deferred` and the move gate re-issues it on the successor after the bump, parked at most its `T_park_max`; a refused re-join poisons and the closed move epoch answers it EIO at once. Rejected: a per-op FUSE quiesce gate (an RMW on one shared line per op on the hot path) | The admission is the one point every mutation crosses; the move gate already re-issues move classes |
| BS.6 The successor's projection | `JoinOptions.predecessor`: its rows load unleased like the joiner's own (`lease_table.joinedProjectionRowOf`) | The checkpoint the successor reads may predate the recovery's tree-0 release; the manager is the truth to ask. Red: a write to a recovered slot shipped to a holder no one was (the eviction row's first runs) |
| BS.7 The owner's fresh join | An expected member's join is a re-assertion only while the window stands and before it re-asserted (`Owner.graceAwaitsReassertion`) | The grace's roster expected the member for the owner's whole life: once it re-asserted and was then evicted, left or remounted, a join without an epoch held nothing and answered `UnknownLease` forever (red in the unit row; seen as the successor's refused membership join under a remounted manager) |
| BS.8 Red | smoke "A LIVE MEMBER EVICTED WITH NOTHING UN-LANDED RE-JOINS IN PLACE": on dev `PoisonedInsteadOfRejoined`. Its negative arm "… WITH UN-LANDED STATE FAIL-STOPS" (write-side custody under the close posture): the poison, EIO, the crash shape. Units: `park_core_model` invariants 2 and 4 with their weakenings, the RETIRED word, the retired latch, the member's `evicted` verdict, the owner's fresh join | Each green on the branch |
| BS.9 Seen, not fixed | A joiner's APPEND to a foreign-slot file reads back zeros on both mounts (dev, no eviction involved). After a re-join the same class reaches B's OWN pre-re-join files through the interposer's ring: an append through a ring session lands zeros on both mounts (A: `slot_door_coherence_refusals` in the hundreds); through FUSE — a held `O_APPEND` fd or a fresh open — the bytes are whole. A fresh online fsck within one ledger poll (1.1 s) of a re-join can still read the predecessor's death record (C15) | Seen before the foreign-append fix (`8bb2f94`, the claim law) landed; a re-check on it is owed. The ring's interposer row overwrites a pre-filled file instead |
| BS.10 The member joins BEFORE the publication (follow-up, 2026-10-24) | The successors open; the member is re-pointed at them and joins afresh (`PublishEndpoint`, `JoinMember`); only then do the tokens drop and the successors publish. A refused join re-points the member at the predecessor, closes every unpublished successor — the clean leave of a fresh join (rotor released, page `Free`) — and poisons over the retired predecessor | Deterministic: nothing ever reached an unpublished successor, so its leave is a fresh join's, never a dismount against `FAILED` latches. Seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_REJOIN_MEMBER_REFUSED` |
| BS.11 The holder's grants | `Holder.dropAll` at the re-join: every grant this mount's holder issued was over the retired incarnation's slots (the readers dropped them at the death fan-out); `.stats` `dlm_token_outstanding` 0 after it | Inert, but a later census or recall would meet a grant over a slot the mount no longer holds |
| BS.12 The retired node cache (the law) | Released (`KvBackend.releaseRetiredCache`: every mapping discarded) once every FUSE op in flight at the publication has answered (the op registry's slot + request unique, marked after the pointer store) and every interception session that had ops in flight then has been seen idle (`IpcHost.inflightDrained`); polled on the member's beat. A node a reader still holds dies at its last release (the node refcount). A background reader that loaded the retired incarnation before the store and outlives the drain (a fleet read shard in progress) maps from the device: every child pointer carries its node seq, so a node the successor's custody rewrote is a seq mismatch that ends typed (`ReloadParent`, the retry budget) — never another node's content | Measured: 17.6 MiB released of 17.7 MiB, VmRSS 295 MiB ≤ the idle-RSS bound 336 MiB. Rejected: a per-op pin on the incarnation (a shared-line RMW per op) |
| BS.13 Every binding re-applied | The writer plane's grant verbs and the fleet worker call `WirePlane.liveManagerCall` (volume 0's live incarnation per call, Acquire); the job node's tree store, world and rekey read `volumes[0]` (`coordinator.Vol0`); the stripe floor reads the live judge; the xv plane's `own_id` (its guards' rank, the stripe supply's "me") re-binds (`Plane.rebindOwner`) | Each held the retired incarnation's object or identity. Functionally inert on two members (one manager, one endpoint, one identity per member; a joiner's fabric is never the coordinator), but a guard rank naming the old id breaks the guards' total order against a third member whose id lies between the two |
| BS.14 A member live again retires its death record | `recovery.Driver.retireRejoined` in the poll's `Recovered`-page branch: the member live in the census and its dead incarnation recovered on every volume ⇒ `retireForJoin` (`dead_members_retired_rejoined`, which also counts the live-page guard's retirements) | The record names the PAIR: standing, it named the successor's Live page — fsck judged the live member dead (C15, red in the main row's scrub), the sweep waited on a page no recovery ends, and a later death of the successor would replay it (its PR key, its timestamp). The live-page guard alone ran only once the predecessor's page went `Free` |
| BS.15 Multi-volume members and the interposer | A member past volume 0 joins with volume 0's `job:enroll` secret (`JoinOptions.secret`: the set-wide records live on volume 0 alone — a joiner of a two-member meta set could not mount at all). An interposer client's ring sessions carry on across the re-join — reads, overwrites and the fsync through the same sessions, no error reaching the app (an append through the ring is BS.9's open defect) | The ring binds inodes, never an incarnation; the host serves through the filesystem, which reads `volumes` per op; its in-flight ops join the cache release's drain (BS.12) |
| BS.16 A JOINED ring's death replay is judged (from the custody follow-ups, BR.3½/BR.11) | The tail's header names its appender; at the death the supervisor reads that appender's page off the device's appender directory (`dir.readPageOf`: the directory pair, then the ring-side pair). The page must be Live and name exactly the header's ring (`ringPartOf` equal to its segments). Its `ledger_tail_seq` is the chain's tail and the scan expects its appender; `planReplay` keeps the prefix law as for the fixed ring. Any other page — Recovering, Recovered, Free, another appender's segment — is `Stale`: nothing written. A failed joined replay is still not held | The fixed ring's law, applied to the ring's own chain: a window past a hole is a state no writer had. The page check also closes what the unjudged replay could do to a ring the manager had already recovered (a re-join's predecessor) or re-leased. Holding is infeasible: the region's successor is the manager's recovery (another process, often another node), which reads the ring off the device at `T_owner`. The ring has no lock to keep, so a copy held on this host reaches no successor. Red: unit "a JOINED ring's death replay is JUDGED too" (judged false, C and D written past B's hole) |

## BT. A block freed by a metadata change returns to allocation only once the record that freed it is durable: the reclaimer's durability hold (2026-10-24; `docs/evidence/rung-10-free-durable.md`)

The ack point (§BB) answers a truncate, a punch or an unlink at its RAM apply, and its window reaches stable media up to one flush interval later. The terminal free ran right after the answer. A `--single-writer` allocator, which takes its free list first, handed the block to the next mint. That writer's DMA landed while the freeing window was still in flight, and a power cut replayed the old map over the writer's bytes: the truncated file read 4 MiB of another file's data, and fsck found nothing. The symmetric holder escaped only by timing: its carve's `fdatasync` covered the freeing window only if that write had already completed. A joiner's shipped free had no cover at all. The debt drainer could discard the block before the free was durable.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BT.1 The law | Every terminal free goes into the durability hold after the retire and the tier purge (`Reclaimer.freeBlock`). Only then does it reach the ladder's device side: elision with `finishFree`, or the queue and its discard. A non-holder's last-reference ship is held the same way. Released means every metadata member's last COMPLETED barrier started after the freeing window landed | ext4 does not reuse the blocks its running transaction freed until that transaction commits. "Landed" is not enough: a device's volatile write cache reorders writes, and the data plane's own commits answer at the landing |
| BT.2 The seal | Each free is pushed on an OPEN list under the hold's lock. A release that finds the SEALED list empty moves the open list there and snapshots every member's conveyor reservation high-water mark (`MetaSet.durabilityMarks`) under the same lock. The sealed list is released once each member's `JournalLane.durableFloor()` (the landed floor its last successful barrier started from) reaches its mark | A free is pushed after its commit's answer, and the commit's window was reserved before its apply, so a snapshot taken after the push covers it. It is one snapshot per batch, not one per free. The mark is in the same coordinate space an fsync's cover uses (§BB.4) |
| BT.3 Who releases | Three paths: the producer's own free (opportunistic), the worker's manners tick while anything is held, and `drainSync`. `drainSync` serves the ENOSPC valve and the dismount: it reseals everything held and pulls a barrier on every member (`MetaSet.fsync`) before it releases. A free that arrives after the stop, or whose member FAILED, stays in limbo and its registration is dropped; the next mount derives the space from the durable refs | A full store must never answer ENOSPC while frees wait at most one flush interval. The valve already waits for reclaim progress, and the pulled barrier is that progress |
| BT.4 Accounting | A held free is supply in sight. `BlockAllocator.durable_held` joins `reachableFreeBlocks`, which is what `df` and the write admission read, and `Reclaimer.pending()` (the valve and the supply parks) counts it. `.stats` adds `block_free_durable_{holds,releases,forced}` and the gauge `block_free_durable_held`. The ledger identity becomes `queued + elided + held ≡ terminal` | ext4's `df` shows its running transaction's frees as free too. The bracket harness's reclaim ledger reads at quiesce, where `held` is 0 |
| BT.5 Model | `free_hold_model.HoldModel` holds. Each weakening fails with `InvariantViolated` (a DMA into a block whose freeing window is not yet durable): `no_hold` (the shipped ladder), `seal_outside_lock`, and `landed_floor` | AGENTS.md's model rule |
| BT.6 Red | smoke `A FREED BLOCK IS NOT REUSED BEFORE THE RECORD THAT FREED IT IS DURABLE` (a `--single-writer` set, dm-delay on the metadata slice, a dm-flakey cut, kill -9 without a supervisor). On `2d44c33` the freed blocks hold `bb bb 00` after the cut: the truncated file reads the writers' 0xbb, and the unlinked file reads zeros because its block was discarded. With the hold they hold `a1 a2 a3`. reclaim "the durability hold" and "a full store whose frees wait for their records' barrier never answers ENOSPC" are green | Each is green on the branch |

## BU. The data plane answers honestly on every path §BQ left: the interposer's handoff waits, a failed re-stage is undone, fsync's wall speaks the device's class, a partial write returns its count, an overlong lease is one line per episode (2026-10-24; `docs/evidence/rung-10-data-plane-answers-2.md`)

§BQ's report left six items: the interception host answered EAGAIN when every lane refused its handoff; a failed truncate or punch commit left its re-stage under the old layout (reads served zeros); fsync's parked leg answered ENOSPC for any block left parked; a write answered at the gate after earlier pieces landed returned the error; the filled scratch logged 6,349 overlong-lease lines; two functions were read only by their tests.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BU.1 A refused handoff waits | `ipc/host/service.zig`: a handoff every lane refuses (every fiber slot busy) parks in its owner's `Waiting` queue (any thread pushes — a direct-drive read's fallback runs on the reaper; the owner drains) and is re-offered at each flush; the owner's park is bounded (`SERVICE_PARK_MAX_NS`, 5 ms) while its sessions live, a teardown kicks it. EIO only past the fail-stop lattice's bound (the write path's stall bound) or for a torn-down session; a queue that cannot grow is ENOMEM. `ipc_handoff_waits`, `ipc_handoff_wait_answers` replace `ipc_handoff_refusals` | §BO/§BQ's law at the interposer: an LD_PRELOAD'd read or write on a regular file never answers EAGAIN. Completions post straight to the slot, so a waiting op holds nothing the lanes need |
| BU.2 A failed re-stage is undone | `kv_fs`: a staged truncate's clip and a staged punch's zeros re-stage the record the committed layout names BEFORE the commit; `AfterCommit.restore` keeps the pre-op image (the truncate's read buffer, the punch's copy) and every path that leaves without the commit re-stages it under (3.7) (`fuse_restage_restores`); a packed file's transformed re-stage is dropped; a ring that cannot take the restore drops the key (the read's lost arm: EIO on a FAILED volume) | §BQ.5's law for the write's re-stage, applied to the truncate and the punch: the ring never holds an image the committed layout does not name |
| BU.3 fsync's wall in the device's class | `fsync.parkedWall`: the parked leg's second wall answers ENOSPC iff a landing since the leg began met a space refusal — the store's `StorageFull` or the device's own ENOSPC, now noted on every parked-landing DMA (the upload, the in-place drains, the W2 fold) — else EIO | A dm-error device answered "No space left on device" |
| BU.4 The short count | `WritePath.write`: a later piece's error after earlier pieces landed returns the landed prefix's count and size claim (`write_short_counts`); the error is the next call's. The kernel's FUSE write honours it: `fuse_perform_write` adds the reply's `out.size`, ends at a short one and returns the bytes so far; the direct path stops at a short reply likewise | Linux's regular-file write. The 7.3 buffered path leaves the copied full folios uptodate after a short or failed reply (the older `folio_clear_uptodate` on a short write is gone), so the page cache can show the unwritten tail until evicted — as it already did for a failed write |
| BU.5 An overlong lease is one line per episode | `transport/slot.zig`: every lease held ≥ 1 s counts `transport_lease_overlong`; the first of an episode logs, and the episode closes after two op-watchdog ticks (10 s) with none | Not an escape: the constant's own law says admission backpressure exceeds 1 s, and the gate holds a payload it has not yet admitted (generic/299 and 750's filled scratch: 6,349 lines, one per gate tick) |
| BU.6 Dead code | `block_alloc.coreError` and `staging.resolveStagedRead` (with `StagedRead`) deleted; `lostStagedRangeLen` keeps its tie test | AGENTS.md: no dead code |
| BU.7 Red | service "a handoff every lane refuses WAITS": -EAGAIN. kv_fs "a staged truncate's and a staged punch's failed commit": the ring held the 8 KiB clip under the 16 KiB layout (the punch first: zeros across the punched range). write_admit "fsync's parked leg answers the device's class": ENOSPC for a device EIO. write_admit "returns the SHORT count": the whole write answered EIO. slot "overlong payload leases": 8 lines for 8 leases | Each is green on the branch |

## BV. Concurrent creates mint into different trees: the per-stream mint window; a mint keeps its ino only if its slot still stands after the fetch_add (2026-10-26; `docs/evidence/rung-10-create-path-8.md`)

Wave 7 (§BD.6) left the apply's hold at 109 µs per window, 62 µs of it waiting for leaves other appliers held. Named: 90 % of those waits were on the RIGHTMOST leaf of an inode tree. The rotor's headroom law counts trees in nodes and broke ties toward the first most-headroom tree, so concurrent creates appended to one tree's right edge until it split, then to the next.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BV.1 The window | A stream's rotor mint takes ONE rotor tree for `windowMints(node_size)` mints (one node of inode records: `foldCapacity / 2 / 93`, the midpoint split's refill over the inode record; 1,386 at 256 KiB) or one checkpoint age (`windowAgeNs`), claimed through a per-slot word (`token ‖ expiry`, one CAS: `mint_window.Claims`); the census's pick skips the trees other streams claim (`acquire.mintChoice`; the over-ceiling verdict reads every tree). `SQUEEZEFS_META_MINT_WINDOW` = `window` (ships) / `census` (the control arm) | Measured (counters, `mdtest -np 32 -n 10000 -u`, file-backed 2-MDT set): leaf waits per apply window 58–66 → 4.2 µs, the hold 111–128 → 55 µs, the rightmost inode leaves' waits 2,932 → 129 ms per create phase; the removal phase 55 → 14 µs. One node of mints re-judges the headroom at the law's own unit; one checkpoint age is when the census's input (the node count) can move |
| BV.2 A tree, not an ino range | The window reserves NO inos: each mint stays the slot cursor's one `fetch_add` (dense; nothing returned or skipped; `statfs`, the stamp's cursors and the DUR-8c fold untouched) | A slot tree is ino-ordered: every fresh range lies above its last key, in its rightmost leaf, until splits cut it — and the headroom law leaves the tree at its first split. Ranges in one tree share its hot leaf (§BD.2 measured no change) |
| BV.3 The stream is the caller | A STREAM is the FUSE caller (`Store.allocateIno(…, caller)`: the request's pid; no caller = the calling thread); the plane keeps `nextPow2(4 × cpus)` sets of four ways keyed by the stream's token | A thread's window: 58 → 44 µs; with ranks bound to hardware threads, 5 µs — an unbound rank moves between CPUs, so one drain worker served two ranks and its window held both in one tree |
| BV.4 Who opens a window | A decision mint (the census; an affinity mint outside a window) opens the stream's window iff another ACTIVE stream decided on the plane within one window age — active = two decisions of its own, noted from its second (`mint_window.Recent`). A lone stream keeps the per-mint census | A one-off caller (a `mkdir`) beside busy streams takes a tree of its own yet never makes a serial creator concurrent: THE TWO-MEMBER row's shell kept charter §5.4's spread (128 guest trees; with every decision counted, two) |
| BV.5 Affinity under windows | The affinity arm yields to another stream's window on the parent's tree (`mint_window_affinity_yields`) and, beside an active stream, makes that tree the stream's window | One-off `mkdir`s funnelled the 32-stream row's directories into the smallest tree; 13–15 k affinity mints then shared its edge (16–20 µs per window, 4.7 with the rule) |
| BV.6 The tie by the cursor | `acquire.mintChoice` breaks a headroom tie at the first candidate at or after the rr cursor, cyclically | The shipped rule took `rotor[rr % n]` if it tied, else the FIRST most-headroom tree: a run of mints over equal trees funnelled onto the lowest-indexed one. Charter §5.5: "ties by the cursor" |
| BV.7 The handover law | `Plane.mintSlot` answers the slot and the lease generation its decision read; `MetaSet.allocateIno` keeps the ino only if, after the `fetch_add`, the slot still names this mount at that generation and is not releasing (`mintStands`), else burns it (`acquire.mint_burns`) and decides again; `rotorCap(MINT_SPREAD)` burns in a row answer `Deferred` (the move gate re-issues). The cursor's `fetch_add` and `snapshot` are seq_cst | Red: a create decided its slot, A handed the slot to B, B minted off the cursor A carried, A's `fetch_add` answered the same ino (65539 twice) — and §X.2's re-issue ships that ino to B as an intent's child. Dev has the race for every census mint; a window caches its decision for a thousand. The release's seq_cst gate store precedes its cursor read; the minter's seq_cst load follows its `fetch_add`: one sees the other. Model `mint_window_model.MintRecheck` holds; no recheck, a recheck blind to the generation, and the gate store drained after the cursor read each fail. `WindowClaim`: a check-then-store claim names two holders |
| BV.8 What remains | Not built | The hold is now work (≈ 9 µs per create under the leaf locks); one hot leaf per stream crosses the 4 KiB writeback threshold more often (freezes 80 k × 8.8 µs → 136 k × 4.1 µs per phase, the same total) |

## BW. Self-apply ships: a corpse's destroy applies leaf by leaf, the pass gives the journal lane a turn between batches, the tail gate bounds the members in flight, the tail holds a window whole (2026-10-26; the owner's "lets go"; `docs/evidence/rung-10-destroy-path.md`)

Wave 7 left `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY=self` unshipped: +40 % file creation at 32 ranks, but removals 0.78× and a collapse at 64 ranks (19.9 k), put down to "the reclaim's grouped destroy windows take the venue and hold leaves across the self-appliers". On `380866f` the reclaim's destroy is not a group (no product path calls `commitGroup`): it is ONE self-applied member of 64 corpses that locked its whole leaf set ascending and held it across every wait — and the collapse's larger cause was the journal lane's own thread: under `self` nothing parked the pass, so at 64 ranks it held `sqz-jrnl` batch after batch while the windows it handed off sat un-issued.

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BW.1 A corpse's destroy applies leaf by leaf | `KvTx.leafwise` (the reclaim's `destroyRun`: every record names an object no name reaches and no kernel holds) makes a self-applying member take its leaves ONE AT A TIME, ascending: lock, revalidate the records resolved to that leaf (a moved leaf re-resolves those alone), their pre-images, apply, the threshold, unlock, then the next (`Conveyor.applyLeafwise`). Its entry is still ONE entry: replay is all or nothing. A failure after a leaf was released (or the poison seam) rolls the member's records back seq-conditionally (`rollbackFailedTx`, a freeze may have taken one into the base) and the member is a PAD (§BC.2). THE LAW: the destroy never waits for a leaf while holding one, so a committer on any of its leaves waits behind it for that leaf's share of its records — measured 3–10 µs — never for its acquisition of the others. A name's change keeps the union (`applyUnion`): the dentry and the inode are seen together | No reader can tell the destroy's apply from an atomic one: the corpses are unreachable by name, unknown to the kernel, and a census reading two leaves at two instants already reads two moments. The union hold put every unlinker on a 64-corpse destroy's ~47 leaves (the corpses' inode leaves are the leaves the unlinkers are on) behind its whole acquisition, and its window's reservation — the completed prefix — open as long. Model 12 (`conveyor_core_model.LeafwiseDestroy`) holds; the union hold fails |
| BW.2 The pass gives its venue a turn between two batches | `PassSeam.yield`: after a batch, with more queued, the pass fiber parks on an immediate timer — the journal lane's loop runs one turn (its CQEs reaped, its inbox — the windows' submits, stage B's wakes — drained, stage B polled) and the timer brings the pass back, still the leader; `meta_conveyor_pass_yields`. The committer venue's yield is a no-op | Under `venue` the applier pacing hold parks the pass; under `self` nothing did (no applier venue, no pad hold on a 512-B grain), and with 64 committers the queue never ran dry: windows waited 256–512 ms from submit to verdict until the window gate parked the pass, the tail's slots ran out, `sqz-jrnl` burned 19.5–22.6 s of a removal phase (the `venue` arm 1.5 s). With the turn: 1.2–2.0 ms, 1.9 s |
| BW.3 The maps a pass churns never fill with tombstones | `GrantPassGate.inflight` (the A1½ recall's in-flight objects) and the conveyor's in-flight window map: every key is new and std's map rehashes only when it grows, so removals' tombstones filled the table and every insert probed it whole (26 % of a 64-rank removal's daemon samples in `passBegin`'s probes; the recall 693 µs per pass). An emptied map clears; past a quarter of its capacity in removals it rehashes in place (amortized O(1), no allocation) | The degradation is a function of churn, not load: the venue arm's recall had crept to 13–25 µs; now ≈ 1 µs in both arms |
| BW.4 The tail gate | Under `self` the pass reserves a window only while the members of the windows in flight plus its batch fit the journal tail's slot table (`Conveyor.awaitTailSlots`, `tail_members`: the pass increments at `track`; a terminal outcome gives its slots back, THEN drops its count, THEN notifies `tail_freed`); parked, it holds nothing; the drain caps a self-applying batch at the table; `meta_conveyor_tail_gate_parks` | Every member acked at the apply claims a slot until its window lands — a batch's worth per window where the venue claims one, the claim the table's size (twice the lane's cells) was sized for — so the window gate bounds nothing: 63 floor-ring windows of 64 members against 256 slots, and the starved lane met the table full 2,169 times in one 64-rank removal (`meta_conveyor_tail_slot_misses`, must-stay-0). Model 13 (`TailSlots`) holds; no gate and the count dropped before the slots each meet a full table, check-before-register strands the pass |
| BW.5 The tail holds a self-applied window whole | The pass claims a tail slot for the window's trailing PAD (`journal.padOps` at the reservation's end) BEFORE it hands out the turns, and every member claims its entry — or its rolled-out PAD — whenever the window answers at the apply, whoever that member answers; a PAD the tail cannot take answers the window's members at the landing. The tail gate counts a window's members plus its PAD | Each member had claimed a slot for its own entry only: the PAD that chains a window to the next was written with the window's image at the landing alone, so the supervisor's judged death replay (§BR.11 (c), the prefix law) stopped at the first un-landed window's end and dropped every later window as past a hole — THE REPLAY PROOF under `self` (red on `380866f` too: 1 window replayed, three acked names gone after the kill). A member answered at the landing (a served verb, a control record) left a hole the same way |
| BW.6 `self` ships | `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY` defaults to `self`; `venue` is the control arm. The startup gate's exclusions judge the pair the process would RUN (a default counts): `SQUEEZEFS_META_PASS_VENUE=committer` alone is refused naming `SQUEEZEFS_META_APPLY='self' (the default)` (§BO.3) | The condition the owner set — the removal regression gone at 32 and 64 ranks — holds on the mechanism, and THE REPLAY PROOF holds under it (BW.5): at 64 ranks the removal's window 227 ms → 1.2–2.0 ms, the recall 606 → ≈ 1 µs, the tail's misses 2,225 → 0, `sqz-jrnl` 19.5 → 1.9 s; correctness runs (a loaded host, not measurements) completed file removal at ≈ 2.1× the venue arm and file creation at 1.14–1.32× at both widths, the directory rows at 0.80–0.91× (BW.7 (a)). The owner's quiet A-B-B-A judges the rates |
| BW.7 Seen, not changed | (a) The directory rows under `self` read removal 0.80–0.88× and creation 0.88–0.91× the venue arm in correctness runs with each phase started after the reclaim drained (0.64–0.97× back to back, the file removal's corpses reclaimed during them); at 32 ranks the rmdir's guarded section is 41 µs against 336, yet the op takes 696 µs against 556 — the gap is outside the commit, not established. The owner's A-B-B-A judges whether `self` keeps the default for them (the flip is one commit). (b) `mkdir` at 64 ranks: every member takes the parent's inode leaf alone (its nlink `Put`), and the rank directories' inodes share one leaf — leaf waits 143–332 µs where the union lock takes it once per window. (c) The turn spin (§BC.3) fell through 560–637 k times per 640 k file ops at 64 ranks, each up to 35 µs on a driven FUSE lane. (d) The reclaim destroys ≈ 350 batches/s (≈ 22 k corpses/s) in both arms; a 640 k removal's corpses drain into the next phase. (e) `self` windows are smaller (≈ 3.8 members against ≈ 10): journal pad 67–95 B per op against 28–30. (f) A member answered at its apply while an earlier member of its window has not yet claimed its slot lies past a hole if the daemon dies in between, and the judged death replay drops it though it was acked — a window's apply span wide (≈ 20–300 µs); the `venue` arm has the same hazard across windows (§AW.4: two windows apply and answer in either order). The replay cannot PAD such a hole: a later window's member may have read the unanswered member's applied records | (a)–(c) are the next wave's levers (the apply posture's hot-leaf combining; the spin's idle rule on driven lanes is an A/B the owner measures); (d) is the reclaim's own per-corpse plan (≈ 37 µs each), not its apply; (e) costs ring bytes, not latency, on the RAM set; (f) is owed to §BB's crash law in both arms: an answer only once the tail holds the chain up to its member (a claimed-prefix word), or a replay told which holes no answered member can depend on (the members of ONE window were all queued before any of them applied) |
| BW.8 Red | backend "§BW a corpse's destroy under self-apply holds ONE leaf at a time": the earlier leaf still write-locked by the destroy while it waited (8797835). conveyor "the pass gives its venue a turn between two batches": expected 2 yields, found 0; conveyor "the in-flight window map never fills with tombstones" and token_table "the gate's flight map never fills with tombstones": tombstones past a quarter of the table (eb82428). conveyor "THE TAIL GATE": INVARIANT TRIPWIRE `meta_conveyor_tail_slot_misses` (eb12f84). backend "§BW the death replay of self-applied windows": the judged replay's `past_hole` 2 for 0 (06433e3) | Each is green on the branch |
| BW.9 A window's arenas are recycled (the idle-RSS law) | `Conveyor.window_arenas` (a window's own arena and its image's) and `part_arenas` (a self-applying member's) park a released arena reset to one buffer of its high-water size, at most 64 KiB (smp_allocator's slab: a larger buffer is mapped and unmapped, never parked); `journal.buildWindowFromOps` / `buildBatchWrite` build in the arena they are given. A self-applied window's PAD is built once, by the pass: the tail's copy and the image's | smp_allocator parks a freed block on the FREEING thread's list and maps a fresh slab for a thread whose own list is empty. Under `self` a window's bytes are filled on the committers (the parts; the image and a second PAD on the last member) and freed at the lane's verdict, so every committer's slabs drained onto the lane's list: gate 144's re-joined member read 400 MiB against the idle-RSS bound 336 (291–303 on every earlier gate); 10,000 creates under a 64 MiB budget left `self` 449 MiB, `venue` 320. Recycled: 313 and 315. Red: conveyor "a self-applied window's arenas are recycled" — frees on another thread than the allocation's, 194 for 32 windows (the `Window` structs are the 32) |

## BX. Self-apply follow-ups: an rmdir never remembers its victim's stripe verdict; an answer ahead of the landing lies inside the resident prefix (2026-10-26; `docs/evidence/rung-10-self-apply-followups.md`)

The owner's A-B-B-A against v0.1.0 left directory removal under `self` at 0.82–0.89× (48 k against 55–60 k) and named a durability hole in both arms: a member (`self`) or a window (`venue`) answered while an earlier reservation is neither landed nor in the journal tail is dropped by the death replay (§BR.11 (c)).

| Call | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BX.1 An rmdir reads its victim's stripe verdict and never remembers it | `KvFs.removeChild` asks `victimMapOf` (the verdict with a plain miss forgotten); every other verdict is remembered as before, a striped victim still installs its map | `mapOf(victim)` missed on every rmdir and inserted the dying directory's plain verdict under the cache's EXCLUSIVE lock; the write-preferring `RwLock` queued every worker's shared probe behind those inserts: 560 of the rmdir's 600 µs before its commit at -np 32. After: 120 µs; directory removal 198 k (self) / 103 k (venue) at -np 32, 194 / 110 k at -np 64 (correctness runs) |
| BX.2 The resident prefix | `InflightRegistry.resident_upto` is the start of the first open reservation the journal tail does not hold whole (`maxInt` when none); `markResident` sets an open one's flag; the word moves under the lock (seq_cst) and fires `resident_hook` after it. An answer ahead of the landing is released only when every reservation below its own entry is landed or resident | The death replay keeps the tail's windows only while the device's scan confirms no new drop (the prefix law, §BR.3½): an answer past a hole was an acked op the supervisor dropped. An SMO entry is resident only by its landing (it never rides the tail) |
| BX.3 Self | A member's claim sets its part's flag and carries its window's claim prefix (seq_cst; the CAS that completes it marks the window resident — its PAD went first); a success not yet inside the prefix takes a window party, takes its share of `pending`, then parks (register, re-check) on the conveyor's residency notify, woken by every prefix advance and every move of the registry's word | Waiting before its share of `pending` would hold back the landing an unclaimed sibling's hole waits for. Model 14 (`ResidentAnswers`): holds; answering on the member's own claim and reading the window's prefix alone answer past a hole; the flag unfenced stalls the prefix |
| BX.4 Venue | The applier marks its window resident at its claim and waits the same way; on the journal lane's own fiber (an `alone` window) it never parks — the answer goes to the landing (`meta_conveyor_resident_landings`) | The landings such an answer waits for are that fiber's to reap. §AW.4's either-order test now applies W2 while W1 is held and answers it after W1 |
| BX.5 The cost — OWED, the owner's ruling | Measured (clean A/B, `self`): file creation 228 → 152 k (-np 32), 211 → 128 k (-np 64); removal 238 → 135 k, 221 → 122 k; 56–67 % of answers wait 115–345 µs; daemon CPU 63 → 83, 65 → 100 µs per op. Options: (a) keep BX.2 and cut the convoy — claims written ahead of the apply, at the member's turn (a failed member re-claims its PAD before its error is answered); (b) the owner's alternative: the tail records each self window's EXTENT at the handout (its PAD slot), the judged replay PLUGS a hole lying wholly inside recorded extents with a PAD and continues, and an answer waits only for an uncovered reservation (an SMO entry, a venue window before its claim) | Every answer now waits for every earlier member's claim: one member slow to run holds all later answers. (b) needs the owner's ruling against §BR.3½'s reason for the prefix law ("a transaction applied past a missing one is a state no writer had" — generic/475's rolled-back window): it rests on every hole being unanswered and every dependent op ordered after the answer it depends on by the guards held to that answer |
| BX.6 Red | kv_fs_stripes "an rmdir reads its victim's verdict and never remembers it": with a reader inside the cache the rmdir joined its queue (`ce09dfe`). backend "§BX an answer at the apply waits until the journal tail holds every reservation below its window" and "§BX the same law on the venue arm": `past_hole` 2 and 1 for 0 (`6956c0a`) | |
